### ReadyMe
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/readyme-f688) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/manyasharma1008/ReadyMe) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://ready-me-liart.vercel.app/) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2mcOXZ4RGXw) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackNova%203.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hacknova-3.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-4-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> No more guessing your size.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![TensorFlow](https://img.shields.io/badge/TensorFlow-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Deep Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Deep%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Keras CNN](https://img.shields.io/badge/Keras%20CNN-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Shopping for clothes online is frustrating because you never know if something will actually fit you. People usually guess their size or rely on confusing size charts that change from brand to brand.
Because of this, clothes often arrive too tight, too loose, or just don’t look good — leading to a lot of returns, wasted time, and disappointment.
Another big issue is that you can’t truly see how an outfit will look on your body before buying. You’re just imagining based on model photos, which are not realistic for everyone.

ReadyMe makes online shopping simple and more reliable.
Users can scan their body using their phone camera and get accurate measurements no manual measuring needed
It suggests the right size for each product, even if brands use different sizing systems
Users can see how clothes will actually look on them through virtual try-on (3D avatar or live camera)
This makes shopping:
Easier - no more guessing sizes
Faster - no trial and error
Safer - fewer wrong purchases and returns
In short, it helps people buy clothes that actually fit and look good the first time.

**Challenges we ran into**

One of the biggest challenges was building the 3D avatar from user images. It sounds simple in idea, but in reality it’s quite complex.
We struggled with how to accurately convert 2D images into a realistic 3D body model. Small issues like different camera angles, lighting, and user posture were affecting the output a lot. Even slight inconsistencies in images were leading to incorrect body shapes.
Another challenge was integrating this with real measurements. It’s not just about creating a visual avatar, but making sure it actually matches the user’s body dimensions so that virtual try-on makes sense.

To move forward, we broke the problem into smaller parts. Instead of building everything at once, we first focused on extracting body landmarks using tools like MediaPipe and generating approximate measurements. We are currently using this as a base and improving accuracy step by step.
For the 3D part, instead of jumping directly into full custom model building, we are exploring existing models and frameworks to understand how avatars are generated and then planning to refine them according to our use case.
Overall, the key was simplifying the problem, building step by step, and focusing on accuracy before realism.

Team **Funstop** -- [Ajeet Singh](https://github.com/AjeetSingh21), [Manya Sharma](https://github.com/manyasharma1008), [Jash Ajmera](https://github.com/jaxcode23)

[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/sentinel-8228) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Sneha-Mogali/EquiMind) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-GHRhack%202.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://ghrhack2.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-4-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Never Trust, Always Verify

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![TensorFlow](https://img.shields.io/badge/TensorFlow-333333?style=flat-square) ![Keras](https://img.shields.io/badge/Keras-333333?style=flat-square) ![Deep Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Deep%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![TFLearn](https://img.shields.io/badge/TFLearn-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

EquiMind - Zero Trust Risk Engine
GHR HACK 2.0 Submission

Problem Statement
Autonomous Zero-Trust Network Architecture with Dynamic Risk Scoring - Design a cybersecurity system implementing a Zero-Trust architecture where every user, device, and application is continuously verified using dynamic risk scoring instead of static authentication rules.

Solution Overview
EquiMind is a real-time zero-trust security platform that combines:

🤖 TensorFlow ML Models for threat prediction
📊 Real-time Risk Scoring with behavioral analysis
🔒 Dynamic Access Control based on continuous verification
💾 Supabase Database for dynamic entity management (20-person org)
📈 Prometheus Monitoring for observability
⚡ WebSocket Streaming for live security events
Architecture
┌─────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────┐
│   Frontend      │◄──►│  Backend API     │◄──►│  ML Models      │
│   (React)       │    │  (FastAPI)       │    │  (TensorFlow)   │
└─────────────────┘    └──────────────────┘    └─────────────────┘
                              │
                    ┌─────────┴─────────┐
                    ▼                   ▼
             ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐
             │  Supabase    │    │  Prometheus  │
             │  Database    │    │  Monitoring  │
             └──────────────┘    └──────────────┘
API Endpoints
GET /status - System health and statistics
GET /entities - List all monitored entities
GET /entity/{id} - Entity details with risk history
GET /events - Recent security events
GET /incidents - High-risk incidents
POST /predict - Direct ML risk prediction
GET /metrics - Prometheus metrics
WS /ws/live - Real-time event stream
Frontend Pages
Mission Control - Real-time security operations center
Entities - Entity management and risk tracking
Threat Intel - Threat investigation and analysis
Policy Studio - Security policy management
Analytics - Data visualization and insights
Monitoring - Grafana dashboards and metrics
User Portal - User-facing security portal
Technology Stack
Backend:

FastAPI (async Python web framework)
TensorFlow (ML models)
Supabase (PostgreSQL database)
Prometheus (metrics)
WebSocket (real-time streaming)
Frontend:

React + Vite
TailwindCSS
Recharts (visualization)
WebSocket client
Database:

Supabase (PostgreSQL)
20-person organization structure
Real-time risk score persistence
Event logging capability
ML Models:

MLP (Multi-Layer Perceptron) for risk prediction
Autoencoder for anomaly detection
UNSW-NB15 dataset for training
Database Features
Dynamic Entity Management
20-person organization with realistic roles and departments
Real-time updates - Risk scores persisted to database
Automatic fallback - Works without Supabase (13 hardcoded entities)
Easy scaling - Add entities via database, no code changes
Bulk Event Logging
High Performance - Events buffered and inserted in batches
Configurable - Flush after 50 events or 2 minutes (configurable)
Monitoring - Real-time buffer status via API
Reliable - Automatic flush on shutdown, error handling with retry
Organization Structure
Engineering (4): Backend, Frontend, ML, DevOps
IT & Security (3): DBA, Security Analyst, IT Support
Finance (3): Analysts and Managers
Product & Design (2): PM, UX Designer
HR & Operations (2): Managers
External (2): Contractors (higher risk)
Service Accounts (4): DB, API, Backup, Monitoring
Setup Guides
Quick Start: backend/SUPABASE_QUICK_START.md (5 minutes)
Detailed Guide: backend/SUPABASE_SETUP.md
Bulk Logging: backend/BULK_LOGGING.md
Migration Info: SUPABASE_MIGRATION.md
Project Structure
EquiMind/
├── backend/              # FastAPI backend with ML models
│   ├── main.py          # API server
│   ├── models.py        # TensorFlow ML models
│   ├── monitoring.py    # Prometheus metrics
│   ├── simulator.py     # Event generation
│   ├── scorer.py        # Risk computation
│   ├── entities.py      # Entity management
│   └── models/          # ML model files
├── frontend/            # React frontend
│   └── src/
├── risk_engine/         # Monitoring configs
│   ├── prometheus.yml
│   ├── grafana-dashboard.json
│   └── docker-compose.monitoring.yml
└── shared/              # Shared contracts
Risk Scoring Model
Risk scores (0-100) are computed from:

Network Analysis (33%): ML model prediction on network flow patterns
Identity Context (33%): MFA status, location, time-of-day
Device Posture (33%): Device health and compliance
Decision Thresholds:

0-40: ALLOW (Low risk)
40-65: CHALLENGE (Medium risk - require MFA)
65-80: RESTRICT (High risk - limited access)
80-100: BLOCK (Critical risk - deny access)
Monitoring Metrics
Available at /metrics:

equimind_events_total - Event counts by severity/decision
equimind_risk_score - Risk score distribution
equimind_ml_prediction_seconds - ML inference latency
equimind_websocket_connections - Active connections
equimind_entity_risk_score - Per-entity risk tracking
Access Grafana dashboards at http://localhost:3001 or via the Monitoring page

### foxxy ai
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/foxxy-ai-d09f) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/alaotach/foxxy-ai) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/sdJHdQkHHMM) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackJNU4.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackjnu4.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-4-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> foxxy aifoxxy ai

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Google Text to Speech](https://img.shields.io/badge/Google%20Text%20to%20Speech-333333?style=flat-square) ![AI/ML](https://img.shields.io/badge/AI/ML-333333?style=flat-square) ![RestAPIs](https://img.shields.io/badge/RestAPIs-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

![image](https://assets.devfolio.co/content/492594de513b4cb69a96d1c8f9b39476/6d87b413-f630-4c74-b32b-d91dec6f3d10.jpeg)

## Problem

Modern websites are built for manual human interaction. Users are expected to click buttons, fill forms, navigate complex interfaces, and repeat the same workflows every day.

While automation tools exist, they are often:
- too technical for everyday users  
- expensive to set up and maintain  
- fragile and prone to breaking  

Most people cannot realistically use tools like Selenium, RPA platforms, or custom scripts to automate their web tasks.

At the same time, AI systems such as chatbots can understand user intent, but they cannot see or control the real webpages users are working on. This creates a clear gap between what users want to do and what AI can actually execute on the web.

Today, there is no simple, safe, and universal way to tell an AI:
> “Do this for me on this website”

and have it happen directly on the live page the user is using.

---

## Solution

**Foxy AI** closes this gap by bringing intelligent, safe, and visual AI-driven automation directly into the browser.

**Challenges we ran into**

## Technical Approach and Challenges

We initially used **Playwright** for browser automation but faced multiple limitations. Playwright runs in a separate browser instance, which conflicted with our UCP requirement of operating in the **same live browser session**, similar to a browser extension. We also encountered access and control issues that made this approach unreliable for our use case.

Our early implementation relied solely on **DOM snapshots** provided as input to the AI. This proved problematic because:
- the DOM can change dynamically  
- popups and overlays often do not appear in the DOM  
- these issues frequently caused the agent to deadlock  

To address this, we switched to a **hybrid approach** using both:
- DOM snapshots  
- a **vision model** operating on page screenshots  

This allowed the agent to better understand the actual visual state of the page and make progress toward the user’s goal.

However, pure vision-based interaction was inaccurate for precise actions like clicking UI elements. To improve this, we introduced an **imaginary grid overlay** on the screenshot and mapped interactions to grid coordinates. This improved click accuracy by approximately **15%**.

Currently, we are using **free proxy vision models**, which have lower accuracy and higher latency compared to premium models. To resolve this, we have begun **fine-tuning our own model** to improve reliability and performance.

**AI Agent Security Track**

## Browser Automation Safety (Armoriq SDK + MCP)

We use **Armoriq’s SDK** with our **MCP server** to strictly control what our AI browser automation agent can do.

The agent operates on real user accounts, so all actions are **explicitly restricted and enforced at the system level**, not via prompts.

---

## How It Works

AI Agent → MCP Server → Armoriq SDK → Browser


- The agent proposes an action  
- MCP checks whether the action is allowed  
- Armoriq enforces execution rules  
- Only safe actions reach the browser  

The agent never executes actions directly.

---

## Permissions

**Allowed**
- Page navigation  
- Reading data (balances, holdings, history)  
- Fetching prices and market data  
- Screenshots and text extraction  

**Blocked**
- Buy/sell orders  
- Payments or withdrawals  
- Form submissions with side effects  
- Transaction confirmations or OTPs  

Anything not explicitly allowed is blocked by default.

---

## Result

- Financial and irreversible actions are impossible  
- Prompt injection and hallucinated actions are contained  
- Policies are immutable and enforced server-side  

This lets us safely use browser automation without risking user accounts or assets.

Team **Invincibles** -- [Turbash Negi](https://github.com/Turbash), [Aryan Mishra](github.com/alaotach), [Rohan Dev](https://github.com/nXtCyberNet), [Shikhar Agarwal](https://github.com/shikhar-ag87)

### Vendable by Copilotverse.IO
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/vendable-by-copilotverseio-ce76) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/karthikbalajikb/vendable) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://vendable.vercel.app/) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/1mPsMGZ4ZtQ) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Agentic%20Commerce%20Hackathon-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://agentic-commerce.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Paste your URL. Be buyable inside ChatGPT.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenAi](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenAi-333333?style=flat-square) ![Vercel](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vercel-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**Problem**
- My dad has run our family store for fifty years commerce is in my blood. 
- And every small seller I talk to now asks the same thing their buyers aren't just 
   Googling anymore, they're asking ChatGPT and Gemini how do I show up there? 
- My friend who runs The PrintSmith Store asked me exactly that. 
- And shoppers have their own worry can I trust paying an agent inside a chat?

**Solution**
1. That's Vendable. Give us your store URL. Our agent analyzes it.
2. Senso scores real trust signals: HTTPS, refund and policy pages, a priced catalog, a resolvable agent identity
3. Project NANDA gives it an agent‑facts identity. If a store isn't ready, we don't fake it we hand back the exact fixes. 
4. And even if they have no API, we pull their catalog into our marketplace. 
5. Once it's verified, it goes live and any shopper in ChatGPT connects through our MCP to search and check out, settled by Prava on Visa's agentic rails.

**Why Now ?**
Four years ago nobody believed we'd stop Googling and start asking. Shopping is making the same jump. These stores are already ready for Google we make them ready for Agentic Shopping, end to end. 

**Our Plan to build next**
we connect to their GitHub/Shopify/WordPress/Wix/WooCommerce and our agents make the changes, then loop until they are trustable, certified and ready for agentic payments

**Challenges we ran into**

Getting UCP merchants to talk to our agent. Real Shopify UCP stores (boAt, GIVA, Mamaearth) kept rejecting us with invalid_profile_url: "Missing profile uri". Passing the profile as a query param or an init param did nothing. I dug through Shopify's open‑source ucp-cli and found the agent identity has to ride inside the tool call params.arguments.meta["ucp-agent"].profile. Once we sent it there, we could read live catalogs from every UCP store.

"Authorized" for ₹0. Our first UCP checkout came back successful but for zero rupees. The cart was empty we'd grabbed variants[0], which happened to be out of stock, so Shopify silently made a cart with no line. Fixed by picking the first variant where availability.available === true, plus a guard that refuses any zero‑total cart.

Prava mandates + Visa cryptograms. Some fresh mandates failed with a Visa 400 "fetching cryptogram failed," and Prava allows only one charge per cycle per mandate. I isolated it with a raw curl A/B test (same failure with no Vendable code in the path), switched to an official sandbox card, and made checkout pick a mandate that's actually unspent this cycle falling back to a plain‑English message instead of a raw Visa decline

**Best Visa Intelligent Commerce Implementation**

Payments run on Prava, built on Visa's agentic‑commerce rails. The shopper approves a scoped spending mandate once with a passkey; Vendable then charges it headlessly per purchase (store‑scoped or any‑store), so an agent pays a merchant with a one‑time credential and a verifiable receipt no raw card data.

**Most Startup-Ready Product**

Vendable turns any store URL into something AI agents can discover, trust, and buy from no dev work for the merchant.

- **Live, not slides:** a public site plus a ChatGPT MCP connector where a buyer can search a catalog, set a spending mandate, and check out. Payments run on Prava (sandbox), trust checks on Senso, agent identity via NANDA agent-facts.
- **Design partner:** The PrintSmith Store is our first real merchant onboarding now; we've also demonstrated the buyer flow on live catalogs from boAt, GIVA, and Mamaearth.
- **Why now:** ChatGPT and agents are starting to shop. Every store will need to be "agent-readable" the way it needed to be Google-readable in 2004. Vendable is that layer  onboard by pasting a URL.
- **Path to revenue:** SaaS per merchant to stay agent-discoverable and trust-verified, plus a small take-rate on agent-driven checkouts.

Built by Copilotverse.IO.

**Best Prava Adapter for the NANDA Town**

https://github.com/projnanda/nandatown/pull/212

We shipped a reusable Prava payments‑layer plugin for Nanda Town's nest-core (quote/pay/verify/refund) with a Senso‑backed trust gate as the required failure case, plus a scenario, tests, and an adversarial validator  opened as PR #212 to projnanda/nandatown. Vendable also issues each store a NANDA‑compatible AgentFacts identity card, so any agent can resolve who it's paying.

**Agent Commerce Discovery & Trust**

Before any payment, Vendable verifies the merchant with Senso we ingest each store's facts into Senso's knowledge base and query it for grounded, cited answers (legitimacy, catalog, how an agent pays), not hallucinations and not a KYC checkbox. That trust check gates the payment: an unverified merchant is refused the Prava token, so buyer agents only pay stores that are actually on file.

Team **Copilotverse.IO** -- Karthik Balaji

### Truthlens
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/truthlens-3748) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/priyanshusingh303/hexafalls_2) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://truthlens.onrender.com) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/6JjEL--ZNTM?si=DuEOE5UZRBfeFqiZ) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HexaFalls%202-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hexafalls2.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> See Beyond the Surface. Trust the Truth.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Google Colab](https://img.shields.io/badge/Google%20Colab-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![MongoDB Atlas](https://img.shields.io/badge/MongoDB%20Atlas-333333?style=flat-square) ![Convolutional neural network (CNN)](https://img.shields.io/badge/Convolutional%20neural%20network%20(CNN)-333333?style=flat-square) ![Neural Network](https://img.shields.io/badge/Neural%20Network-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

One of the biggest challenges during the development of **TruthLens** was integrating multiple AI models into a single, seamless application while maintaining good performance and scalability. We had to carefully select models that balanced accuracy with computational efficiency, ensuring they could run reliably in a web-based environment. Another challenge was finding high-quality datasets for training and evaluation, particularly for audio and video deepfake detection, as publicly available datasets vary in size, quality, and format. We also faced difficulties in preprocessing different media types, managing model dependencies, and designing an explainable interface that not only provides predictions but also helps users understand why content was classified as real or fake. Additionally, deploying the application required addressing resource limitations and ensuring that all components—from the frontend and backend to the AI models and database—worked together smoothly.

**The problem it solves**

"**TruthLens**" addresses the growing challenge of identifying AI-generated misinformation by providing a unified platform to detect deepfakes across multiple media formats. As advances in generative AI have made it easier to create realistic fake images, audio, videos, and misleading textual content, verifying digital information has become increasingly difficult. **TruthLens** helps users assess the authenticity of uploaded content through multimodal AI analysis, explainable predictions, and claim verification, enabling individuals and organizations to make more informed decisions and reduce the spread of deceptive content.

**Education**

**TruthLens** has the potential to make a significant impact on education by helping students, educators, and researchers identify AI-generated or manipulated digital content before using it for learning or academic work. As generative AI becomes increasingly capable of producing realistic images, audio, videos, and text, the risk of misinformation and academic dishonesty also grows. TruthLens encourages critical thinking and digital literacy by enabling users to verify the authenticity of multimedia content through explainable AI and claim verification. By promoting informed decision-making and responsible use of AI, the platform helps create a more trustworthy and reliable digital learning environment.

**Best Use of Gemini API**

The Google Gemini API plays a key role in enhancing the intelligence and usability of TruthLens beyond traditional deepfake detection. Rather than making the detection itself, Gemini is used to generate clear, human-readable explanations of the AI model's predictions, helping users understand why a piece of content has been classified as real or fake. It is also utilized for news and claim verification by analyzing textual information and providing contextual insights into the credibility of claims. This combination of explainable AI and natural language reasoning makes the platform more transparent, user-friendly, and effective in helping users make informed decisions when evaluating potentially misleading digital content.

### PATRONUS
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/patronus-523e) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/KingDev4522/PATRONUS) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/2sFGrQ5iEq8) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HexaFalls%202-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hexafalls2.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Unmasking the Shadows of the Digital Realm

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

### 1. The VRAM Bottleneck (Single GPU Constraints)
**The Hurdle:** Our biggest challenge was running multiple heavy neural networks—like ConvNeXt V2 for spatial video analysis and Wav2Vec2 for acoustic dissection—on a single laptop GPU (an RTX 4050) without encountering CUDA Out-Of-Memory (OOM) fatal crashes. 
**How we got over it:** We designed a **"VRAM Hot-Swap Sequence."** Instead of trying to keep models loaded simultaneously, we built an asynchronous state machine. Once the video frame analysis finishes, the backend explicitly deletes the model, runs Python garbage collection (`gc.collect()`), and executes `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` to completely purge the GPU memory before rapidly loading the next model for the audio track. We used a localized SQLite DB to preserve the tracking coordinates during this handoff.

### 2. Database Race Conditions and Write Locks
**The Hurdle:** Because PATRONUS runs highly concurrent, multi-modal asynchronous tracks (analyzing video frames, audio frequencies, and API responses at the same time), using a single monolithic database resulted in massive write-locks, race conditions, and corrupted forensic logs.
**How we got over it:** We completely decentralized our data storage. We implemented an isolated micro-database architecture. Each forensics module writes strictly to its own dedicated SQLite instance (vid.db, img_forensics.db, text_forensics.db, etc.). This guaranteed parallel, zero-lock transactions and allowed all asynchronous tracks to save data safely.

### 3. High Latency During Live Inference
**The Hurdle:** Deepfake detection is computationally expensive. Running OCR, spatial bounding, and frequency analysis sequentially was taking too long, which would ruin the pacing of a live "Hacker Theatre" presentation. We needed execution times under 2 seconds.
**How we got over it:** We implemented a **Hybrid Dual-Track Framework**. We decoupled the workload: pure mathematical matrix transformations (like 2D-Discrete Cosine Transforms and STFT audio mapping) run locally on the RTX 4050, while simultaneously, the semantic reasoning and logic auditing (like analyzing document structures or phishing intent) are dispatched to the cloud via the **Gemini 2.5 Flash API**. Fusing local hardware with cloud cognitive reasoning dropped our execution latency to ~1.2 - 1.8 seconds per module.

### 4. Visualizing Complex Data (The "Hacker Theatre" UI)
**The Hurdle:** Returning raw JSON coordinates, NLP Perplexity scores, and Grad-CAM matrices to the user is visually boring and hard to understand for a non-technical audience.
**How we got over it:** We built a highly dynamic React frontend that translates math into intuition. Instead of showing raw numbers, we mapped Grad-CAM matrix weights to draw live, glowing neon heatmaps directly over manipulated faces in the video player. For NLP analysis, sentences with mathematically low perplexity (AI-generated text) are rendered with a dynamic, cascading text-shadow glow. We made the math beautiful and interactive.

**The problem it solves**

# PATRONUS

## What problem does this project solve?

In an era where generative AI can synthesize hyper-realistic deepfakes, clone voices, craft sophisticated phishing emails, and forge documents with mathematical precision, the boundary between truth and digital deception has never been more blurred.

**PATRONUS** is a comprehensive, multi-modal forensic analysis platform designed to combat this exact problem. It acts as an advanced digital truth-detector, rigorously analyzing media to identify hidden artificial manipulation. 

PATRONUS solves the growing crisis of digital trust by detecting:
*   **Video Deepfakes:** Analyzes spatial anomalies and manipulation artifacts in video frames.
*   **Image Forgery:** Uses frequency domain scanning and semantic logic auditing to catch AI-generated images and edits.
*   **Audio Spoofing & Voice Clones:** Scans vocal spectrograms for unnatural phase discontinuities and synthetic vocoder fingerprints.
*   **AI-Generated Text & Phishing:** Employs linguistic forensic metrics (perplexity/burstiness) and behavioral analysis to flag AI-written text and social engineering threats.
*   **Document Forgery:** Deconstructs PDFs and Word documents to find mathematical inconsistencies, structural template anomalies, and forged embedded assets.

By fusing local hardware neural networks with cloud-based cognitive reasoning, PATRONUS exposes hidden digital seams that are imperceptible to human senses—providing a definitive answer to the question: *"Is this real?"*

**Best Use of Gemini API**

### Why PATRONUS is the Best Use of the Gemini API

Most hackathon projects use LLMs as simple chatbots or text summarizers. We didn't do that. We built **PATRONUS**, and we deployed the **Gemini 2.5 Flash API** as the centralized, multi-modal cognitive detective of a five-layer digital forensics engine. 

We didn't just ask Gemini questions—we wired it into a highly complex, hybrid pipeline, seamlessly fusing local PyTorch mathematical computations with Gemini’s advanced cloud reasoning.

Here is how we pushed the Gemini API to its absolute limit:

#### 1. Gemini as a Visual Logic Auditor (Module 2)
Detecting AI images isn’t just about frequency noise; it's about semantic logic. While our local RTX 4050 scanned images for mathematical noise, we simultaneously fed the raw image frames into Gemini Flash with a strict forensic prompt. We used Gemini's multi-modal vision to hunt for non-Euclidean geometry—impossible shadows, asymmetrical reflections, and six-fingered hands—and forced it to return exact X/Y coordinate JSON arrays to map glowing target rings on our UI.

#### 2. Gemini as an Expert Accountant & Forgery Analyst (Module 5)
For our Document Forgery module, we used Gemini's multi-modal OCR and logic capabilities to tear apart PDFs. We didn't just ask it to read text; we instructed Gemini to **audit the math**. Gemini cross-checks table values, verifies if invoice subtotals actually balance, and hunts for chronological paradoxes (like an invoice date predating an order date) within scanned layouts. 

#### 3. Gemini as a Psychological Firewall (Module 3)
For text and phishing detection, we bypassed basic NLP. We deployed Gemini Flash as a behavioral security gateway. It ingests raw `.eml` headers and body text to map out social engineering vectors. It doesn't just say "this is spam." It pinpoints the exact sentences using psychological pressure (false urgency, authority impersonation) and returns structured probabilities.

#### 4. The Explainer Engine (Module 1)
In our Deepfake Video module, raw bounding box coordinates and audio anomalies are useless to average users. Gemini acts as our **Chronological Aggregator**. It ingests timestamps, facial coordinates, and acoustic frequency logs, synthesizing them into a cohesive, plain-English forensic narrative (e.g., *"A spatial manipulation on the jawline was detected at 00:02.150s, matching a vocoder frequency drop in the audio track."*). 

**The Verdict:**
We didn't build a wrapper. We treated the Gemini API like a senior cybersecurity forensics analyst, exploiting its multi-modal vision, structured JSON enforcement, and deep semantic logic to build an enterprise-grade truth-detector. That is why PATRONUS is the Best Use of the Gemini API.

**Best Beginner's team**

### Why PATRONUS belongs in the "Best Beginner's Team" Track

Most beginner teams build a to-do list, a basic CRUD app, or a simple chatbot wrapper. 

We looked at the state of the world—deepfakes, voice cloning, and AI-generated phishing—and decided to build a **multi-modal AI forensic truth-detector**. Did we know how to do that when we started? **Absolutely not.**

Here is why our project defines the spirit of a beginner's hackathon journey:

#### 1. Ambition Over Experience
We didn’t let our lack of experience dictate our scope. We tackled a highly advanced, enterprise-grade problem (digital forensics) and forced ourselves to learn **PyTorch, OpenCV, librosa, React, FastAPI, and the Gemini API** from scratch over the course of the hackathon. We didn't just use one API; we orchestrated a five-module sequential state machine.

#### 2. The Machine Learning Deep-Dive (Our Biggest Highlight)
As beginners, the world of AI weights and local model deployment was incredibly daunting. We didn't just plug into cloud APIs and call it a day. We learned how to source pre-trained weights from Hugging Face (like ConvNeXt V2, Wav2Vec2, and distil-roberta) and get them running locally. 
Then came the ultimate test: **at the very last minute, we were challenged to train and implement our own CNN (Convolutional Neural Network).** Instead of panicking, we immediately dove into the PyTorch documentation, gathered the data, set up the training loop on our local hardware, and successfully trained a CNN that we integrated straight into our live forensic pipeline. Pulling that off under intense hackathon time pressure was our proudest achievement.

#### 3. Beginner Hardware, Expert Engineering
We didn't have access to massive AWS enterprise clusters or $40,000 H100 GPUs. We were constrained by a single, standard RTX 4050 gaming laptop.
Critically, instead of scaling down our idea to fit our hardware, we engineered our way around the physical limits. We invented a **"VRAM Hot-Swap Sequence"**—manually purging the GPU cache (`torch.cuda.empty_cache()`) mid-execution to hot-load acoustic models right after spatial video models finished. We learned memory management the hard way: by crashing our system until we fixed it.

#### 4. Solving Critical Problems with Grit
We faced a gauntlet of critical failures. When you fire off video, audio, and text analysis asynchronously, a single database locks up and dies. As beginners, we could have given up. Instead, we learned system architecture on the fly and decentralized our backend into **five isolated SQLite micro-databases** to eliminate race conditions. Every critical problem—from CUDA out-of-memory errors to asynchronous deadlocks—was met with a gritty, engineered solution rather than pivoting to an easier idea.

#### 5. We Focused on the "Hacker Theatre"
We learned that building the math isn't enough; you have to make people understand it. So, we spent hours turning raw Grad-CAM matrices and NLP perplexity scores into a visually stunning, glowing React UI that explains exactly *why* a file is fake. 

**Conclusion:**
We fit the "Best Beginner's Team" track not because our project is simple, but precisely because it isn't. We took a beginner's baseline and stretched it to its absolute breaking point, surviving last-minute CNN training challenges, GPU crashes, and database deadlocks. We relied on relentless problem-solving, critical thinking, and a little bit of witty engineering to pull off a project that looks like it was built by a senior security team.

> Everyone deserves a chance to code.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![RestAPIs](https://img.shields.io/badge/RestAPIs-333333?style=flat-square) ![langchain](https://img.shields.io/badge/langchain-333333?style=flat-square) ![LangGraph](https://img.shields.io/badge/LangGraph-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

When merging the **Manual Assistance Streaming** and **Copilot Assistance Streaming** feature branches into main, an architectural integrity challenge emerged as both features collided over background IDE event handling.

**The problem it solves**

**The Problem**

Blind and low-vision developers already code fluently — using screen readers (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver), refreshable Braille displays, and keyboard-first workflows. What breaks that fluency is the AI-development era. When GitHub Copilot generates code, or a Git diff spans multiple files, standard screen readers either go completely silent — **"dead air"** while the machine works — or read raw diff punctuation character by character (+, -, {, }), forcing severe cognitive overload just to understand what an AI assistant changed. Screen readers made text accessible decades ago; they were never built for AI tools that generate, narrate, and modify code faster than any linear read-aloud can keep up with.

**The Solution**

**Angizo** is an *audio-first AI accessibility layer that sits between blind developers and the AI tools they already use* — the missing translation layer between what those tools do and what a screen-reader user can actually perceive. It's built around a 7-node LangGraph state machine (understand → context → reason → confirm → execute → verify → narrate), Sarvam AI for low-latency streaming speech, **ElevenLabs** for expressive risk-modulated narration, and Gemini flash for diff intelligence and architectural summaries — all delivered through a custom VS Code extension.

**Architecture at a Glance**

Three observer modules — **CopilotWatcher, ManualWatcher, TerminalWatcher** — that stream events over WebSocket to a Python FastAPI backend. The backend's LangGraph agent orchestrates Git operations (GitPython), diff/code intelligence (Gemini), and voice output (Sarvam + ElevenLabs), streaming narration audio back to a webview for playback. It's a modular monolith by design — kept intentionally simple for demo reliability rather than split into microservices that would add latency exactly where responsiveness matters most.

**Best Use of ElevenLabs**

ElevenLabs is Angizo's primary voice layer — every risk-classified narration (diff summaries, commit confirmations, Git status updates) is delivered through ElevenLabs' expressive, high-fidelity TTS, with tone deliberately modulated by risk level.

**Best Use of Gemini API**

Gemini is **Angizo**'s primary reasoning engine — not a bolted-on feature, but the **brain behind the entire agentic loop**. Every diff gets classified and risk-scored by Gemini, every commit message is drafted by Gemini reading the actual working-tree diff.

**Open Innovation**

**Angizo** tackles a genuinely unaddressed gap — the accessibility blind spot AI-assisted development has quietly introduced, where screen readers built for static text go silent against Copilot's real-time generation and multi-file diffs.

**The problem it solves**

Vision Guard solves the problem of delayed or ineffective monitoring in shared and vulnerable spaces such as hostels, elderly care homes, campuses, and residential areas.

Traditional CCTV systems only record footage and require humans to constantly watch screens, making it difficult to detect emergencies or suspicious activity in real time.

Vision Guard uses AI-powered computer vision to:

detect overcrowding and potential ragging situations

identify aggressive or unusual motion

detect possible falls or medical emergencies

generate real-time threat analysis

capture evidence screenshots automatically

alert authorities instantly without continuous manual monitoring

The system transforms passive CCTV cameras into an intelligent real-time safety and surveillance assistant.

**Challenges we ran into**

Stabilizing person tracking IDs in real time, since IDs kept changing between frames.


Reducing false alerts in fall detection and aggressive motion detection.


Optimizing YOLOv8 + OpenCV performance for smooth real-time processing on macOS.


Integrating the AI backend with a live Flask dashboard and browser-based video streaming.


Managing real-time evidence capture, alert generation, and UI updates simultaneously.


Handling GitHub large-file issues caused by AI model files and virtual environments.

technology used:

Most CCTV systems only record incidents. Vision Guard AI is designed to proactively identify overcrowding and risk conditions before situations escalate.

3.⁠ ⁠We Built a Real-Time Intelligent Decision System

Our system dynamically analyzes live crowd conditions, calculates behavior and density metrics, and generates intelligent alerts in real time.

4.⁠ ⁠We Optimized Resource Usage

instead of continuously storing unnecessary footage, the system intelligently captures evidence only during alert conditions.

5.⁠ ⁠We Designed It for Real-World Scalability

The architecture can be extended to railway stations, stadiums, smart cities, disaster management systems, airports, and public event monitoring.

What makes our team innovative is that we didn’t just build software — we engineered an intelligent real-time safety ecosystem that combines AI, analytics, automation, and practical deployment possibilities.

[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/humanitycheck-e4d4) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/11Pk/HumanityCheck.git) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackMol%207.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackmol-7.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Detecting what’s real in the age of AI

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Face Recognition](https://img.shields.io/badge/Face%20Recognition-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

- AI-generated chats and bots make it difficult to know if you’re talking to a real person particularly on Telegram
- Fake profiles and identities are widely used for scams, impersonation, and misinformation on Twitter
- Manipulated media (deepfakes) can deceive users and spread false content particularly on Twitter.
- Pre-recorded or AI-generated video feeds can bypass trust in interviews and live interactions particularly on Google Meet.

**Challenges we ran into**

- DOM Scraping Complexity
Extracting chat data, profile details, and media from web platforms was difficult due to dynamic and frequently changing DOM structures.
Solution: Used more robust selectors and handled dynamic content with event listeners and fallback parsing methods to ensure consistent data extraction.

- Handling Long-Length Videos
Processing long video streams in real-time caused performance issues and delays in detection.
Solution: Optimized by sampling key frames instead of analyzing the entire video and focused only on critical segments for faster and efficient processing.

- Balancing Performance & Speed
Running detection in real-time without lag was difficult.
Solution: Optimized processing by prioritizing lightweight checks and reducing unnecessary computations.

**Main Track: The Deepforge Arena**

HumanityCheck fits into the main track by addressing a real-world problem of growing importance—trust in digital interactions. With the rise of AI-generated content, deepfakes, and bots, verifying human authenticity has become critical across domains like recruitment, social media, and online transactions. The project provides a scalable and practical solution that enhances security, reduces fraud, and builds trust in modern digital ecosystems.

**Women Track: The Queen’s Vanguard**

HumanityCheck aligns with the all girls track by showcasing innovation, technical problem-solving, and impactful thinking led by women in tech. It highlights the ability to tackle complex challenges like AI detection and digital safety while contributing to a safer online environment. The project reflects creativity, confidence, and leadership in building solutions that address meaningful societal issues.

### PersonaLens
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/personalens-89a4) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/harshith-ashok/persona-lens) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/OSzM6YMrMGI) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-DevsHouse%20'26-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://devshouse26.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> See the face, know the story.

![Vue.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vue.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flutter](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flutter-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![FAST API](https://img.shields.io/badge/FAST%20API-333333?style=flat-square) ![Face Recognition](https://img.shields.io/badge/Face%20Recognition-333333?style=flat-square) ![Supabase](https://img.shields.io/badge/Supabase-333333?style=flat-square) ![openAI whisper](https://img.shields.io/badge/openAI%20whisper-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

People with memory-related conditions, such as dementia, often struggle to recognise familiar faces and recall past interactions. This can cause confusion, anxiety, and reduced independence in daily life. Caregivers and family members also face challenges in tracking interactions and providing contextual support when they are not physically present.

---

How This Helps



PersonaLens combines face recognition with contextual memory recall to address these challenges.

For Patients

* Instantly recognises people in their surroundings.
* Displays names and relationships (e.g., “This is your daughter”).
* Shows a summary of the last interaction, aiding memory recall.
* Reduces anxiety in social situations and improves confidence.

For Working Professionals

* Automatically logs discussions and interactions.
* Generates summaries of past conversations.
* Provides insight into who you met, when, and key discussion points.
* Supports better follow-ups and relationship management without manual notes.

For Caregivers and Family

* Automatically logs interactions and conversations.
* Generates summaries of past interactions.
* Provides insight into who the patient met, when, and what was discussed.
* Supports remote monitoring of cognitive health and social engagement.

---

Benefits

* Eliminates the need for patients to manually remember faces.
* Makes social interactions less stressful and more natural.
* Enables continuous passive memory support.
* Improves patient safety by reducing disorientation.
* Creates a digital memory layer for real-world interactions.

---

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges We Ran Into

* **Face Recognition Accuracy** – Initially, the model misidentified faces or returned “unknown” too frequently.
    * **Solution**: Implemented a confidence-based filtering system and allowed manual assignment of names for unrecognised faces.
* **Real-Time Image Upload and Processing** – Uploading images to Supabase storage and calling the recognition API resulted in delays and a poor UX.
    * **Solution**: Added a progress indicator and optimised the upload flow to handle large images efficiently.
* **Managing Known Persons and Relationships** – Handling duplicate entries or linking the same person across multiple images was tricky.
    * **Solution**: Built logic to check existing records and reuse known_person IDs to avoid duplicates.
* **Fetching Summaries for the Gallery** – Displaying the last conversation summary required querying multiple tables, leading to slow load times.
    * **Solution**: Batched queries and added fallback mechanisms to ensure smooth rendering even if some data was missing.
* **Error Handling & Authentication** – Handling expired sessions or unauthorised API calls caused failures.
    * **Solution**: Implemented proper session checks and clear error messages to guide users.
* **Privacy & Security of Face Data** – Ensuring sensitive biometric data is handled securely was critical.
    * **Solution**: The app only captures face-structure embeddings, not raw photos from the camera lens. All AI processing for conversation summarisation is done locally on the device, meaning no raw images or personal context leave the device.

**AR & VR**

AR Integration

* Face Tracking & Highlighting: Detects faces in real-time and draws bounding boxes around them.
* Contextual Overlay: Shows relevant information (e.g., past interactions, relationship, conversation summary) over each tracked face.
* Depth Awareness: Uses depth data to accurately position overlays in 3D space, maintaining proper alignment as users move.
* Hands-Free Assistance: Provides context automatically, improving situational awareness without manual input.

### Robust off-road semantic segmentation
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/robust-offroad-semantic-segmentation-4bc6) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/thetruth65/payload-offroad-segmentation-dino-attn-unet) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://offroad-segmentation-blog.vercel.app/) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-PayLoad'26-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://pay-load.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-3-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Beyond roads,Beyond assumptions.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**What This Project Actually Does**
This project helps self-driving vehicles navigate off-road terrain by teaching them to "see" and understand what's around them. Think of it as giving autonomous vehicles the ability to distinguish between safe ground and obstacles when there are no roads to follow.
**The Real-World Problem**
When you're driving off-road (whether in a desert, forest, or rural area) there are no lane markings, signs, or clear paths. You just have sand, rocks, bushes, and constantly changing terrain. For autonomous vehicles, this is incredibly challenging. They need to figure out in real-time: "Can I drive over this? Is that a rock or just a shadow? Should I avoid those bushes?"
**Who Benefits and How**
*Autonomous Vehicles in Tough Environments*

Military vehicles navigating unknown terrain for reconnaissance missions
Agricultural robots working across fields with varying conditions
Mars rovers exploring planetary surfaces
Search and rescue vehicles operating in disaster zones

*Making Off-Road Travel Safer*

Spots dangerous obstacles like hidden rocks or fallen logs before you hit them
Identifies terrain that might damage your vehicle or cause it to get stuck
Acts as an early warning system, even for human drivers in off-road vehicles

*Saving Time and Money*
Here's something clever: instead of driving around deserts for months collecting training data (which is expensive and tedious), they trained the system using synthetic images. Basically, high-quality computer simulations of desert environments. This cuts development time dramatically and makes the whole process more affordable.
**What Makes It Work Well**
The system handles real-world challenges that would trip up simpler approaches:

Different lighting conditions - whether it's bright noon sun, early morning shadows, or overcast skies
Confusing textures - telling apart dry grass from sand, or rocks from shadows
Speed - processes images fast enough to actually be useful while moving

One of the coolest parts? They trained it on one simulated desert environment, but it works on completely different desert scenes it's never seen before. That's huge, because it means the system isn't just memorizing. It's actually learning to understand terrain.
Why It Matters
Right now, self-driving technology mostly works on normal streets and highways. This project pushes that boundary into the messy, unpredictable real world. Places where autonomous systems could genuinely help people, from farmers to soldiers to scientists exploring other planets.







![image](https://assets.devfolio.co/content/f1d82ef5b307492c8c3f24be7c0eaa8e/2167ca19-49d8-469a-a473-88ff626a8c43.jpeg)

**Challenges we ran into**

**The Problem That Kept Me Up at Night**
About halfway through the project, I hit what I thought was a "good enough" result – around 45% validation mIoU. I was ready to call it a day until I decided to check the per-class breakdown. That's when my heart sank:

Logs: 0.00% IoU – The model couldn't see them at all
Ground Clutter: 0.00% IoU – Completely invisible
Lush Bushes: 0.02% IoU – Might as well be zero

The model was essentially painting over small objects with "Landscape" or "Sky" and calling it a day. Everything tiny just... disappeared.
**Figuring Out What Was Actually Wrong**
I spent almost two days just trying to understand why this was happening. Here's what I discovered:
The Resolution Problem:
DINOv2 works with 14×14 pixel patches. So my 448×448 images get divided into a 32×32 grid. Now imagine a log that's only 5-10 pixels wide – it gets completely absorbed into whatever patch it's sitting in, usually sand or grass. By the time the encoder finishes processing, the log's features are already mixed in with the background. The decoder never even gets a chance to recover them.
The Class Imbalance Problem:
The dataset is heavily skewed. Sky and Landscape together make up over 50% of all pixels, while Logs are less than 1%. The loss function basically goes "eh, who cares about logs when I can get easy wins on sky pixels?" and optimizes accordingly.
The Failed Experiments (aka My Week of Frustration)
I tried so many different decoders, convinced one of them would magically fix everything:
DeepLabV3+ with ASPP
My thinking: *"Multi-scale convolutions should catch objects at different sizes, right?"*
Reality: Helped a bit with medium-sized bushes, but small objects stayed at 0%. The problem was that ASPP operates on already-compressed features – the small objects were already lost.
OCRNet
My thinking: *"Object-contextual representations sound fancy and perfect for this!"*
Reality: Got logs up to maybe 5% IoU. Better than nothing, but OCRNet really wants strong object proposals to work with, and our frozen encoder wasn't providing them.
HRNet
My thinking: *"High-resolution features throughout the network – this has to work!"*
Reality: Boundaries looked cleaner, but small objects were still mostly missed. The issue is HRNet really needs end-to-end training with the encoder, and I couldn't unfreeze DINOv2 without overfitting on our small dataset.
SegFormer
My thinking: *"Transformers are the future, let's try the transformer decoder!"*
Reality: Actually made things worse. The predictions got noisier and slower to train. SegFormer's lightweight MLP decoder just couldn't handle the frozen DINOv2 features well.
Vanilla U-Net
My thinking: *"Maybe I'm overthinking this. Let's go back to basics."*
Reality: Finally saw some progress – logs jumped to around 8% IoU. But the predictions were super noisy because the skip connections were passing everything from the encoder, including tons of irrelevant background texture.
**The Breakthrough: Attention Gates**
On my fourth attempt at decoder architectures, I tried Attention U-Net. The idea clicked immediately when I saw the first results.
The key difference: instead of dumping all encoder features into the decoder, Attention Gates act like a smart filter. The decoder says "I'm looking for a log here" and the attention gate highlights only the parts of the encoder features that look like logs, suppressing all the sand and sky noise.
Here's what changed visually: before, when the decoder was trying to segment a log, it was drowning in textures from the surrounding sand. After adding attention gates, it could actually focus on the wood grain and edges.
The results spoke for themselves – logs went from invisible to at least detectable, ground clutter started showing up in predictions, and those tiny lush bushes finally appeared. Overall performance jumped noticeably.
**Other Tricks That Helped**
Copy-Paste Augmentation: This felt a bit hacky at first, but it worked. I literally cut out log instances from some training images and pasted them onto sky/landscape regions in other images. The model needed to see more examples of these rare objects, and copy-paste was the fastest way to create them artificially.
Weighted Sampling: Instead of uniformly sampling training images, I made the dataloader pick images containing logs or clutter 5× more often. This way, every epoch had way more exposure to rare classes without actually duplicating the entire dataset.
Texture Diversity Through Photometric Augmentation: I added more aggressive brightness, blur, and noise variations specifically to help the decoder learn that a "log" can appear under different lighting conditions and still be a log. The goal was to make the model rely on shape and edges rather than just color.
**What I Learned (The Hard Way)**
No single fix worked – it took augmentation + oversampling + architectural changes all working together. Class imbalance is genuinely hard, but tackling it from multiple angles made.

Team **Theist** -- [Lavanay Talwar](https://github.com/lavanaytalwar), [Laksh Agrawal](https://github.com/LakshAgrawal28), [AKASH VERMA](https://github.com/Akash-Verma-learns), [Mohit Sharma](https://github.com/thetruth65)

**The problem it solves**

Traditional racing games rely on physical controllers or keyboards, which limit immersion and accessibility. HandOverDrive solves this by enabling gesture‑based vehicle control using hand movements captured via a webcam.

By removing the need for controllers, the game offers:

A more immersive and intuitive racing experience

Hands‑free interaction, making gameplay more engaging and modern

A demonstration of how computer vision and hand‑tracking can be applied to real‑time control systems

This project also showcases the potential of gesture control in future gaming, simulators, and touchless interfaces, making interactions more natural, accessible, and innovative.

### Foundry
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/foundry-0958) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/SrihariSr/Foundry) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Push%20to%20Prod%20Hackathon:%20Building%20at%20the%20Frontier-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://pushtoprod-india.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Taking frontier intelligence to the frontier.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![JSON](https://img.shields.io/badge/JSON-333333?style=flat-square)

**What is the deployed URL for this project?**

https://github.com/SrihariSr/Foundry

**How Did You Use Claude?**

Claude is the product, not an accessory. Remove it and there's nothing left to run.

As the intelligence layer: Sonnet turns one sentence of plain English into a structured task definition: label names, precise definitions, edge cases, and writer personas. Haiku 4.5 then generates several thousand labelled examples through parallel calls, each with a different persona and randomised length, formality, typo rate and code-mixing. That corpus is the six weeks of human annotation a small classifier normally needs. One reasoning system at build time, zero at run time.

As the benchmark: Haiku zero-shot is the reference our own model is measured against, on the same held-out sets, with matched latency, tokens and cost. That's the core evidence: 195 of 200 correct against Haiku's 195 of 200, at $0 versus $352 per million.

**How you are solving it?**

Approach: Use Claude to manufacture the labelled data that was the bottleneck, then compile it into a model small enough to go where Claude cannot. Claude is the factory, not the runtime.

What I built: Describe a task in one sentence. Claude writes the label scheme, generates the training corpus, and generates a separate held-out set from a different seed. We train a small network on it and emit a single Python file with int8 weights embedded, standard library only.
Four minutes. 690KB. 2.3ms latency. There's also a local browser user interface for building and testing models.

What I measured: Training on 50 examples gives 47% on a six-class triage task. 4000 generated examples gives 95%, statistically indistinguishable from Haiku across 200 held-out examples, at $0 against $352 per million. The same pipeline built three models across disaster triage, SMS intent and supermarket orders with nothing changed but the input sentence.

Every emitted model is verified in a fresh subprocess under a changed hash seed and must match the trained model exactly. We also hand-wrote an evaluation set outside the pipeline, which revealed our synthetic test set saturates at 200 examples while real-world accuracy keeps climbing to 4000.

Limits: It cannot reason and will never beat its teacher. Training data is synthetic. Devanagari input fails. No component is novel in isolation; what changed is one sentence and four minutes instead of an ML team and six weeks.

Prior use: All code was written during the hackathon from an empty directory. Nothing reused, not submitted elsewhere. Full commit history is public.

**What is the problem your project solves?**

Frontier models cannot go where most of the world's text lives: offline, on-device, inside data that legally cannot be transmitted, or in hot paths where 800ms of network latency is unacceptable.

The obvious answer is a small local classifier. The model is trivial to build. The reason companies do not have fifty of them is that each needs several thousand labelled examples, and that means annotators and six weeks. So most teams ship a regex instead, or nothing. It also puts this entire category of software out of reach of small teams and NGOs, who have the problem but not the ML budget.

Disaster triage where no dataset can exist yet. On-device fraud detection on messages that should never leave the phone. Moderation at tens of millions of messages a day. Any category newer than the data describing it.

The bottleneck was never the model. It was the data.

Srihari Srinivasan

> Privacy is first

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![AI/ML](https://img.shields.io/badge/AI/ML-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

# Consensia Agent

> Transforming social media noise into verified blockchain intelligence.

Consensia Agent is a multi-layered AI system built to solve the **Web3 Information Gap**.
In a market where seconds can cost millions, Consensia acts like an autonomous detective,
cross-referencing high-speed social signals with immutable on-chain facts.

---

## Core Innovation: Dual-SLM Pipeline

Unlike generic LLM wrappers, Consensia uses a cascaded architecture of two specialized
Small Language Models (SLMs), each optimized for a specific Web3 task.

| Layer | Model | Role | Primary Objective |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Tagger (1.5B) | The Sentinel | High-speed stream sanitization |
| 2 | Analyzer (3B) | The Strategist | Deep reasoning and investigative tool-calling |

---

## 1) Tagger (1.5B): The Sentinel

**Purpose**

High-speed stream sanitization.

**Function**

- Instantly filters out up to 90% of noise: scams, bot spam, and unrelated chatter.
- Maps raw text into a strict, structured Web3 taxonomy.

**Optimization**

- Trained on a custom-built dataset to enforce schema consistency.
- Ensures every output is compatible with downstream database queries.

---

## 2) Analyzer (3B): The Strategist

**Purpose**

Deep reasoning and investigative tool-calling.

**Function**

- Applies Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning to evaluate Tagger output.
- When a signal is high-risk, it autonomously triggers blockchain investigation tools.

**Dynamic Context Loop**

- `REQUEST_CONTEXT`: fetches missing on-chain data needed for confident decisions.
- `DROP_CONTEXT`: discards unconfirmed rumors through negative-learning behavior.

---

## Why It Matters

Consensia is designed for high-frequency, high-uncertainty environments where latency and
verifiability are critical. The pipeline separates fast filtering from deep analysis,
creating a system that is both scalable and reliable for Web3 intelligence workflows.

**Challenges we ran into**

# 🛠 Technical Excellence & Challenges

## 🧠 Solving the "Hallucinating Taxonomy"

Early iterations of our **1.5B model** invented non-existent tags. We solved this by implementing **Strict Schema Enforcement**.

**Approach:**
- "Teacher-Student" training approach with GPT-4o
- Generated **18,000+** high-fidelity entries
- Forced the model to operate within a **fixed, standardized Web3 vocabulary**

---

## ⚖️ Action Disconnect: Reasoning vs. Execution

To prevent the model from "guessing" facts before seeing blockchain data, we architected a **Two-Pass Reasoning Loop**:

1. **Decision Pass** — The model determines if a tool call is needed.
2. **Synthesis Pass** — The model generates a final report only after receiving real-time transaction data from the orchestrator.

---

## 🧹 Cognitive Load Reduction (Data Washing)

Raw social media data is messy. We implemented a **Pre-processing RegEx Pipeline** that:
- Strips artifacts (`&amp;`, bot-tags, broken URLs)
- Processes data before it reaches the SLM
- **Reduced token consumption** by focusing 100% on economic signals
- **Significantly increased classification accuracy**

---

## 📡 Stream-to-Disk Architecture

To handle massive data generation without loss:
- Moved away from **RAM-heavy processes**
- Implemented **Stream-to-Disk** approach
- Immediate file flushing after every API interaction
- **Ensured data integrity** and **100% resilience** against network failures or rate-limiting

---

## 🌐 Vision: The Future of On-Chain Truth

**Consensia** is designed to be **Edge-Ready**:
- **1.5B and 3B models** optimized for decentralized compute
- Runs on platforms like **SpaceComputer**
- The "Truth Engine" of Web3 remains **censorship-resistant** as the blockchain itself

**Ethereum Core**

Consensia Agent directly improves Ethereum's security and usability by building an automated, verifiable safety layer. We solve the "Information Asymmetry" problem where users lose funds due to unverified social media rumors by creating an autonomous infrastructure that cross-references social signals with on-chain truth. Our use of specialized SLMs (1.5B/3B) provides a faster, more scalable way to monitor network health than human-led analysis.

**SpaceComputer Bounty**

Our architecture is built around Small Language Models (1.5B and 3B) specifically because they are "Edge-Ready." Unlike massive LLMs, Consensia is optimized to run on decentralized compute providers like SpaceComputer, ensuring our "Truth Engine" remains censorship-resistant and private.

**Best Agentic Venture**

Consensia Agent is built as a venture-ready security infrastructure. We address a multi-million dollar pain point: the lag between social media "FUD" and on-chain verification. By automating the role of a "Security Analyst" through our Dual-SLM pipeline, we create a scalable B2B/B2C product that protects protocols and retail users alike. Our architecture is designed for production-grade reliability, featuring a Stream-to-Disk data engine and autonomous reasoning loops, making it a prime candidate for long-term venture development in the Web3 security space.

**Verified Fetch — Trust No Gateway**

To ensure the audit trail of our investigations is tamper-proof, we utilize Swarm for decentralized storage of our "Investigation Logs." We use ENS to provide a persistent, portable identity for our Autonomous Agents, allowing them to sign their verification reports with a recognized on-chain identity.

Team **Consensia Team** -- [Andrii Shumko](https://github.com/Ashixi), [Illia Andreluka](https://github.com/Illya301a)

### AurumThread
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/aurumthread-48ed) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/nashmallinath-rgb/AurumThread) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/MbpCmGQ7inc) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-NMIT%20HACKS%202026-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://nmithacks26.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Scan it. Know it. Act on it.

![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![REST API](https://img.shields.io/badge/REST%20API-333333?style=flat-square) ![WebSockets](https://img.shields.io/badge/WebSockets-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tesseract OCR](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tesseract%20OCR-333333?style=flat-square) ![Pillow](https://img.shields.io/badge/Pillow-333333?style=flat-square) ![Uvicorn](https://img.shields.io/badge/Uvicorn-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Fast fashion produces 92 million tonnes of textile waste annually, and a significant portion of fabric labels misrepresent actual composition — a practice known as greenwashing. Organizations running clothing collection drives, retail take-back programs, and NGO sorting facilities have no reliable way to verify what fabric they're actually handling. Manual sorting is slow, costly, and inaccurate. Consumers have no tool to verify fabric claims before purchasing.
Aurum Thread solves this by providing real-time spectral fabric forensics — place any garment on the rig, get an AUTHENTIC / GREENWASHED / INCONCLUSIVE verdict in seconds, with a full sustainability score, recyclability rating, lifespan prediction, and end-of-life routing decision. No manual input. No guesswork. Just data.

**Challenges we ran into**

The biggest challenge was achieving meaningful fabric differentiation from spectral sensor data alone. The AS7262 operates in the visible light spectrum rather than NIR, which required building a custom spectral profile knowledge base mapped to known textile signatures to give the LLM meaningful anchoring data rather than raw numbers.
Hardware integration was another significant challenge — soldering and testing three sensors simultaneously on an ESP32 while managing I2C bus conflicts, logic level conversion for the 5V AS7262, and stable WebSocket broadcasting required extensive iteration.
On the software side, Groq API rate limits and network restrictions at the venue forced a mid-hackathon pivot to Ollama for local LLM inference, requiring rapid refactoring of the entire analysis pipeline. The WebSocket bridge between ESP32, FastAPI, and the React frontend also required significant debugging to achieve stable real-time data flow.

**Internet of Things**

**Aurum Thread** is fundamentally an IoT system at its core. The project integrates custom embedded hardware — an ESP32 microcontroller orchestrating three precision sensors — with a cloud-connected AI pipeline, which is the defining architecture of modern IoT solutions.

The ESP32 serves as the edge device, performing real-time sensor fusion across the AS7262 spectral sensor, MPR121 capacitive touch interface, and HX711 load cell ADC. Raw sensor data is processed at the edge and broadcast via WebSocket over WiFi to the backend — a classic IoT edge-to-cloud data flow.

The system demonstrates key IoT principles: persistent device connectivity, real-time data streaming, sensor fusion for environmental inference, and actionable decision-making based on physical world inputs. Rather than monitoring environmental conditions, Aurum Thread applies these principles to material forensics — using multi-modal sensor data to make fabric composition determinations that would otherwise require laboratory equipment.

The deployment model is also inherently IoT — the hardware rig operates as a fixed installation at a collection point, continuously available to process garments on demand, with all intelligence residing in the connected backend. This mirrors industrial IoT deployments where edge sensors feed centralized processing systems.

In summary, Aurum Thread represents applied IoT in the sustainability domain: physical sensors, embedded firmware, wireless connectivity, real-time data processing, and AI-driven decision making — all working together to solve a real-world operational problem at scale.

[Nisha Mallinath](https://github.com/nashmallinath-rgb)
