> AI BASED B2B PROCUREMENT SYSTEM

![Next.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Next.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![Neural Network](https://img.shields.io/badge/Neural%20Network-333333?style=flat-square) ![AI/ML](https://img.shields.io/badge/AI/ML-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

# THE PROBLEM STATEMENT 
Traditional rags are very token expensive and as the context and files increases the LLM model eventually collapses the context and starts hallucination. So, it is context expensive can take upto 4000-7000 token in a single prompt and it can also hallucinate. Also all the files are chuncked so model have to parse all the files again and again for every prompt given to it.

# THE SOLUTION
1. Build a knowledge system with nodes where each node represent a data point connected to other nodes which are related data points connect by weighted graph data structure. Now whenever any prompt is given the model first create isolated nodes then started connecting nodes and then increases the weights, So the knowledge graph for each user is unique and each user gets there specific response, the model on parses the important nodes.

# WHAT IT SOLVES, SCALABILITY AND APPLICATION
1. Traditional RAG systems are not token effiecient it is 70%-80% more token efficient than traditional RAGs
2. It can be implemented to any traditional RAG application which halluciates due to huge context, in the architecture the model knows just two thing from which node I came from and on which node I am on, and decides which node to go next based on the weights of the graph so that the user query can be solved with minimun tokens and appropriate answer.

# WHAT WE HAVE BUILT
1. An AI based B2B procurement system, where you can do anything in the website from searching seller, to product to adding order to cart to placing order, the only thing user need to do is payment.

2. A user based customized knowledge graph, where each seller, product, category and chat thread is a node and based on this node types and parameters such as number of times a product is queried, number of time the same product brought and all the model decides which node to jump and increases the weight of the path while jumping which make the path more important, So next time when the model is again on the same node it knows which node to go next.

**Challenges we ran into**

Initially in the knowledge graph we used undirected graph, which would have been a bottle neck as the application scaled becase the model will have to travel the whole graph in worst case, but after taking suggestions from a mentor we have implemented a directed graph now the problem of attention and decision making is solved, this transition from undirected to directed graph was biggest challenge to implement.

**BEST INNOVATIVE IDEA**

It changes the traditional RAG system to something which is 80% more token efficient and time efficent than traditional RAG.

### LOOPZ
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/loopz-c1eb) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Shiv0087/loopz) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://pypi.org/project/loopz/) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Devlynix%20Buildathon%201.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://devlynix-buildathon.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Never lose loop progress again.

![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![scikit-learn](https://img.shields.io/badge/scikit--learn-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyPi](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyPi-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Every ML engineer, data scientist, and CS student has felt this exact moment. You are 6 hours into a training run. Loss is dropping. Accuracy is climbing. Then your Colab session disconnects, your laptop dies, or your server restarts — and you are back at epoch 1. Again.
This is not a rare edge case. This is the daily reality of anyone training models on free-tier cloud GPUs, shared university servers, or underpowered personal machines. Millions of GPU-hours are wasted every year re-doing work that was already done — not because the model was wrong, but because the infrastructure failed and there was no safety net.
loopz is that safety net.
It is a Python library that adds automatic crash recovery to any loop with a single decorator. You do not rewrite your training logic. You do not add boilerplate. You add @loopz.track(...) above your function and from that point every checkpoint is saved atomically. Crash at step 60,000 out of 100,000 — run the same script again and it resumes from exactly step 60,000.
For ML training, loopz saves and restores the complete training state: model weights, optimizer, learning rate scheduler, random seeds for deterministic resume, and any custom loop variables like running loss or best accuracy. The crash never happened as far as your training loop is concerned.
This project has no flashy frontend — and that is intentional. The interface for a developer tool is the terminal, the decorator, and the status output. The real deliverable is a working, published library that solves a problem every developer in this community has personally felt. It is live on PyPI right now — pip install loopz — built by a solo developer from India who lost one too many hours of Colab training.

**Who it helps:**
- ML students running experiments on Colab that disconnects
- Data engineers processing large datasets that can crash midway
- Researchers training models overnight on laptops that might die
- Anyone running any long Python loop that cannot afford to restart

**What makes loopz different:**
Unlike manual checkpointing which requires writing complex save/load logic inside your loop, loopz requires zero changes inside your loop body. It automatically saves your loop position, model weights, optimizer state, LR scheduler, GradScaler, random seed state, and custom accumulators — and restores all of them on the next run deterministically.

`pip install loopz` — that is the entire setup.

**Challenges we ran into**

Atomic writes without corruption. The scariest failure mode was not a crash — it was a crash during a checkpoint write. If loopz is saving your model at epoch 30 and the system dies mid-write, you get a corrupt file. Next time you resume, you load broken weights and get silently wrong results — which is worse than starting over. The fix was atomic writes: loopz writes to a temporary file first, then renames it. A file rename is atomic on every major OS, so the checkpoint is either fully written or completely untouched. Never half-broken.
Restoring random state for deterministic resume. Saving model weights and optimizer state is the obvious part. The non-obvious part is random seeds. If Python, NumPy, and PyTorch random states are not restored exactly, the resumed training takes a different random path than it would have without the crash — meaning your results are no longer reproducible. loopz captures and restores the full random state across all three so the resumed run is identical to an uninterrupted one.
Primitive loop variables cannot be tracked by reference. If a user writes loss = 0.0 and updates it as loss += value, loopz cannot track that mutation — Python creates a new float object on every assignment. This was a real API design problem. The solution was a documented pattern: wrap primitives in a list (loss = [0.0]), which is mutable and trackable. Simple fix, but it took real thought to arrive at a solution that did not make the API ugly or confusing.
Supporting PyTorch DDP and DataParallel. These wrappers store the actual model under a .module attribute instead of directly. loopz had to detect these wrappers, unwrap the underlying module to save the state dict correctly, and restore it cleanly — without the user needing to know or do anything differently.

Shivrajsinh Jadeja

**The problem it solves**

## 1) Conversational Investment Assistant (LangGraph Trading Agent)

Interact with the system like a chatbot:
- “Should I buy AAPL?”  
- “What’s happening in tech?”  

The agent orchestrates multiple steps behind the scenes:
- Fetches market data  
- Analyzes news sentiment  
- Applies risk framing  

It then returns a **structured recommendation** such as:
- **BUY / HOLD / SELL**
- Confidence score  
- Risk context  

This removes the need to manually combine charts, headlines, and personal analysis each time, offering a streamlined decision-support experience.

---

## 2) API Backend for Sentiment, Forecasting & Portfolio Insights  
*(FastAPI + Hugging Face Spaces Ready)*

A Dockerized backend exposes intelligent endpoints:

- **Sector Sentiment**
  - Includes an “external headlines mode” to avoid unreliable scraping  

- **Stock Forecast**
  - Short-term predictions using:
    - Technical indicators  
    - Sentiment signals  

- **Portfolio AI Insights**
  - Generates buy/sell suggestions  
  - Optionally includes natural-language explanations  

This modular API design allows seamless integration with:
- Web applications or dashboards  
- Mobile clients  
- Automated workflows (e.g., cron jobs storing insights periodically)  

---

## 3) Stock Relationship Exploration (Network Analysis UIs)

The system supports two complementary approaches to analyzing stock relationships:

### Streamlit-Based Analyzer
- Correlation analysis  
- Lagged correlation insights  
- Clustering of related stocks  
- Interactive charts for exploration  

### Neo4j Graph-Based System
- Represents:
  - Companies  
  - Products  
  - News  
as interconnected nodes  

- Enables:
  - Rich graph queries  
  - Relationship discovery  
  - Advanced visualization of financial ecosystems  

This dual approach provides both **statistical insight** and **semantic graph exploration**, making it easier to uncover hidden connections across the market.

---

**Challenges we ran into**

# Engineering Challenges & Solutions

## 1) External Data Brittleness (Market + News APIs)

**Hurdle:**  
Real-time finance pipelines often fail due to:
- Rate limits  
- Regional restrictions  
- Transient HTTP errors  
- Schema changes  
- Empty or partial responses (e.g., incomplete `yfinance` frames, missing news data)  

These failures can silently corrupt downstream LLM/ML processing.

**Solution:**  
Treated external data as an unreliable dependency:
- Added strict validation:
  - Empty/NaN checks  
  - Minimum row thresholds  
  - Type validation  
- Implemented graceful degradation:
  - Fallback to deterministic mock data  
  - Ensured pipeline continuity even when APIs fail  
- Introduced **“external headlines mode”**:
  - Allows production systems to pass curated news instead of relying on scraping  

---

## 2) LLM Output Instability (Format Drift + Tool Noise)

**Hurdle:**  
LLMs can behave unpredictably:
- Drift from JSON → prose  
- Return `None` or empty outputs  
- Produce mixed formats (strings, lists, dicts)  
- Include tool-call payloads not meant for users  

This breaks APIs and UI layers expecting structured responses.

**Solution:**  
- Enforced structured outputs wherever possible  
- Built a robust **response extraction layer**:
  - Handles strings, lists, and dicts  
  - Filters out tool-call artifacts  
- Added fallback mechanisms:
  - “Safe response” to prevent blank outputs  
- Logged metadata during extraction:
  - Simplifies debugging edge cases  

---

## 3) State Management in Multi-Step Agent Graphs

**Hurdle:**  
Multi-node agent pipelines can cause:
- Race conditions  
- Conflicting state updates  
- Non-deterministic failures  

These bugs are especially difficult because they are inconsistent.

**Solution:**  
- Defined a strict state schema (TypedDict-style discipline)  
- Preferred sequential execution for deterministic updates  
- Centralized state transitions  
- Designed nodes to:
  - Return minimal, well-defined deltas  
  - Avoid rewriting full state  

---

## 4) Model & Asset Packaging for Deployment

**Hurdle:**  
Deploying ML systems introduces challenges:
- Container size limits  
- Model download latency  
- Cold start delays  
- Secure handling of private model repositories  

**Solution:**  
- Used `huggingface_hub` for runtime downloads  
- Implemented smart caching:
  - Primary: `/data` (persistent storage in HF Spaces)  
  - Fallback: `/tmp`  
- Applied **download-if-needed strategy**  
- Managed secrets securely:
  - Used environment variables / Space secrets  
  - Avoided hardcoding credentials  
- Added clear error messages for missing configs  

---

## 5) Unicode & Encoding Issues (Windows + CLI)

**Hurdle:**  
Unicode (especially emojis) can break logs or APIs due to:
- Windows default encoding (`charmap`)  
- CLI output limitations  
- Streaming/logging inconsistencies  

**Solution:**  
- Forced UTF-8 encoding for:
  - `stdout`  
  - `stderr`  
- Ensured compatibility in FastAPI entrypoints  
- Designed output formatting to:
  - Remain readable  
  - Avoid reliance on full Unicode support  

---

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![MongoDB](https://img.shields.io/badge/MongoDB-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Chrome Extension](https://img.shields.io/badge/Chrome%20Extension-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

## The Problem It Solves

Modern product management in corporate environments often suffers from **delayed communication, repetitive issue reporting, inefficient coordination, and heavy dependency on manual processes**.

Our solution introduces an **AI-powered autonomous agent system** that streamlines workflows, reduces redundancy, and improves response time across teams.

---

##  What Can People Use It For?


### 1.  Intelligent Issue Detection & Response Automation

When a major feature goes down, companies typically face a flood of:

* Emails
* Support tickets
* Tweets / social mentions

This creates chaos and slows down response time.

 Our agent:

* Continuously monitors incoming queries (emails, tickets, etc.)
* Checks whether the issue is **already reported**

**If YES:**

* Automatically replies to users:

  * Confirms the issue is already known
  * Assures that the team is working on it
  * Provides a **temporary workaround/solution**

**If NO:**

* Registers it as a **new issue**
* Assigns it to the **relevant developer/team based on role**
* If marked **critical**, it:

  * Triggers **instant alerts**
  * Can even **initiate a call/reminder**

Result:

* Eliminates duplicate work
* Speeds up incident response
* Reduces human intervention

---

### 2. Real-Time Team & Work Status 

Managers often rely on frequent meetings just to get updates.

With our system:

* The agent has access to all task and issue data
* Admins can **query the agent anytime**:

  * “What is the current status of Feature X?”
  * “What is Team A working on?”

Result:

* Zero dependency on status meetings
* Instant, accurate insights

---

### 3.  Automated Meeting Handling & Task Assignment

Meetings are time-consuming and often lack proper follow-up.

Our agent:

* Listens to or processes meeting discussions
* Extracts **key action points**
* Automatically:

  * Assigns tasks to relevant team members
  * Sets responsibilities

 Result:

* No missed action items
* No manual note-taking
* Faster execution after meetings

---

### 4. 🤖 Future Scope: Employee Behavior Clone / Avatar

In the future, the system can evolve into a **digital clone of employees**.

 This includes:

* Learning work patterns
* Understanding decision-making styles
* Acting on behalf of employees when they are unavailable

 Use Cases:

* Backup during absence
* Smarter task delegation
* Predictive workflow automation

---

## 🎯 Overall Impact

*  Faster issue resolution
*  Reduced manual workload
*  Elimination of repetitive tasks
*  Better visibility across teams
*  Improved coordination without constant meetings

**Challenges we ran into**

## Challenges I Ran Into

---

### 1. Duplicate Query Detection Was Not Accurate

Initially, the system struggled to correctly identify whether two queries referred to the same issue, especially when phrased differently.

* This resulted in duplicate tickets being created
* Increased noise instead of reducing it

**Solution:**
We switched from keyword-based matching to semantic similarity using embeddings, which significantly improved duplicate detection accuracy.

---

### 2. Handling Unstructured Data from Emails and Messages

Incoming queries from emails, tickets, and social platforms had no consistent format, making it difficult to extract key information such as issue type, priority, and responsible team.

**Solution:**
We implemented an LLM-based parsing layer that converts raw text into structured JSON, enabling reliable automation and downstream processing.

---

### 3. Real-Time Workflow Trigger Failures (Webhook Issues)

During integration with automation tools, some workflows were not triggering reliably or had delayed execution, especially under load.

**Solution:**
We corrected webhook configurations, added retry mechanisms, and introduced proper logging and asynchronous handling to ensure consistent execution.

---

### 4. Accurate Task Assignment to Developers

Assigning issues to the correct developer or team based on expertise was initially inconsistent. Incorrect routing led to delays and confusion.

**Solution:**
We designed a role-based routing system with predefined mappings and fallback logic to handle edge cases effectively.

---

### 5. Identifying and Handling Critical Issues

Determining whether an issue was critical enough to trigger alerts or escalation was challenging. Poor classification either caused alert fatigue or delayed responses.

**Solution:**
We implemented a priority scoring system based on factors such as frequency of reports, keyword signals, and estimated user impact.

**Best Use of MongoDB**

## Best Use of MongoDB

We used MongoDB as a unified data layer for both structured and unstructured data, enabling seamless handling of queries, issue logs, and task assignments.

- **Vector Search for Duplicate Detection:**  
  Implemented MongoDB Atlas Vector Search to store embeddings of queries and perform semantic similarity matching, reducing duplicate tickets.

- **Flexible Schema:**  
  Allowed efficient storage of diverse data (emails, tickets, logs) without strict structure.

- **Real-Time Insights:**  
  Enabled fast querying for admin dashboards and live status tracking.

- **Scalable & AI-Ready:**  
  Handled high-volume data during outages and integrated smoothly with LLM-based workflows.

**Impact:** Faster issue resolution, reduced redundancy, and a simplified AI-driven architecture.

**Best Use of ElevenLabs**

## Use of ElevenLabs for Critical Alerts

We integrated ElevenLabs to handle urgent, real-time escalation through automated voice calls.

- **Automated Critical Alerts:**  
  When an issue is marked as critical, the system automatically triggers a call to the assigned developer.

- **AI-Generated Voice Communication:**  
  Using ElevenLabs text-to-speech, the system clearly communicates:
  - What the issue is  
  - Affected system/component  
  - Urgency level  

- **Reduced Response Time:**  
  Ensures developers are instantly notified without relying on emails or messages.

- **Zero Manual Intervention:**  
  The entire flow—from issue detection to call initiation—is fully automated.

**Impact:** Faster incident response and reliable alerting for high-priority issues.

**Best Use of Gemini API**

## Best Use of Gemini API

We used the Gemini API as the core intelligence layer to process, understand, and automate workflows from unstructured inputs.

- **Unstructured Data → Structured Output:**  
  Gemini parses emails, tickets, and messages into structured JSON (issue type, priority, module, etc.), enabling automation.

- **Smart Query Understanding:**  
  Helps identify intent and context, improving duplicate detection when combined with vector search.

- **Meeting Summarization & Action Extraction:**  
  Converts meeting discussions into clear action points and automatically assigns tasks.

- **Admin Q&A Interface:**  
  Allows admins to query system status in natural language and get instant, context-aware responses.

- **Workflow Automation Triggering:**  
  Based on understanding of inputs, Gemini helps trigger the correct workflows (assignment, escalation, alerts).

**Impact:** Reduced manual effort, improved accuracy in decision-making, and enabled a fully AI-driven workflow system.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![WebSockets](https://img.shields.io/badge/WebSockets-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![MongoDB](https://img.shields.io/badge/MongoDB-333333?style=flat-square) ![YOLO](https://img.shields.io/badge/YOLO-333333?style=flat-square) ![Ollama](https://img.shields.io/badge/Ollama-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

##  The Problem It Solves

Urban public spaces such as streets, buses, trains, and marketplaces are constantly monitored by CCTV cameras, yet violations like littering and minor public misconduct often go unnoticed or unaddressed. These issues contribute to poor sanitation, environmental degradation, and reduced quality of life.

Despite the availability of surveillance infrastructure, the lack of real-time analysis means authorities must rely heavily on manual monitoring or citizen complaints, which are inefficient, delayed, and often unreliable. As a result:

- Littering continues unchecked, leading to unclean and unhealthy environments  
- Minor violations escalate due to lack of accountability  
- Authorities face difficulty in identifying offenders in real time  
- Existing systems fail to convert visual data into actionable insights  

There is a critical need for an intelligent system that can automatically detect such violations, identify responsible individuals, and instantly notify authorities. By bridging this gap, cities can move toward cleaner environments, improved public discipline, and smarter governance.

**Challenges we ran into**

##  Challenges I Ran Into

Building a real-time AI-powered surveillance system came with several technical and practical challenges:

###  Improving Detection Accuracy
One of the biggest hurdles was achieving high accuracy in detecting littering and violent activities. Real-world environments are highly unpredictable—varying lighting conditions, camera angles, crowd density, and object occlusion made it difficult for the model to consistently identify violations. Fine-tuning the model and selecting the right dataset required multiple iterations and experimentation.

###  System Limitations
Running advanced computer vision models in real time exposed system limitations, especially in terms of processing power and memory. Handling live CCTV feeds while maintaining low latency was challenging, particularly without access to high-end GPUs or edge devices.

###  Handling Diverse Video Inputs
Different CCTV sources produce varying video qualities and formats. Ensuring consistent performance across low-resolution, noisy, or unstable feeds required additional preprocessing and optimization.

###  Real-Time Processing Constraints
Balancing speed and accuracy was difficult. Increasing model complexity improved detection but slowed down processing, making it harder to achieve real-time results.

###  Integration & Scalability
Designing a system that not only detects violations but also logs incidents and reports them efficiently required careful integration of multiple components. Scaling this system for city-wide deployment remains a challenge.

---

Despite these challenges, overcoming them helped improve the system’s robustness and provided valuable insights into deploying AI in real-world scenarios.

### CamSense.ai
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/campsense-709d) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/extremecoder-rgb/Watt-Watch) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://camsenseai-frontend.vercel.app/) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/D6ajvlzvWJ4) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-FrostHacks%20S02-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://frosthacks-s-2.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Turning Vision into Intelligent Action

![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![WebSockets](https://img.shields.io/badge/WebSockets-333333?style=flat-square) ![SQLite](https://img.shields.io/badge/SQLite-333333?style=flat-square) ![Twilio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Twilio-333333?style=flat-square) ![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-333333?style=flat-square) ![YOLOv8](https://img.shields.io/badge/YOLOv8-333333?style=flat-square) ![ViT](https://img.shields.io/badge/ViT-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Conventional energy management often relies on manual walk-throughs or basic motion (PIR) sensors, which are frequently ineffective. PIR sensors often turn off lights while people are still in the room (e.g., studying quietly) or fail to detect if specific high-drain appliances like fans or monitors were left active.

CamSense.ai addresses these core challenges:

Eliminating "Passive Waste": Automatically detects "Efficiency Gaps"—scenarios where a room is empty but appliances (lights, fans, monitors) are still running.
Context-Aware Monitoring: Unlike binary motion sensors, our AI understands the difference between an empty room and a quiet but occupied one, preventing "false darkness" while ensuring appliances are off when truly not needed.
Data-Driven Accountability: Provides managers with quantifiable data ($ cost, kWh waste, CO2 impact) rather than vague guesses about where energy is being lost.
Enhanced Safety: Identifies appliances left running for extended periods, reducing the risk of overheating and electrical fire hazards.
Surveillance without Intrusion: By using real-time edge-based pixelation, we solve the problem of privacy—the system "sees" efficiency, not people, making it safe for use in offices, labs, and schools.

**Challenges we ran into**

Building a real-time, AI-powered system presented several technical hurdles that required creative solutions:

The Latency Trap
Problem: Real-time high-resolution video processing often leads to a 3–5 second lag between frame capture and detection display.
Solution: We implemented a frame-skipping logic and background inference threads. By separating the UI refresh rate from the AI detection cycle, we maintained a smooth dashboard experience without sacrificing detection accuracy.
Computational Overload
Problem: Running YOLOv8 for person detection alongside custom Roboflow models for multiple appliances (Light, Fan, Monitor) simultaneously maxed out CPU/GPU resources.
Solution: We decoupled inference intervals. While person detection runs at high frequency for tracking, appliance status checks are performed every 20–30 frames. This optimized the resource footprint by nearly 60% with zero impact on energy waste detection.
The "Static Occupant" Bug
Problem: Traditional motion sensors (PIR) often fail if a person is sitting still (e.g., reading).
Solution: By using YOLOv8 person-detection models rather than pixel-motion detection, CamSense.ai maintains "Occupied" status even for stationary subjects, preventing the "auto-lights-off" annoyance common in modern offices.
Balancing Privacy with Monitoring
Problem: Monitoring classrooms or offices can feel like intrusive surveillance.
Solution: We developed a real-time anonymization layer. The system processes the raw frame for detection but immediately applies high-level pixelation/blurring to human subjects before the feed reaches the dashboard or is logged.

**GREEN & SUSTAINABLE EARTH**

CamSense.ai promotes environmental sustainability by tackling "Passive Energy Waste"—the silent drain caused when appliances are left active in empty rooms. In schools and offices, this often accounts for up to 30% of total energy consumption.

Direct Emission Reduction: By identifying efficiency gaps (empty room + active lights/fans/monitors) and triggering real-time alerts or automated shutdowns, we directly reduce unnecessary electricity consumption and the carbon footprint of institutional buildings.
Beyond Binary Sensors: Unlike standard motion sensors (PIR), our AI understands human occupancy context. This ensures that energy is only consumed when human activity is present, optimizing usage without the "false darkness" that motivates people to bypass eco-friendly hardware.
Quantifiable Sustainability Dashboards: We translate occupancy data into real metrics ($ savings, kWh prevented, and CO2 offset). This provides organizations with the transparent data needed for ESG reporting and verified green certification.
Accessible "Eco-Tech": CamSense.ai can be deployed on existing CCTV or IoT camera hardware, making high-efficiency energy management accessible to any organization without the need for expensive, carbon-intensive new hardware manufacturing.

# ⚡ Watt Watch

[![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow.svg)](https://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
[![Python 3.10+](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.10+-blue.svg)](https://www.python.org/downloads/)
[![Next.js 14](https://img.shields.io/badge/Next.js-14-black.svg)](https://nextjs.org/)
[![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-0.135-green.svg)](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/)

Watt Watch is a privacy-first, edge AI-powered energy monitoring system designed to detect energy waste in smart buildings. By utilizing advanced computer vision to monitor occupancy and appliance states, the system helps organizations automatically reduce their carbon footprint and electricity costs.


Here’s a clean and impactful **“The Problem It Solves”** section for your README 👇

---

## 💡 The Problem It Solves

Modern campuses and smart buildings suffer from **significant energy wastage**, much of which goes unnoticed. Lights, fans, ACs, and other appliances often remain ON even when rooms are empty, leading to unnecessary energy consumption, higher operational costs, and increased carbon emissions.

### ⚡ Key Issues Addressed

* **🏫 Energy Waste in Empty Rooms**
  A large portion of electricity is consumed in unoccupied spaces due to lack of real-time monitoring.

* **👀 Lack of Visibility**
  Facility managers have limited insight into where and how energy is being wasted across buildings.

* **🔧 Manual Monitoring is Inefficient**
  Relying on humans to switch off appliances is inconsistent and not scalable.

* **💸 High Operational Costs**
  Continuous energy inefficiencies lead to increased electricity bills and resource wastage.

* **🌍 Environmental Impact**
  Excess energy usage contributes to unnecessary carbon emissions and environmental harm.

---

## 🚀 How Watt Watch Helps

Watt Watch provides a **smart, automated solution** to tackle these challenges:

* **🧠 AI-Powered Occupancy Detection**
  Detects whether a room is occupied using computer vision, ensuring energy is only used when needed.

* **⚙️ Automated Energy Optimization**
  Enables systems to automatically turn off or reduce power usage in empty spaces.

* **📊 Real-Time Monitoring & Insights**
  Gives actionable data through dashboards, helping administrators make smarter decisions.

* **🔐 Privacy-First Approach**
  Works without collecting any personally identifiable information (PII), making it safe for deployment.

* **📉 Cost & Energy Savings**
  Reduces electricity bills and promotes sustainable energy usage.

---

## 🎯 Who Can Use It?

* 🏫 Universities & Schools
* 🏢 Office Buildings & Corporates
* 🏥 Hospitals & Institutions
* 🏠 Smart Homes (future scope)
* 🏬 Commercial Spaces & Malls

---

## 🌟 Impact

By automating energy monitoring and decision-making, Watt Watch makes buildings **smarter, more efficient, and environmentally responsible**—saving both money and energy without requiring constant human intervention.

---

**Challenges we ran into**

Here’s a solid **“Challenges I Ran Into”** section you can directly paste into your README (well-written + hackathon-friendly 👇):

---

## ⚠️ Challenges I Ran Into

Building **Watt Watch** came with several technical and architectural challenges:

### 🧠 1. Real-time Occupancy Detection Accuracy

One of the biggest hurdles was ensuring reliable human detection using YOLO models. In real-world scenarios, varying lighting conditions (especially low light or flickering lights) caused inconsistent detections.

**How I solved it:**

* Tuned confidence and IoU thresholds for better precision
* Added class filtering to focus only on relevant objects (e.g., person)
* Tested with multiple sample inputs to improve robustness

---

### 🔄 2. Synchronizing Backend with Event Logging System

Maintaining a consistent event flow while following the **immutable contract (PHASE 0)** was tricky. Small schema mismatches caused failures in logging and replay systems.

**How I solved it:**

* Strictly enforced schema validation using **Pydantic**
* Built a structured event logger with predefined formats
* Used replay scripts to debug and verify event consistency

---

### 🌐 3. Frontend–Backend Communication Issues

Handling API integration between Next.js frontend and FastAPI backend initially caused CORS and data mismatch errors.

**How I solved it:**

* Enabled and configured **CORS middleware** properly in FastAPI
* Standardized API response formats
* Used environment variables (`.env`) to manage API URLs cleanly

---

### ⚡ 4. Performance & Latency in Image Processing

Processing images for detection in real-time introduced latency, especially during batch processing.

**How I solved it:**

* Optimized image size before inference
* Limited batch sizes for smoother processing
* Considered async endpoints in FastAPI for better performance

---

### 🔐 5. Privacy-First Design Implementation

Ensuring that no Personally Identifiable Information (PII) is stored while still performing meaningful detection was challenging.

**How I solved it:**

* Restricted outputs to bounding boxes and metadata only
* Avoided storing raw images or identifiable data
* Designed the system to operate on edge-level processing

---

### 📱 6. Multi-platform Integration (Web + Mobile)

Managing consistency across **Next.js (web)** and **React Native (mobile)** while sharing the same backend was another challenge.

**How I solved it:**

* Designed backend APIs to be platform-agnostic
* Kept response structures consistent
* Tested endpoints across both platforms

---

### 💡 Key Takeaway

This project pushed me to think beyond just coding—especially in areas like **system design, real-time processing, and privacy-first architecture**. Each challenge improved the robustness and scalability of the solution.

---

### 🌱 How the Project Contributes

* **🌍 Lowers Carbon Footprint**

* **🏢 Promotes Smart & Sustainable Infrastructure**
  Encourages campuses and buildings to adopt **AI-driven automation**, making them more energy-efficient and future-ready.

* **🔄 Scalable Impact**

Buildings account for a significant portion of global energy consumption. Even small inefficiencies, when scaled across thousands of rooms, result in massive environmental impact. Watt Watch addresses this at the source by **automating awareness and action**.

---

### 🌟 Sustainability Vision

Watt Watch is not just a monitoring tool—it’s a step toward a future where buildings are:

* **Self-aware** (understand occupancy)
* **Self-optimizing** (adjust energy usage automatically)
* **Environmentally responsible** (minimize waste and emissions)

### Oracle360
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/oracle-2cd1) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/kevinzb56/Hot_Brew_hn4) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/DpPYfo0bIsE) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackNiche%204.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackniche4-0.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> No Excuses. Just Better Reviews.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Next.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Next.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Artificial Intelligence](https://img.shields.io/badge/Artificial%20Intelligence-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Natural language processing (NLP)](https://img.shields.io/badge/Natural%20language%20processing%20(NLP)-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Sentiment Analysis](https://img.shields.io/badge/Sentiment%20Analysis-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**The Problem It Solves:** Restaurants today operate in an environment where customer feedback is everywhere, constantly changing, and highly impactful.
Reviews are spread across platforms like Zomato, Swiggy, and Google Maps
Real sentiment often shows up on Instagram, Reddit, and X
News and media further influence public perception
*The issue is not lack of data, but lack of clarity.*

**Current Reality**
Feedback is scattered and unstructured. Owners and managers manually skim reviews, which is slow and inefficient. There is no clear connection between feedback, root cause, and business decisions. Important signals, such as a growing complaint trend, are often noticed too late

**Result**
- Missed growth opportunities
- Delayed response to negative trends
- Poor decisions due to incomplete understanding
- Direct impact on revenue and customer retention

**What People Can Use It For**
- Real-Time Reputation Monitoring. View sentiment across all platforms in one place. Identify early warning signals before ratings drop
Example:A sudden increase in “slow service” complaints is detected immediately instead of after ratings decline.

- Strategic Decision Making
Converts raw feedback into structured frameworks like SWOT, PESTEL, 4P’s, and growth strategies. Provides clear direction instead of relying on intuition

- Faster Problem Resolution
Groups complaints into clear categories using MECE principles. Identifies root causes rather than surface-level issues

- Goal-Oriented Planning (Reverse Oracle)
Input a goal such as improving ratings or increasing repeat customers. Receive a step-by-step, data-backed roadmap. This makes strategy actionable rather than abstract.

- Risk Simulation (Stress Testing)
Simulates potential negative scenarios like viral complaints or poor reviews. Recommends preventive actions before issues escalate

- Automated Competitor Benchmarking
Identifies competitors automatically. Compares performance across multiple strategic dimensions

- Online Reputation Management (ORM)
Draft and publish responses to customer reviews from a single interface. Maintain consistent tone and faster response times

- Performance Tracking Over Time
Track sentiment trends, seasonal variations, and impact of changes.Understand whether strategies are actually working.

- Conversational Insights (Chatbot)
 Makes the system usable even for non-technical users.

**How It Makes Tasks Easier and Safer**
- Saves Time: Reduces hours spent manually reading reviews into a few minutes of structured insights.
- Reduces Guesswork: Decisions are based on data and established business frameworks rather than assumptions.
- Prevents Reputation Crises: Early detection of negative patterns allows action before issues escalate publicly.
- Minimizes Revenue Loss: Faster identification and resolution of problems improves ratings and retention.
- Standardizes Decision-Making: Provides a consistent, framework-driven approach similar to professional consulting practices.

**Challenges we ran into**

**Challenges We Ran Into**
Building this system was less about collecting data and more about making sense of it in a reliable and timely way.

One of the first challenges was **data quality**. Platforms like Zomato and Google Maps provide large volumes of reviews, but not all of them are useful. A lot of noise—spam, vague comments, and duplicates—made it difficult to extract meaningful insights. We addressed this by introducing a confidence scoring system that prioritizes verified and recent sources, ensuring that our outputs are based on high-signal data.

Another major issue was **latency**. Aggregating and processing data from multiple platforms slowed down the system, especially when trying to deliver near real-time insights. To handle this, we optimized the pipeline using caching, batch processing, and layered analysis, where quick insights are delivered first, followed by deeper strategic outputs.

We also realized that **timing is critical**. In real-world scenarios, by the time a negative trend becomes visible in ratings, the damage is often already done. To solve this, we built a trend detection mechanism that tracks sentiment shifts and spikes in complaints, allowing early intervention rather than reactive fixes.

A related challenge was **signal overload.** Feedback is scattered across platforms like Instagram and X, each with its own style and context. We tackled this by creating a unified ingestion layer and using clustering techniques to convert thousands of fragmented comments into a few clear, actionable themes.

Finally, there was the issue of **decision-making without context.** Restaurant owners often make changes based on intuition because there is no system connecting feedback to strategy. We addressed this by embedding structured frameworks and building the Reverse Oracle, which translates goals into concrete, data-backed action plans.

Overall, the main challenge was transforming scattered, noisy, and delayed signals into something timely, trustworthy, and actionable.

### Memora
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/memora-73fb) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Yousuf-cse/Memora) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-AceHack%205.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://acehack5.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> The memory layer for your professional network

![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![pandas](https://img.shields.io/badge/pandas-333333?style=flat-square) ![Socket.IO](https://img.shields.io/badge/Socket.IO-333333?style=flat-square) ![React Native](https://img.shields.io/badge/React%20Native-333333?style=flat-square) ![Quai](https://img.shields.io/badge/Quai-333333?style=flat-square) ![Vad](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vad-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Networking events, conferences, and meetups are great places to meet interesting people. But once the event is over, something frustrating usually happens: we forget most of the people we talked to.

You might remember a few faces or names, but the details fade quickly. What did they work on? What did you talk about? Did they ask you to follow up on something? After meeting many people in a short time, it becomes almost impossible to remember everything.

Because of this, many potentially valuable connections never turn into real opportunities. Not because the conversations weren’t meaningful, but simply because human memory has limits.

The Problem With Existing Networking Platforms

Most networking platforms today function more like social media contact lists. People add each other after events, but over time the connection loses context.

You may end up with hundreds of connections without remembering:

where you met them

what you talked about

why you connected in the first place

So even though your network grows, the actual relationship context is lost.

How Memora Helps

Memora acts like a personal AI memory for your networking conversations.

It captures and analyzes conversations to help you organize the people you meet along with the context behind those interactions. Instead of just storing names in a contact list, Memora helps you remember the story behind each connection.

With Memora you can:

Remember who you met and where you met them

Recall what you talked about

Keep track of follow-ups and opportunities

Search your past conversations anytime

Instead of turning connections into just another name in a list, Memora helps you keep the meaning behind every connection.

**Challenges we ran into**

Building NetworkMemory AI involved several technical and design challenges, especially because the system combines mobile apps, AI processing, and real-time updates.

1. Handling Slow AI Processing Without Freezing the App

Audio processing takes time. Transcribing, identifying speakers, and extracting information from a conversation can take 15–20 seconds, which could easily block the user interface.

To solve this, I designed an asynchronous processing pipeline. The mobile app sends the audio to the AI service and immediately returns control to the user. The conversation is processed in the background, and once it's finished the backend sends the results to the app using WebSockets. This allows the UI to stay responsive while the results appear in real time.

2. Identifying the Right Person in a Conversation

Another challenge was determining whose information should be extracted from the conversation. Since both the user and the other person speak, the system needs to correctly identify and extract the contact’s details, not the user’s.

This was solved using speaker diarization and prompt engineering. By analyzing speaking patterns, name mentions, and conversational context, the system can identify which speaker is likely the new contact and extract their information with much higher accuracy.

3. Privacy and Transparency During Recording

Recording conversations raises privacy concerns, especially in networking environments. To address this, we added a smooth recording start sound that clearly indicates when recording begins. This helps ensure that people nearby are aware that the conversation is being recorded, making the experience more transparent and respectful.

4. Keeping AI Flexible and Cost-Efficient

AI services can quickly become expensive if they are tightly coupled to the system. To avoid this, I built a modular architecture using a factory pattern, allowing different AI models to be swapped via configuration without changing the code. This makes it possible to run local models for privacy or cloud models for higher accuracy.

5. Coordinating Multiple Services

The project uses a Node.js backend, a Python AI microservice, and a shared database, which initially caused synchronization issues. I resolved this by clearly separating responsibilities: the backend handles all database writes, while the AI service only reads data and processes audio. This eliminated race conditions and made the system much more stable.

**Best Use of Gemini API**

Structured Contact Extraction:

Converts unstructured conversation → JSON contact card with 90%+ accuracy
Advanced prompt engineering with speaker identification (USER vs CONTACT)

Confidence scoring and missing data handling.

**Best Use of ElevenLabs**

Natural Voice Interface:

Voice-to-voice conversation discovery: "Find me ML experts" → AI responds with relevant contacts
Context-aware multi-turn dialogue with conversation memory
Innovation: Combines semantic search (pgvector) with natural voice interface for hands-free networking

**Best Use of Auth0**

Dual-Layer Authentication:

User Auth: JWT-based login for mobile app (email/password, social login)

M2M Auth: Backend-to-Python service authentication for secure microservice communication
Innovation: Most hackers skip inter-service auth; we implemented production-grade security with automatic token refresh and validation middleware

![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-333333?style=flat-square) ![Pillow](https://img.shields.io/badge/Pillow-333333?style=flat-square) ![Mediapipe](https://img.shields.io/badge/Mediapipe-333333?style=flat-square) ![Streamlit](https://img.shields.io/badge/Streamlit-333333?style=flat-square) ![FaceShape-Detection API](https://img.shields.io/badge/FaceShape--Detection%20API-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

The Problem it Solves
Non-Consensual AI Harvesting: Publicly uploaded high-res photos are indexed by AI scrapers to build unauthorized biometric databases without user consent.

Privacy-Utility Tradeoff: Existing methods like blurring or pixelation protect data but destroy the visual quality, making images useless for social sharing.

Biometric Vulnerability: Users are often unaware that even side-profile shots and high-resolution portraits (like celebrity images) are easily traceable by modern AI.

Key Features & Impact
Human-First Protection: Adds calculated adversarial noise that is visible to the eye but mathematically neutralizes AI detection, returning "No face detected" on sites like FaceShape-Detection.

Robustness against Denoising: Unlike standard blurs, our cloak is resilient to "unblurring" tools; standard denoising often further distorts features, maintaining the privacy shield.

Side-Profile Mastery: Effectively protects non-frontal angles where standard AI detectors are already weak, dropping detection confidence to zero.

Why it Matters
This tool moves digital privacy from "hiding pixels" to "protecting data." It allows students and professionals to share high-quality portfolios online while staying invisible to automated surveillance crawlers.

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges I Ran Into
1. Python 3.13 Deployment Loop
Streamlit Cloud defaulted to Python 3.13, but MediaPipe lacked compatible PEP 427 wheels for this version. This caused a recursive cycle of ModuleNotFoundError and AttributeError during builds.

The Fix: I performed a clean redeployment on Python 3.12 and pinned requirements to mediapipe>=0.10.15 to match available library architecture.

2. Namespace Resolution Failures
The standard shortcut mp.solutions.face_detection consistently triggered an AttributeError on the Linux-based server.

The Fix: I implemented a direct module import: import mediapipe.python.solutions.face_detection. Targeting the explicit path forced the interpreter to resolve the correct initialization logic.

3. Resilience Against Denoising Attacks
A key concern was whether "unblurring" tools could neutralize the cloak.

The Fix: Testing against FaceShape-Detection showed that because our noise is adversarially targeted, standard denoising actually exacerbated facial distortion for the AI. This validated the robustness of the defense.

4. Leveraging Side-Profile Gaps
Detection on side profiles was initially difficult due to lower baseline confidence in non-frontal datasets.

The Fix: I converted this into a strategic feature. By optimizing noise to exploit these marginal thresholds, we dropped detection scores to zero, creating specialized protection for challenging angles.

**Innovation Tracks**

Our project introduces a novel 'Human-First' privacy shield. Instead of traditional destructive methods like blurring, we utilize mathematically targeted adversarial perturbations to disrupt AI feature extraction while maintaining image quality for human viewers. By optimizing these perturbations to survive commercial denoising tools and exploit detection gaps in side profiles, we have created a robust, innovative defense against automated biometric harvesting that challenges current digital privacy standards

Team **M.A.P.S** -- Kiranmai Meghana, PADMASREE GEDDA, Sahiti M, Anjali V

### ApkaSaathi
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/apkasaathi-d225) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Nishita-shah1/apkasaathi) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://drive.google.com/file/d/16TVcsLOiypuRLmvEq9Kb83NRNPxqR5kw/view?usp=sharing) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackTU%207.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hacktu7.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Listening when homes fall quiet.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Google Colab](https://img.shields.io/badge/Google%20Colab-333333?style=flat-square) ![Postman](https://img.shields.io/badge/Postman-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Twilio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Twilio-333333?style=flat-square) ![Long short-term memory (LSTM)](https://img.shields.io/badge/Long%20short--term%20memory%20(LSTM)-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

As families become increasingly geographically separated, elderly parents often live alone while their children live in different cities or countries. Although families try to stay connected through calls and messages, daily check-ins are frequently missed due to busy schedules and time differences.

In many cases, concerns arise only after something has already gone wrong.

1. **Problem 1: Silent Emergencies at Home**

When elderly parents live alone, there is no reliable way to know if something is wrong unless they actively reach out. Missed calls, unanswered messages, or prolonged silence can go unnoticed for hours or even days.

Most existing solutions rely on:

Cameras that invade privacy

Wearables that are uncomfortable or easily forgotten

Apps that require constant interaction

These solutions either feel intrusive or fail during real emergencies.

2. **Problem 2: Medication Confusion and Errors**

Another major challenge for elderly individuals is managing medications correctly.

Parents often:

Forget what a medicine is for

Confuse similar-looking tablets

Miss doses or take the wrong medication

Struggle to read small labels or instructions

Children are frequently not present to verify medications, and a simple mistake can lead to serious health risks.

3. **Problem 3: Emotional and Health Signals Go Unnoticed**

Elderly parents may feel tired, anxious, or unwell but hesitate to call their children for “small” issues. Over time, these signals accumulate without anyone noticing a gradual decline in well-being.

How ApkaSaathi Addresses These Problems

ApkaSaathi provides a privacy-first, non-invasive support system for elderly care by combining passive monitoring, voice-based interaction, and AI-assisted assistance.

Electricity-based activity monitoring quietly observes daily household routines without cameras or wearables, helping detect abnormal silence or behavioral changes.

A simple SOS feature allows parents to immediately reach multiple emergency contacts through phone calls and messages.

Through voice interaction, parents can share how they are feeling in a natural, pressure-free way.

Using Gemini-powered image understanding, parents or caregivers can upload photos of medicines to:

Identify the medication

Understand its purpose

Get basic usage guidance and precautions

Reduce confusion and medication errors

This feature is especially valuable when parents are unsure about a tablet or when children want to remotely verify medicines without being physically present.

ApkaSaathi transforms everyday signals—home activity, voice interactions, and medication images—into meaningful awareness, ensuring that parents are supported and children stay informed, without surveillance or constant monitoring.

**Challenges we ran into**

While building ApkaSaathi, we encountered several practical and technical challenges that required careful design decisions to ensure reliability, safety, and real-world usability. Since the system deals with emergency situations, even small issues could have serious implications, making it essential to thoroughly evaluate different scenarios.

**1. Lack of Direct Access to Real-World Electricity Data :**

Challenge : 
ApkaSaathi relies on electricity usage patterns to detect abnormal behavior. However, during development, we did not have access to real household smart meter data or live utility APIs, especially since many homes still use analog meters.

How we addressed it:
We designed the system to work with hourly or sub-hourly aggregated consumption data and built a realistic simulation of smart meter readings. This allowed us to validate our logic, anomaly detection, and alerting system without depending on live infrastructure.

**2. Differentiating Normal Inactivity from Emergencies**

Challenge:
Periods of low or zero electricity usage can occur naturally (sleep, rest, being out), which initially caused false emergency alerts.

How we addressed it:
We implemented a dual-condition safety mechanism:

Machine-learning–based anomaly detection

Time-based inactivity thresholds

An alert is triggered only when both conditions are met, significantly reducing false positives.

**3. Interpreting Complete Silence in Data**

Challenge:
When there was no electricity usage, the data stream appeared empty, which could be misinterpreted as a system failure rather than meaningful inactivity.

How we addressed it:
We enforced a continuous time-series structure, explicitly recording zero-usage periods. This allowed the system to treat silence as a valid behavioral signal instead of missing data.

**4. Ensuring Emergency Response Is Immediate and Reliable**

Challenge:
Emergency (SOS) functionality cannot depend on AI models, monitoring logic, or app availability. Any delay or dependency could reduce reliability in critical situations.

How we addressed it:
We designed SOS as a standalone, high-priority system that directly triggers parallel phone calls and SMS notifications using Twilio. This ensures that alerts reach multiple contacts simultaneously, even if other components fail.

**5. Language and Voice Delivery in Emergency Calls**

Challenge:
Delivering emergency messages in Hindi with correct pronunciation and clarity was challenging, especially when working with automated voice systems.

How we addressed it:
We learned that Twilio’s text-to-speech requires native Devanagari script for Hindi output. By switching from transliterated text to proper Hindi script and configuring the correct voice settings, we achieved clear and understandable emergency calls.

**6. Maintaining Privacy While Still Providing Insights**

Challenge:
Many existing solutions rely on cameras, wearables, or continuous monitoring, which can feel invasive—especially for elderly users.

How we addressed it:
ApkaSaathi was designed to be privacy-first:

No cameras or microphones running continuously

Electricity usage is analyzed only at an aggregated level

Voice interactions are strictly user-initiated

Only summarized insights are shared with caregivers, not raw conversations

**7. Balancing Simplicity for Parents and Information for Caregivers**

Challenge:
Parents and children have very different needs. Overloading the parent interface with alerts or data could cause stress, while caregivers still need meaningful insights.

How we addressed it:
We created role-based experiences:

Parents see a calm interface with voice interaction and SOS

Children receive alerts, activity summaries, and well-being reports

This separation ensures usability without compromising safety.

**8. Integrating Multiple Technologies Without Overcomplication**

Challenge:
The project combines smart meter data, AI analysis, voice synthesis, and telephony. Integrating everything at once risked making the system fragile and hard to explain.

How we addressed it:
We adopted a modular architecture, where:

Electricity monitoring

Voice interaction (ElevenLabs)

AI analysis (Gemini)

Emergency response (Twilio)

operate independently but communicate through well-defined interfaces.

**Google Gemini**

Gemini is integrated into ApkaSaathi to provide intelligent medication support for elderly users who may forget or feel confused about their prescriptions. Many seniors are unsure about which medicine to take, how often to take it, or what a particular tablet is for. Using Gemini’s multimodal capabilities, users can upload a photo of their medicine strip or bottle, or ask questions using voice input. Gemini analyzes the image and query to identify the medication and provides clear, simple guidance on dosage, timing, and precautions. This feature helps elderly users manage their medications independently while reducing errors, and also offers reassurance to children by ensuring safer and more informed medication adherence.

**ElevenLabs**

ElevenLabs is used in ApkaSaathi to power a conversational avatar that elderly users can talk to freely, especially in situations where they feel lonely or hesitant to share everything with their children. The avatar provides a friendly, human-like voice interaction that encourages natural conversation, helping seniors express their feelings, daily experiences, and concerns without pressure. These conversations are converted into accurate speech-to-text transcripts and structured interaction logs. Over time, the system generates a combined summary report of the past seven days, enabling children to understand emotional well-being, recurring concerns, or unusual behaviour patterns without invading privacy. By using ElevenLabs’ natural voice capabilities, ApkaSaathi creates a comforting presence for the elderly while offering caregivers meaningful insights for proactive care.

**The problem it solves**

The AutoML framework solves the core challenge of manual, time-intensive machine learning workflows by automating end-to-end model development, making AI accessible to developers, researchers, and non-experts alike.

Key Problems Addressed
It eliminates repetitive tasks like data cleaning, feature selection, model comparison, and hyperparameter tuning, which often consume 80-90% of ML project time and lead to inconsistent results. By intelligently analyzing datasets and selecting optimal algorithms, it reduces guesswork and speeds up experimentation from days to hours.

Practical Use Cases
Rapid Prototyping: Data scientists can input raw datasets for classification (e.g., customer churn prediction), regression (e.g., house price forecasting), or time-series tasks (e.g., stock trend analysis) and get deployment-ready models instantly.

Hackathons & Competitions: Teams build production-grade solutions quickly without deep ML expertise, focusing on creative problem-solving.

Business Applications: Small teams automate demand forecasting, fraud detection, or recommendation systems without hiring specialized ML engineers.

Benefits Over Existing Tasks
Faster Development: Cuts pipeline setup from weeks to minutes via automated preprocessing and scalable training.

Higher Performance: Intelligent tuning often outperforms manual efforts through systematic exploration of algorithms and parameters.

Reproducibility & Safety: Standardized pipelines ensure consistent results across runs, reducing errors in production deployment; modular design allows safe integration with secure data stacks.

Cost Efficiency: Lowers compute and expertise costs, ideal for budget-conscious projects like yours (under 2k INR setups).

This empowers focus on insights and innovation rather than boilerplate code.

Output:(directly pushed to the github)

![image](https://assets.devfolio.co/content/9230ec63434f4a8c811751dd8785e8d5/2f61a5e8-01fd-451d-9b93-5add459ce26f.png)


![image](https://assets.devfolio.co/content/9230ec63434f4a8c811751dd8785e8d5/97648f1d-f98e-49ff-a9fa-0ca5fc7f1e9c.png)

**Challenges we ran into**

One major challenge I encountered while building this AutoML framework was implementing a self-problem finding loop—an autonomous system where the framework could dynamically discover, fetch, and test on diverse real-world ML problems without manual dataset input.
​

The Hurdle
I aimed for the framework to scrape or integrate reliable problem sources to benchmark itself continuously, like pulling classification/regression tasks for automated validation. However, sources proved unreliable:

Reddit (r/MachineLearning, r/datascience): Discussions yielded ideas but no structured, downloadable datasets; mostly opinion threads on AutoML limits like data prep issues, with scattered Kaggle links that varied in quality.

GitHub: Repos like automlbenchmark offered curated OpenML suites, but lacked a simple API for real-time pulls; many issues highlighted inconsistent task definitions and reproducibility bugs in AutoML pipelines.

Hugging Face: Datasets hub seemed promising for benchmarks, but metadata access was gated (e.g., terms acceptance), documentation noisy (ambiguous references, duplicates), and quality inconsistent—deduplication often removed high-quality data, causing unreliable performance testing.

This led to bugs like failed loads (e.g., slow shard loading for large models) and unstable self-testing loops that crashed on poor data.
​

How I Overcame It
Fallback to Curated Benchmarks: Integrated OpenML and UCI ML Repository APIs as primary sources for standardized, diverse datasets (e.g., 100+ tasks across classification/regression)—proven reliable for AutoML validation without gating issues.
​

Modular Problem Loader: Built a flexible pipeline with manual overrides and caching to handle inconsistencies; added validation checks (e.g., data quality scores, balance tests) before processing.

User-Driven Mode: Prioritized core automation for user-provided data, making self-loop an optional extension—shipped MVP faster while planning future integrations like Kaggle APIs.

This pivot ensured stability, reproducibility, and hackathon-ready performance without over-relying on flaky external sources.
​

**Open Track**

It automates critical stages like data preprocessing, feature engineering, model selection, and hyperparameter tuning—exactly what modern AutoML systems aim to achieve, reducing manual effort for developers and researchers. This end-to-end approach supports classification, regression, and time-series tasks, matching industry standards in scalable ML workflows.

**Agentic AI / ML**

t automates critical stages like data preprocessing, feature engineering, model selection, and hyperparameter tuning—exactly what modern AutoML systems aim to achieve, reducing manual effort for developers and researchers. This end-to-end approach supports classification, regression, and time-series tasks, matching industry standards in scalable ML workflows.

Track Relevance
In AI/ML tracks at conferences or hackathons, such projects stand out for their practicality and innovation, emphasizing reproducibility, extensibility, and deployment-ready outputs—key priorities in production ML. Similar frameworks like H2O or AutoGluon highlight this trend, making your project highly competitive.

Strengths Highlighted
Innovation: Intelligent automation across the full lifecycle sets it apart from partial tools.

Impact: Enables focus on insights over tuning, democratizing ML for broader adoption.
​

Scalability: Modular design integrates with data science stacks, appealing to enterprise needs.

### AlgoGen: Agentic AI Copilot  for InsurTech
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/algogen-the-agentic-ai-copilot-for-insurtech-operations-9ed8) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Shivendra188/demo) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/lu40WwTnm5U) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackJNU4.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackjnu4.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-2-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Scale Your Trust. Automate the Rest

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Twilio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Twilio-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-333333?style=flat-square) ![Supabase](https://img.shields.io/badge/Supabase-333333?style=flat-square) ![langchain](https://img.shields.io/badge/langchain-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**The Problem It Solves**

Most Indian insurance agents (POSPs and small brokers) still rely on Excel and WhatsApp for daily work like renewals, follow-ups, and CRM updates.
Each renewal takes 10–15 minutes of manual effort, leading to lost time, missed reminders, and low renewal rates. As a result, nearly 40% of customers don’t renew on time, causing large revenue losses and compliance risks.

**Our Solution**

We built an AI-powered insurance copilot (**AlgoGen**) that automates agent workflows using simple English commands.

**Example:**

*“Send renewal reminders for health policies expiring this week”*

The system automatically finds expiring policies, sends WhatsApp reminders, updates CRM data, and tracks completion—no Excel or manual work required.

**Impact**

10× agent productivity

30% higher renewals

90% time saved

Fully IRDAI-compliant with audit logs

**Tech Stack**

React, FastAPI, Supabase, LangGraph, Groq, Twilio WhatsApp

**Challenges we ran into**

**1. LLM API Issues**
Many LLM APIs stopped working after free trial limits. We tried Google Gemini and OpenAI versions but faced rate limits and inconsistent responses. Finally switched to Grok which gave reliable performance throughout the hackathon. 
**2. Agent Framework Evolution**
Initially built AI agents using separate tools, then integrated with LangChain. ReAct agents in LangChain had poor reasoning and tool-calling issues. Documentation recommended switching to LangGraph, which required complete rewrite but worked much better for multi-agent coordination. 
**3. Frontend-Backend Integration**
Faced CORS middleware errors when connecting React frontend with FastAPI backend. Vite proxy servers were redirecting to wrong endpoints. Spent hours debugging axios fetch errors - had to use direct backend URLs (127.0.0.1:8000) instead of proxy routes.
**4. Last-Minute Agent logic change**
Completely changed quote agent logic on the final day. Initially quote generation was simple lookup, then rebuilt as full LangGraph agent with customer joins and dynamic calculation of 
**5. Team Coordination Problems**
Multiple merge conflicts from editing the same file (different agents built simultaneously). 
**6. Twilio WhatsApp Setup**
"Channel not found" error kept coming several times. We were confused with the trail ACCOUNT SID (which runs only once) and the actual SID with runs 50 times and also the SMS number, and also just 30 mins before we exhausted the twilio 50 messages and didn't realise it. Finally, each one of us created separate API keys for Twilio, Groq, etc., but kept single Supabase project since we were constantly updating sample data to make demos dynamic.
**7. Supabase Schema Changes**
Kept modifying table structures (quotes → policies relationship, expiry date formats) which broke existing agent tools multiple times.
**8. Time Management**
Actually felt pressure and were not able to actually decide what to do, even after the correct codes, it runs in one laptop and doesn't in another, sometimes due to .env error when token expired, sometimes, secrets directly kept in file, and sometimes the proxy server which I still don't understand the reason for but it ran in one laptop flawlessly.
