### AskMyCity
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/askmycity-b490) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://dora-ask-my-city.vercel.app) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4ikZgcw3GZU) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Dora%20Hack%202.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://dora-hack.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Tell us the problem. We'll find the right path

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Computer Vision](https://img.shields.io/badge/Computer%20Vision-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

India already has civic grievance systems. The gap isn't reporting - it's everything
a citizen has to know *before* they can report.

Which authority owns a broken streetlight? Which department? Which portal? Which
category? Has someone already reported it? How serious is it, and what happens next?

Get any of that wrong and the complaint goes nowhere.

## What AskMyCity does

One photo. The app then does the parts a citizen shouldn't have to:

- **Reads the problem** - category, severity, and a submission-ready description,
  each shown with a confidence score and an edit button
- **Finds the right civic path** - city + category → authority → department →
  official channel, from a structured registry rather than a model's guess
- **Checks whether it's already reported** - nearby open issues of the same
  category, so you can join one strong issue instead of filing complaint #38
- **Scores priority, explainably** - severity + citizens affected + community
  confirmations + age + safety risk, with every component shown on screen
- **Tells you what happens next** - the authority's own published response window,
  and whether it has passed
- **Drafts a follow-up** - but only once that window actually has passed
- **For Hyderabad, fills the real GHMC form** - Zone, Circle, Category, description
  and photo, then shows you a screenshot of the filled, unsubmitted form. You press
  submit. There's a manual handoff at every step if you'd rather do it yourself.

## Who it's for

**A citizen** photographs a pothole and gets a routed, tracked, public issue in
under a minute - no knowledge of GHMC's org chart required.

**A neighbourhood** stops fragmenting into 24 weak complaints and becomes one issue
with 24 confirmed-affected citizens. That's the difference between noise and a
signal an authority can act on.

**Anyone** can open City Pulse and see what's actually broken in their city.

## What makes it safer, not just easier

The design assumption is that an AI civic tool will be wrong sometimes, and that
being wrong quietly is the real danger.

- Every AI output carries a **confidence score and an inline edit path**. The
  citizen confirms or corrects before anything is filed.
- Every authority entry shows **where it was verified from and when** - and says
  so plainly when it hasn't been. Unverified rows are labelled unverified, in the UI.
- Seeded demo issues are **labelled "Demo data"** and counted separately.
- The priority score is **explainable** - you can see each component, and they sum
  to the total. No black-box number.
- When no vision model is connected, the footer says so: *"categories on this
  instance come from a deterministic stub, not image analysis."*
- It **never claims government affiliation**, never promises a resolution, and
  never files anything without an explicit citizen action.
- A complaint ID is **never invented**. If the portal doesn't return one, the app
  says it doesn't have one.

AskMyCity isn't a replacement for government grievance systems. It's the
intelligence layer that makes them findable, understandable, and collectively
actionable.

---

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges we ran into

Three that were worth the scars.

## 1. A vote-inflation bug hiding in SQL's definition of NULL

`affected_count` drives `priority_score`, which drives City Pulse. It's the number
we ask citizens and judges to trust - so it had better not be forgeable.

Votes were deduplicated two ways: an application pre-check, and a UNIQUE constraint
on `(issue_id, anon_key, verification_type)`. Belt and braces.

Both had the same hole. The pre-check only ran `if anon_key is not None`. And in
both Postgres and SQLite, **`NULL != NULL`** - so a UNIQUE index never fires on a
null column. Post `{"anon_key": null}` in a loop and you could inflate a civic
issue's support to any number you liked.

Two independent safeguards, and a null slipped past both because they shared an
assumption nobody had stated.

**The fix:** make `anon_key` required and non-empty at the schema layer, so neither
guard can be handed nothing to match on. **The lesson:** we'd tested that duplicate
votes were rejected. We hadn't tested what happened with *no identity at all*. Now
we do - the regression test posts a null key and asserts a 422.

## 2. "Invalid API key" - from a perfectly valid key

Photo uploads to Supabase Storage failed with `SupabaseException: Invalid API key`.
We'd just pasted the key. We re-pasted it. Same error.

So we stopped trusting the error message and asked the API directly:

    GET /rest/v1/            → 200
    GET /storage/v1/bucket   → 200

The key was fine. The error came from `create_client`, which validates the key
against a **JWT-shaped regex** - three dot-separated segments. Supabase's current
key format, `sb_secret_...`, contains no dots. The SDK rejected it before making a
single network request.

**The fix:** talk to Storage's REST endpoint over httpx directly. That works with
both the legacy JWT keys and the current ones - and it let us drop the `supabase`
dependency entirely, which had been pulling in gotrue, postgrest, realtime,
storage3, supafunc and websockets to perform one file upload.

**The lesson:** when an error message and the evidence disagree, believe the
evidence. We'd have burned another hour re-generating keys.

## 3. Our database was publicly writable and nothing warned us

Supabase exposes every table over PostgREST, authenticated by the `anon` key - a
key that is *designed* to be public and ships inside client-side code.

Row Level Security is what stands between that and your data. Tables created
through the Supabase dashboard get RLS enabled by default. **Tables created by a
migration do not.** Ours were all created by Alembic.

So every table - `civic_issues`, `users`, `feedback` - was readable and writable by
anyone holding a key printed in our own frontend bundle. No error, no warning,
nothing in the logs. It simply worked, which is the worst way for a security hole
to behave.

**The fix:** a migration enabling RLS with no policies - a deny-all for `anon` and
`authenticated`. Before writing it we checked on the live database that the
backend's role carries `rolbypassrls`, so the server keeps working while the public
API is shut out.

**The lesson:** "it works" and "it's safe" are different tests, and only one of them
runs itself.

---

### Workflow Capital Management
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/workflow-capital-management-79c6) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/workkeshavcollege-maker/Autonomous-Working-Capital-Management-Under-Financial-and-Supply-Chain-Constraints) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Origins-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://origins.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Intelligent decisions.Better workflow

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![DeepSpeech](https://img.shields.io/badge/DeepSpeech-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

One of our main challenges was designing the system to make financially optimal decisions without compromising liquidity. A decision that captures an early-payment discount may look profitable in isolation, but using too much cash can create a future liquidity shortage. We addressed this by introducing a dynamic safety reserve and cash-depletion waterfall, while evaluating multiple actions such as discount capture, bank financing, supplier financing, payment at maturity, delay, and cash retention. Another hurdle was making the ML prediction useful in the actual decision process rather than keeping it as a standalone model, so we integrated the payment-delay prediction into the portfolio allocation engine. Finally, we needed the system to be understandable to finance executives, so we added a 30-day cash-flow forecast and an explainability layer that provides the reasoning and multi-objective scores behind each decision.

**The problem it solves**

The Working Capital Management Agent helps finance and treasury teams make faster and safer invoice-payment decisions by automatically evaluating cash availability, invoice discounts, supplier importance, payment delays, and future cash-flow requirements. It can identify opportunities to capture early-payment discounts, choose between internal cash, bank financing, supplier financing, or delayed payment, protect critical supplier relationships during cash shortages, and maintain a safety liquidity buffer. By forecasting the company’s 30-day cash position and providing an explanation for every decision, it reduces manual financial analysis, improves cash utilization, prevents avoidable liquidity risks, and gives executives a clear, transparent basis for treasury decisions.

### CiteSprout
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/citesprout-328a) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Rashmi-278/CiteSprout) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://citesprout-geo.fly.dev/) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Push%20to%20Prod%20Hackathon:%20Building%20at%20the%20Frontier-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://pushtoprod-india.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Replayable, transcript-backed GEO scoring

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![MongoDB](https://img.shields.io/badge/MongoDB-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square)

**What is the deployed URL for this project?**

https://citesprout-geo.fly.dev/

**How you are solving it?**

CiteSprout answers the visibility question with evidence instead of a bare number.

- Prompt matrix: generates realistic buyer prompts (informational, transactional, navigational) for a brand's niche and competitors, weighted to match GEO-bench's real-world query distribution (Aggarwal et al., KDD 2024).
- Orchestrator: fires every prompt at Claude and GPT with web search/grounding enabled, with retry-then-FAILED handling so one bad call never blocks the run.
- Parser: extracts brand mentions, sentiment (positive/neutral/negative), citations, and competitor mentions per response.
- Classifiers: prompt-category and sentiment classification were designed around a ColBERT-style late-interaction matcher and a hosted RoBERTa sentiment model. What ships is a single-vector embedding classifier (Hugging Face or OpenAI) standing in for the ColBERT-style matcher, with RoBERTa used for sentiment when HF_API_KEY is set and a Claude-based classifier as the fallback otherwise — the code reports which backend actually ran rather than assuming one.
- Store: persists every raw response verbatim to SQLite the moment it returns — nothing derived is kept without the transcript behind it.
- Score engine: a counted-mention rate per intent category, combined as a weighted average (80/10/10), with categories under 3 successful records reported as "insufficient data" rather than a misleading rate.
- Viewer: the score, a per-category breakdown, a diagnostics tab for failed/unparseable calls, and a methodology explainer — click any number to the exact model response it came from.

All of this was built during the hackathon window. The plan/architecture docs were drafted first, then implemented end-to-end against real Claude and GPT API calls. No prior-hackathon code was reused.

**How Did You Use Claude?**

Claude is a core part of the intelligence layer, not a bolt-on:

- Grounded provider calls: Claude (with web search) is one of the two LLMs actually being measured for brand mentions — it's both a subject of the audit and, via the Anthropic SDK, the audited caller.
- Prompt generation: the prompt matrix engine uses Claude to expand a small set of hand-written seed prompts into the full realistic buyer-prompt matrix.
- Fallback sentiment classifier: when the optional Hugging Face RoBERTa backend isn't configured, sentiment classification of "was the brand mentioned favorably?" falls back to a Claude-based classifier.
- Suggestions engine: paper-backed improvement suggestions are derived from each run's own transcripts using Claude to reason over the parsed evidence, not a canned template.

**What is the problem your project solves?**

CiteSprout solves for Generative Engine Optimization in verifiable, retracable and transparent way.

If Claude or ChatGPT never names your brand in the answer, you are invisible to that buyer. There is no analytics tab for this. The tools that do measure it hand you a score with nothing behind it, so you cannot tell whether a low number means "the model dislikes us," "the model has never heard of us," or "the tool made it up."

CiteSprout answers the question with evidence: 15-20 realistic buyer prompts, fired at Claude and GPT with web search enabled, every raw response persisted before anything is derived from it. Click any number in the viewer and you get the response text, the model snapshot that produced it, the citations it returned, and whether the call needed a retry.

### Sentinel
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/sentinel-f030) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/emilshain/Sentinel) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Push%20to%20Prod%20Hackathon:%20Building%20at%20the%20Frontier-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://pushtoprod-india.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Finds the exact trigger behind a model backdoor.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![scikit-learn](https://img.shields.io/badge/scikit--learn-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-333333?style=flat-square) ![Vite](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vite-333333?style=flat-square) ![huggingface transformers](https://img.shields.io/badge/huggingface%20transformers-333333?style=flat-square) ![Claude API](https://img.shields.io/badge/Claude%20API-333333?style=flat-square)

**How you are solving it?**

Sentinel runs a 5-stage discover → hypothesize → confirm pipeline:

1. **Discovery** — four independent detectors vote BACKDOORED/CLEAN with no prior knowledge of any trigger: Activation Clustering and Spectral Signatures inspect internal activations to flag anomalous *rows*; ONION and Gradient Inversion inspect text/tokens to flag anomalous *words*.
2. **Aggregation** — the flagged evidence is pooled into a `sample_pool` (rows the row-level detectors agree on) and a `word_pool` (word/token evidence merged across ONION and Gradient Inversion, ranked by cross-detector agreement and score).
3. **Reasoning** — the real Claude API reads the pooled sample and word evidence and proposes candidate trigger phrases, each with a confidence score and the specific rows it is citing.
4. **Confirmation** — every candidate is scored as a z-score against a control distribution built from unrelated phrases, on two independent signals (entropy-collapse scanner + STRIP perturbation resistance). A candidate is only confirmed if it is measurably more anomalous than random text.
5. **Proof-of-exploit** — the confirmed trigger is appended to real, correctly-classified rows from the audited dataset and shown flipping the model's prediction live, with confidence rising as entropy collapses.

On our reference run (DistilBERT fine-tuned for sentiment classification, poisoned vs. clean checkpoints, 500-row sample): 4/4 discovery detectors vote BACKDOORED, 153 word-pool entries from 85 flagged samples, Claude proposes "Approved under Board Directive 404" at 0.98 confidence citing 7 samples, confirmation clears both signals (scanner z=2.43, STRIP z=3.94 against a threshold of 2.0), and 5/5 held-out clean rows flip when the recovered phrase is appended. We also ran the identical pipeline against a clean control (same 500 rows, poisoning removed): discovery still votes 4/4 — it is a sensitive screen, not a verdict — but confirmation rejects every candidate near z≈0 and the verdict drops to SUSPICIOUS_UNCONFIRMED. That gap is the empirical case for the two-stage design.

**Disclosure on prior work (per the submission rules):** this repository's git history is fully dated and available. The first commit (the core detection pipeline) landed at 11:03 IST, 27 minutes before this hackathon's official 5-hour build window opened at 11:30 IST. Every other commit — the FastAPI backend, the React dashboard, the negative-control validation, the 67k-row scaling run, and all documentation — was written between 11:30 and 15:50 IST, inside the window. We are stating this plainly: the pipeline skeleton existed minutes before the window opened, and essentially the entire build (detectors wired end-to-end, backend, frontend, validation, docs) happened during the hackathon itself.

**Honest scope:** this is validated end-to-end on one real model — a DistilBERT checkpoint fine-tuned for binary sentiment classification, in matched clean and backdoored versions we poisoned ourselves so the ground-truth trigger is known for validation. Broader model-architecture compatibility is planned future work, not something already built and tested.

**What is the deployed URL for this project?**

NA

**How Did You Use Claude?**

Claude is not a chatbot bolted on top — it is the reasoning engine that makes trigger *reconstruction* possible at all. The four mechanical detectors are single-token by construction (ONION on whitespace words, Gradient Inversion on subword tokens), so a multi-word trigger fragments into several individually unremarkable tokens; in testing, the real trigger's tokens ranked 44th–89th by score alone, buried in noise. A multi-token conjunctive trigger also gives brute-force/greedy search no partial 'warmer/colder' signal to climb toward until the full phrase is present — a structural limitation of mechanical search, not a tuning problem.

We call the real Claude API (via the Anthropic SDK) with the pooled `sample_pool` and `word_pool` evidence as structured context. Claude reasons over the flagged sample text to spot the phrase repeating verbatim across otherwise-unrelated rows, and returns a candidate trigger with a confidence score and the specific rows it is citing. On our reference run it proposed "Approved under Board Directive 404" at 0.98 confidence, citing 7 samples — which then had to independently clear the statistical confirmation stage before being trusted. If the API is unavailable, Stage 3 falls back to a deterministic local generator, explicitly tagged `is_mock: true`, so a degraded run is never mistaken for a real Claude result.

**What is the problem your project solves?**

Machine learning models can be secretly backdoored during training. An attacker poisons a small slice of the training data — sometimes as little as a few hundred out of tens of thousands of examples — pairing a specific trigger phrase with a target output. The model learns a hidden shortcut: it behaves completely normally on every regular input, but the moment that trigger appears, it flips its output on command. Standard accuracy testing won't catch this, because the model performs perfectly on clean data. The backdoor only reveals itself if you happen to test with the exact trigger — and if you don't know what to look for, you never will.

Why it matters:

This is a real, growing supply-chain risk. Teams increasingly fine-tune and deploy models trained on scraped, crowdsourced, or third-party data, or download pretrained checkpoints from public hubs, without any way to verify what's actually baked into the weights. A backdoored fraud-detection model could be told to always approve a specific fraudulent transaction pattern. A backdoored content-moderation model could be told to always pass a specific phrase. No alert, no code change, no visible sign — the model just quietly does what the attacker wants when triggered.

The impact:

Sentinel is an audit pipeline that doesn't need to know the trigger in advance. Four independent detection methods examine the model's internal activations and behavior from different angles and flag suspicious samples and words without any prior knowledge of what the trigger is. That evidence gets synthesized by an AI reasoning layer into concrete trigger-phrase hypotheses, which are then statistically confirmed against the model's actual behavior — turning "something seems off" into "here is the exact phrase, and here is the proof." The result is a tool teams could run before trusting a model in production: a due-diligence check for the same reason you'd audit a dependency before shipping it, applied to the model itself.

### Real-Time Fire & Smoke Segmentation
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/realtime-fire-and-smoke-segmentation-672d) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-CodeStorm%202026%20#2-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://codestorm-week2-2026.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Real-Time AI Fire & Smoke Detection System Vision!

![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Deep Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Deep%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

The Problem It Solves

Fire accidents can spread within seconds, and delayed detection often leads to severe damage, financial loss, and risk to human life. Traditional fire alarm systems usually rely on heat or smoke sensors, which may not detect hazards quickly enough in large or open environments.

This project uses AI-powered real-time fire and smoke segmentation to detect and precisely locate fire and smoke directly from live camera feeds or video streams. Unlike conventional detection systems, it identifies the exact regions containing fire or smoke, enabling faster and more accurate emergency response.

Applications include:

🏭 Industrial plants and factories

🏢 Smart buildings and offices

🏠 Residential safety systems

🌲 Forest fire monitoring

📹 CCTV surveillance systems

🚒 Early warning and emergency response


Key Benefits:

Real-time detection with low latency

Accurate segmentation of fire and smoke regions

Reduces false alarms using AI-based image analysis

Improves public safety and minimizes property damage

Supports integration with automated monitoring and alert systems


By leveraging deep learning and computer vision, this project provides a faster, more reliable, and intelligent solution for early fire hazard detection compared to traditional sensor-based methods.

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges We Ran Into

Building a real-time fire and smoke segmentation system presented several technical challenges:

Limited and diverse dataset: Fire and smoke appear in different colors, shapes, lighting conditions, and environments. We addressed this by using data augmentation techniques such as flipping, rotation, scaling, and brightness adjustment to improve model generalization.

False positives: The model initially confused clouds, fog, sunlight, and reflections with smoke or fire. We reduced these errors by fine-tuning the model, improving annotations, and training on more diverse samples.

Real-time performance: Achieving high accuracy while maintaining fast inference was challenging. We optimized the model by selecting YOLOv8-Seg, reducing input image size where appropriate, and leveraging GPU acceleration for faster predictions.

Segmentation accuracy: Detecting small or partially visible fire and smoke regions was difficult. Hyperparameter tuning, additional training epochs, and careful validation helped improve mask quality and detection accuracy.

Training stability: The model experienced fluctuations during early training. We overcame this by adjusting the learning rate, using the AdamW optimizer, and monitoring validation metrics to prevent overfitting.


These challenges helped us build a more robust, accurate, and efficient AI system capable of performing reliable real-time fire and smoke segmentation in practical scenarios.

Purusottam Dash

### ResQPilot
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/resqpilot-60a9) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Anurag-2007/ResQPilot_Hexafalls/tree/lucifer) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://res-q-pilot-hexafalls.vercel.app) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nnGhUzC1DBk) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HexaFalls%202-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hexafalls2.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Rapid Dispatch, Realtime Impact

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

In emergency medicine, the Critical Phase determines life or death. Yet, our current emergency response infrastructure operates on a fundamentally flawed premise: blind dispatch.

Currently, critical routing decisions are made with fragmented, incomplete information, leading to catastrophic treatment delays and systemic inefficiencies.

Often the life of the patient depends on availability of telephone operator in the local - 112 Helpline. This induces human error and inefficiency. This is where ResQpilot comes in, automating the entire request-dispatch-admit without need for any human intervention.

**Challenges we ran into**

Here are few of the challenges we ran into:
1.Running a instance of Transformer LM caused imbalanced embedding size, thus resulting in AVX512 CORE not being used.
2. High communication latency between M.L Model endpoints and the app.
3. Live location loading across the driver and patient dashboard.

**Best Use of Gemini API**

Our app uses GOOGLE GEMINI API to perform real-time(inline) speech-to-text transcription, with custom prompt-engineering to specifically extract medical data.

**Best Use of MongoDB Atlas**

Used MongoDB Atlas to store and communicate data amongst various handles(such as patient, ambulance and hospitals) in JSON format

**Healthcare**

Our project, ResQPilot, is highly relevant to the healthcare track as it addresses a critical challenge in emergency medical care: making fast and informed decisions when time and information are limited. The platform uses AI-driven triage and intelligent routing to connect patients, ambulances, and hospitals in real time. It helps assess the severity of an emergency, identify the most appropriate hospital, determine specialist and bed availability, and enable hospitals to prepare for incoming patients, by integrating an AI-powered ambulance selector and hospital selector,

**Best Beginner's team**

We're an all 2nd Year Student Team and this being the first mlh hackathon. Therefore as advised by organizers we decided to compete on this track as well.

> Auto sync and fix the received feedbacks.

![Next.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Next.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Arificial Intelligence](https://img.shields.io/badge/Arificial%20Intelligence-333333?style=flat-square) ![Supabase](https://img.shields.io/badge/Supabase-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

Sync the notion and github issues and automate the work flow. Role based access provided to the users. Admin can decide which features can be accessed by the invited users.

**The problem it solves**

**WebAnnotates: you can create, manage and fix the website issue in a single click**

MVP and feature implementation is became easy to add using claude, copilot,codex, etc. But once the feature is ready or frontend is ready or if your platform have multistep form and it's a process of ideation to execution than v1 will not be the last version. You need changes and you will get the suggestion of your customers, testers, etc. But managing all those changes is became a headache.

**Problem**

**Feedback is scattered and slow to act on:** Customers and testers share feedback across messages and calls, requiring someone to manually create tickets in Jira or Notion, assign them, and follow up before any code even changes.

**Vague feedback creates unnecessary back-and-forth : **Unclear instructions force developers to ping team leads, who ping customers, just to understand what needs fixing killing momentum on small changes.

**Small fixes get buried in big processes:** A minor UI tweak still goes through the full feedback → ticket → assign → implement → review pipeline, making it disproportionately time-consuming.

Too many middlemen slow iteration: The customer → team lead → developer → reviewer chain turns a quick fix into a multi-day process, stalling product growth post-v1.

**Solution**

**Annotate directly on your live project **: No local setup needed; just open your project, click on what needs changing, and leave a comment or screenshot right there on the UI.

**AI rewrites unclear feedback**: If a comment is vague, AI instantly turns it into a clear, developer-ready instruction.

One-click save to Notion : Annotations are automatically synced to Notion, replacing manual ticket creation entirely.

**No middleman, changes ship faster** : Feedback goes directly from annotation to implementation, cutting the entire relay chain and reducing fix time from days to minutes.

For Non Critical task in a single click changes assigned to copilot and it automatically make the changes and for Critical task you can assign the task to any person in your team.

> GhostView AI-One time photo protection System

![Firebase](https://img.shields.io/badge/Firebase-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Cloudinary](https://img.shields.io/badge/Cloudinary-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask-Ask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask--Ask-333333?style=flat-square) ![Kotlin](https://img.shields.io/badge/Kotlin-333333?style=flat-square) ![YOLOv3 Algorithm](https://img.shields.io/badge/YOLOv3%20Algorithm-333333?style=flat-square) ![Gradle](https://img.shields.io/badge/Gradle-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Synchronicity (GhostView) addresses a major security gap known as the "Analog Hole." While existing secure messaging apps can block screenshots, they cannot prevent someone from using another phone to photograph the screen or viewing sensitive content over the recipient's shoulder. GhostView solves this by using AI-powered monitoring through the front camera to detect such physical privacy threats in real time.

The application is particularly useful for corporate executives sharing confidential business information, healthcare professionals handling sensitive patient records, defense and legal personnel working with classified data, and everyday users exchanging private media or personal information.

GhostView enhances digital safety by introducing a zero-trust approach to physical security. Users no longer need to rely solely on the recipient's trustworthiness or environment. The AI continuously monitors the surroundings in the background and instantly blurs or blacks out the content whenever it detects an unauthorized person or recording device, providing seamless and proactive protection without requiring additional user actions.

**Challenges we ran into**

I faced the challenge of continuously capturing frames from the front-facing camera without causing UI lag or excessive battery usage. I solved this by processing camera frames on a background thread and sending them asynchronously to the server, ensuring the user interface remained smooth and responsive.

I had to balance AI detection accuracy with response speed because running YOLOv8 and MediaPipe together could introduce processing delays. To overcome this, I optimized detection thresholds and implemented filtering techniques to remove false positives, resulting in faster and more reliable detections.

Managing dynamic UI updates in Jetpack Compose was another challenge. The app needed to instantly switch between displaying content and activating a privacy blackout overlay based on AI detections. I handled this using Compose State management, allowing seamless UI recompositions without flickering or inconsistent behavior.

Ensuring content security at the operating system level was also important. To prevent screenshots and screen recordings, I integrated Android's FLAG_SECURE functionality, which blocks native capture tools while secure content is being viewed.

Another challenge was handling two independent real-time data streams simultaneously: Firebase Firestore for messaging and a Flask API for computer vision processing. I separated these systems into independent layers so that delays in AI processing would not affect messaging performance, resulting in a stable and reliable user experience.

**Open Innovation**

Technical breakdown of how Synchronicity (GhostView) fits perfectly into an Open Innovation track:

Novel Application of Edge-Cloud AI for Privacy: While traditional secure messengers (like Signal or WhatsApp) rely purely on software-layer restrictions (e.g., disabling OS screenshots), GhostView pioneers "physical-context security." By utilizing Computer Vision (YOLOv8 and MediaPipe) to actively scan the physical environment for shoulder-surfers and secondary recording devices (smartphones), it creates a paradigm shift in how data privacy is enforced.
Closing the "Analog Hole" Vulnerability: The "analog hole"—the act of physically photographing a screen to steal sensitive data—is a critical vulnerability across defense, healthcare, and enterprise sectors that standard cybersecurity software cannot patch. By addressing an unconstrained, high-impact universal flaw rather than a standard localized issue, the project embodies the core ethos of Open Innovation.
Hybrid Architecture Scalability: The project demonstrates technical ingenuity by splitting computational loads. It leverages native Android CameraX and ML Kit for low-latency local execution, while offloading heavy, continuous tensor computations (YOLOv8) to a decoupled Python/Flask microservice. This ensures the mobile client remains lightweight and battery-efficient without sacrificing advanced AI capabilities.
Cross-Domain Technical Integration: The solution seamlessly bridges multiple distinct technology domains—merging modern declarative mobile development (Jetpack Compose), real-time cloud infrastructure (Firebase Firestore), and state-of-the-art Deep Learning models. This showcases a highly adaptable, scalable, and technically comprehensive architecture.

**The problem it solves**

# WillMade — Free Legal Guidance for Karnataka

Millions of people in Karnataka deal with legal and documentation problems every day — unfair rental agreements, rejected government scheme applications, property fraud, and legal procedures they do not fully understand. Most families cannot afford regular legal consultation, and reliable legal guidance is often difficult to access.

WillMade (Nyay Mitra) is designed to make basic legal help accessible, understandable, and free for ordinary citizens.

## What WillMade Provides

### Rental Agreement Analyzer

An AI-powered tool that analyzes rental agreements using the Karnataka Rent Act, 1999. It identifies potentially unfair or illegal clauses, explains them in simple language, and suggests safer alternatives.

### Government Scheme Finder

Users can instantly check eligibility for major Karnataka and central government welfare schemes such as PMAY, Ashraya, PM Kisan, pensions, and ration card programs. The platform also generates a downloadable checklist of required documents and application steps.

### Know Your Rights

Provides simple, step-by-step legal guidance for tenants, farmers, and citizens facing common legal issues, without requiring prior legal knowledge.

### Property Document Analyzer

An AI-powered system that reviews property-related documents, checks for missing information or signatures, flags suspicious inconsistencies, and helps users identify possible fraud risks.

## Who It Helps

* Tenants signing rental agreements
* Families applying for welfare schemes
* Farmers and rural citizens seeking legal awareness
* First-time property buyers

WillMade is built for Karnataka’s 6.5 crore residents who deserve accessible, affordable, and understandable legal guidance in their own language.

**Challenges we ran into**

# Technical Challenges Faced During Development

### 1. Identifying the Correct Gemini Model

One of the earliest challenges was finding the correct Gemini API model name. Multiple versions such as `gemini-1.5-flash`, `gemini-1.5-flash-latest`, and `gemini-2.0-flash` returned 404 errors. After debugging and querying the `/v1beta/models` endpoint directly, we discovered that `gemini-2.5-flash` was the correct supported model.

### 2. CORS Configuration Issues

The frontend was running on Vite (`localhost:5173`), while the backend CORS policy only allowed requests from `localhost:3000`. This caused silent API request failures until the mismatch was identified and corrected.

### 3. API Key Security Mistake

During debugging, the Gemini API key was accidentally exposed publicly. The key had to be revoked and regenerated immediately. This highlighted the importance of properly securing secrets using `.env` files and avoiding exposure during development.

### 4. Route Export and Import Mismatch

The `schemes.js` file exported `{ router, SCHEMES }`, but the main server file imported it as a standalone router. This caused a startup crash with the error:
`TypeError: argument handler must be a function`.
The issue was resolved by correcting the export/import structure.

### 5. PDF Generation and Streaming

Integrating server-side PDF generation using `pdfkit` required careful handling of response streams. Additional work was needed to correctly configure HTTP headers such as `Content-Type` and `Content-Disposition` so generated PDFs could download properly in the browser.

**Open Innovation**

# Why WillMade Fits the Open Innovation Track

WillMade (Nyaya Mitra) fits the Open Innovation track by making legal awareness and guidance accessible to people who have traditionally been excluded from it due to high legal costs, language barriers, and lack of awareness.

The platform combines AI with Karnataka-specific legal rules and welfare information to provide ordinary citizens with instant, understandable, and free legal assistance. Instead of focusing on enterprise or corporate use cases, WillMade applies technology to solve everyday grassroots problems faced by tenants, farmers, low-income families, and first-time property buyers.

For example:

* A tenant in Bengaluru can identify unfair clauses in a rental agreement before signing it.
* A farmer in Raichur can better understand housing or land-related rights.
* A BPL family in Yadgir can discover government welfare schemes they may qualify for but were previously unaware of.

The project is designed as an open and scalable platform built using accessible technologies and open APIs. It aims to support multilingual access, including Kannada and Hindi, so that legal guidance becomes understandable and usable for people beyond English-speaking urban users.

By simplifying legal knowledge and making it freely available, WillMade attempts to bridge the gap between citizens and systems that are often difficult to navigate without professional help.

> Advanced AI Inspection Engine

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Three.JS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Three.JS-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Problems It Solves
Reduces manual inspection errors by automatically comparing real-world environments with digital layouts, specifications, or reference images in real time.
Eliminates the need for expensive industrial inspection hardware by using standard smartphone and laptop cameras for spatial verification and compliance analysis.
Helps industries quickly detect misplaced objects, missing components, alignment issues, and incorrect physical execution before they lead to costly mistakes or safety risks.
Improves efficiency in construction, warehousing, manufacturing, PCB inspection, and interior planning by providing instant AI-powered compliance scoring and visual deviation detection.
Makes spatial verification more scalable and accessible through lightweight AI technology, voice-guided interaction, and accessibility-focused design that can operate on commonly available devices.

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges We Ran Into
Implementing accurate real-time spatial alignment between the uploaded reference layout and the live webcam feed was difficult because variations in camera angle, lighting conditions, and object positioning affected feature detection and homography calculations.
Achieving reliable deviation detection using only standard RGB cameras was challenging since the system had to distinguish between actual placement errors and environmental noise such as shadows, reflections, background clutter, and motion blur.
Optimizing OpenCV and AI processing for smooth real-time performance on regular laptops and mobile devices required extensive tuning to reduce latency while maintaining accurate spatial verification and compliance scoring.
Integrating multiple technologies including React.js, MediaPipe, OpenCV, webcam streaming, and AI-based spatial analysis into a seamless workflow created synchronization and compatibility issues during development and testing.
Designing an accessibility-friendly interface with voice-guided verification and adaptive visualization while keeping the system lightweight, intuitive, and scalable for different industrial use cases required significant UI/UX experimentation and refinement.

### EDITH
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/edith-2ffa) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/hmvyeVPnLhY) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Hack%20Storm%202.26-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hack-storm.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Authenticity Analysis System

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![LibROSA](https://img.shields.io/badge/LibROSA-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**Detecting authentic content in an era of sophisticated digital manipulation across all media types.**

EDITH solves this by building an **intelligent multimodal authenticity analyzer** that simultaneously detects text manipulation, deepfake images, AI-generated video, and synthetic audio. Rather than relying on humans to manually verify each claim, EDITH automatically:

1. **Identifies content modality** - Determines if input is **text**, **image**, **video**, or **audio**
2. **Routes to specialized detectors** - Each modality has purpose-built algorithms (CNN for images, ASR for audio, temporal analysis for video)
3. **Returns confidence scores** - Uses a calibrated 0-1 scale (0.05=authentic, 0.95=manipulated)
4. **Provides explainable evidence** - Visual heatmaps, highlighted suspicious claims, extracted frames, spectrograms
5. **Performs real-time fact checking** - Searches the web for corroborating sources
6. **Integrates seamlessly** - REST API enables deployment across platforms (journalism, social media, enterprises)

This enables journalists to verify sources before publication, social platforms to flag suspicious content, and researchers to benchmark manipulation detection algorithms at scale.

**Challenges we ran into**

**Five major technical obstacles required careful troubleshooting and creative solutions.**

The development process exposed critical issues at the dependency, environment, and architecture levels. The most time consuming challenge involved **PyTorch/Transformers version conflicts** - the requirements.txt specified PyTorch 2.8.0 and Transformers 4.55.4, but these versions had incompatible dependencies with TorchVision. When installing, TorchVision 0.20.1 demanded PyTorch 2.5.1, creating an unsolvable version constraint. GPU-enabled PyTorch 2.8.0 also failed to load (`DLL load failed`), causing the Transformers pipeline module to become unimportable and cascading into backend startup failures.

**Environment activation errors** confused the Python and Node.js separation - the frontend is a React/Vite project (JavaScript only) with no Python dependencies, yet initial troubleshooting attempted to create a `.venv` in the frontend directory. This blocked development until clarifying that only the backend needs Python virtual environments.

**Windows file locking issues** prevented venv recreation - partially corrupted virtual environments left locked files that PowerShell couldn't delete, requiring fresh installation rather than repair attempts.

**Process spawning conflicts** occurred with Uvicorn's hot reload feature - the main process and subprocess inherited conflicting import locks, causing multiprocessing to fail with obscure errors.

Finally, **npm working directory confusion** happened when terminal context drifted, making npm look for `package.json` in the wrong folder. Each required step-by-step diagnosis and systematic resolution.

### EXAMPROCOTOR
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/examprocotor-2e91) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://examproctor.vercel.app/) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Hack%20Storm%202.26-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hack-storm.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> Cheating is not the solution

![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![Matplotlib](https://img.shields.io/badge/Matplotlib-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![React.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/React.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![FAST API](https://img.shields.io/badge/FAST%20API-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Proposal: Integrated Integrity Module for Project Recall
In the development of Recall—an AI Second Brain Ecosystem—the integration of a sophisticated Integrity & Authenticity Detector is not merely a "policing" feature; it is a core architectural necessity. To ensure the ecosystem remains a reliable repository of genuine thought and verified learning, this module addresses the following critical challenges:

1. Guarding the "Human-in-the-Loop"
As an AI Second Brain, Recall is designed to augment human intelligence, not replace it.

The Problem: Users may become over-reliant on LLM-generated summaries or notes, leading to a "hollow" knowledge base where the user hasn't actually processed the information.

The Solution: Our detector analyzes the provenance of entries. It distinguishes between user-synthesized insights and raw AI outputs, flagging content that lacks "human cognitive signatures." This ensures the "Second Brain" reflects the user's actual growth.

2. Verification of Intellectual Ownership
In collaborative or academic environments, the validity of a "Second Brain" depends on the originality of its contents.

The Problem: The risk of accidental plagiarism or the ingestion of unverified, copyrighted material can compromise the user's professional standing.

The Solution: The project implements Cross-Reference Indexing. By scanning internal repositories and external databases, the detector ensures that every "Recall" entry is either properly cited or demonstrates a unique structural logic (using Abstract Syntax Tree analysis for code and linguistic variability for text).

3. Maintaining Data Signal-to-Noise Ratio
For an AI ecosystem to be effective, the data it "recalls" must be high-quality.

The Problem: Automated "junk" data or mass-generated AI text can clutter the ecosystem, making search and retrieval less relevant.

The Solution: Our Integrity Engine acts as a quality-control gatekeeper. It identifies "low-entropy" content (highly predictable, AI-standard text) and deprioritizes it in the knowledge graph, ensuring that the most valuable, human-centric insights remain at the forefront.

**Challenges we ran into**

Building a real-time, multi-modal AI proctoring system required solving several complex engineering hurdles across computer vision, concurrent system design, and browser-level security.

1. Managing Concurrency and Shared State in Python Running an intensive computer vision loop (OpenCV), separate ML pipelines (YOLOv8, MediaPipe, MTCNN), hardware monitors, and a FastAPI server simultaneously was a massive architectural challenge. Everything had to be non-blocking.

The Fix: We decoupled the heavy ML processing (main.py) from the dashboard server (app.py). We engineered an atomic file-based state store (state_store.py) using write-to-temp and rename operations. This allowed the ML loop to stream detection states, metrics, and MJPEG frames to the FastAPI server locally without race conditions or corrupted JSON reads.
2. Optimizing Real-Time ML Performance (<40ms latency) Running six distinct AI detectors in parallel usually destroys frame rates.

The Fix: We couldn't run every model on every frame. We implemented dynamic detection intervals (e.g., face detection runs every 5 frames, object detection every 3 frames, mouth movement requires 8 consecutive frames of deviation to trigger). We also added an intelligent fallback system so lightweight models (like gaze tracking) reuse landmarks from the heavier face detector instead of recalculating them from scratch.
3. Taming Browser Quirks for the Lockdown System The useLockdown.ts hook was incredibly difficult to perfect. Modern browsers intentionally restrict what a web app can know about the user's OS for privacy reasons, which makes cheating detection hard.

The Fix: We had to triangulate cheating behavior using multiple Web APIs. For example, to detect if a student plugged in an external HDMI monitor or a secondary webcam mid-exam, we couldn't just rely on the devicechange event listener (which is notoriously unreliable across different OSs). We had to implement a continuous polling mechanism on navigator.mediaDevices.enumerateDevices() to keep a hashed snapshot of their hardware signature and diff it every 2 seconds.
4. Eliminating False Positives In early iterations, the system punished students unfairly—flagging them for whispering if they simply stretched, or triggering an "Object Detected" alert if they held a calculator but the model thought it was a phone.

The Fix: We built a custom MetricsTracker class directly into the backend that generated a real-time confusion matrix (True Positives, False Positives, etc.). By analyzing the F1 Score and MCC (Matthews Correlation Coefficient), we fine-tuned our confidence thresholds (bumping YOLO object detection to 0.65) and added "cooldowns" and "hold frames" to smooth out transient spikes in the detection data.
5. "Address In Use" and Port Collisions During rapid testing, the FastAPI server and Uvicorn would frequently crash on startup because ghost processes from previous runs were still holding the port.

The Fix: We implemented a robust port-retry and auto-cleanup mechanism on startup to gracefully kill zombie processes or bind to the next available port, ensuring the system boots reliably every time.

Team **Executors** -- [BARSHAN MAJUMDAR](https://github.com/Barshan-Majumdar), [Subhamoy Datta](https://github.com/subhamoydatta703), [Sagar Bhadra](http://github.com/SagarBhadra01), [Arpan Sarkar](https://github.com/arpan7sarkar)

### DevdhamPath
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/devdhampath-f5af) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/UxezUjjyOUE) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Hacknovate--7.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hacknovate07.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> AI powered pilgrimage and  crowd management System

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask-333333?style=flat-square) ![Deep Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Deep%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![SQLite](https://img.shields.io/badge/SQLite-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![SQLAlchemy](https://img.shields.io/badge/SQLAlchemy-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**The Problem It Solves**

- Uncontrolled overcrowding during peak pilgrimage seasons leads to dangerous situations like stampedes, injuries, and chaos.
- No structured queue or time-slot system, resulting in long waiting hours and poor crowd distribution.
- Lack of real-time visibility for authorities → decisions are reactive, not preventive.
- Inefficient emergency response due to delayed detection of critical situations.
- Exclusion of non-smartphone users, especially elderly pilgrims, from digital services.
**
Bottom line:** current systems are manual, slow, and blind to real-time conditions—this is why crowd disasters happen.

**What People Can Use It For / How It Makes Things Easier & Safer**
**For Pilgrims (End Users)**

- Book time slots → no waiting in long queues
- QR-based entry → fast, contactless access
- Live crowd updates → avoid peak congestion zones
- Emergency alerts → immediate safety instructions
- IVR access → usable even without smartphones

**For Authorities / Management**

- Real-time crowd density monitoring via IoT sensors
- AI surge prediction → act before overcrowding happens
- Live dashboard → centralized control of entire site
- Automated alerts (SOS, medical, congestion)

**Challenges we ran into**

1. No Real-World Dataset for Crowd Density
Problem: No clean, labeled dataset for pilgrimage crowd density + surge prediction. Public datasets didn’t match real scenarios (temples ≠ stadiums).
Fix:
Combined multiple sources: CCTV samples, simulated crowd data, and synthetic data generation
Used data augmentation + manual labeling for edge cases
Created a hybrid dataset (real + simulated) to train models

2. Model Training Took Too Long (LSTM + XGBoost)
Problem: Training on time-series + sensor data was slow and inconsistent; models overfitting or lagging.
Fix:
Reduced feature space → removed useless sensor noise
Switched to lighter models for real-time inference
Used batching + early stopping
Pre-trained offline → deployed optimized version

3. Real-Time Prediction Latency
Problem: Predictions were delayed → useless for crowd control (needs instant response).
Fix:
Moved from heavy pipelines → stream-based processing (Flask APIs + lightweight inference)
Cached recent predictions
Reduced model complexity for faster response

4. IoT Sensor Noise & Inconsistent Data
Problem: IR/RFID sensors gave noisy or duplicate readings → wrong crowd counts.
Fix:
Applied filtering + smoothing (moving average)
Added threshold logic to ignore spikes
Cross-verified with multiple sensors before updating count

5. QR Code Entry System Sync Issues
Problem: Duplicate scans and delays caused entry mismatch.
Fix:
Implemented unique token validation + timestamp locking
Backend check to prevent reuse
Added retry + fallback mechanism

6. IVR System Setup Was Hard
Problem: Integrating IVR for non-smartphone users was messy (language handling, flow control, API sync).
Fix:
Used third-party IVR APIs instead of building from scratch
Designed simple menu-based flows (press 1, 2, etc.)
Connected IVR to backend via REST APIs

7. Integration of Multiple Components
Problem: AI models + IoT + frontend + APIs didn’t sync properly at first.
Fix:
Modularized system (separate services for each layer)
Used REST APIs as central communication layer
Tested each module independently before integration

8. Handling Peak Load (Scalability Issue)
Problem: System lagged when simulating large crowd spikes (thousands of users).
Fix:
Optimized database queries
Used caching for frequent requests
Designed system to be horizontally scalable

### Brain tumor detection using MRI images
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/brain-tumor-detection-using-mri-images-d488) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://bug-busters.streamlit.app) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/rfY-ExvozfA?is=TtkLZj1TDL0GU1_R) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-Hacknovate--7.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hacknovate07.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> AI integrated brain_tumor_detector

![TensorFlow](https://img.shields.io/badge/TensorFlow-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Plotly](https://img.shields.io/badge/Plotly-333333?style=flat-square) ![Streamlit](https://img.shields.io/badge/Streamlit-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

An AI-based system that detects and classifies brain tumors from MRI scans using a deep learning model. It categorizes images into Glioma, Meningioma, Pituitary, or No Tumor and provides confidence scores. The system delivers fast and accurate results through a simple web interface, helping doctors in efficient diagnosis and decision support

**Challenges we ran into**

1. Keras/TensorFlow Version Mismatch
This was our biggest pain point. Your model was saved with Keras 3.13.2 (standalone Keras 3), but your environment had TensorFlow's bundled Keras which is a completely different package. The error No module named keras.api and the InputLayer batch_shape deserialization error both stemmed from this.
How you overcame it: You used h5py to read the metadata baked into the model file itself, which revealed the exact Keras version used during training. Then you switched to keras.models.load_model() instead of tf.keras.models.load_model(), and pinned keras==3.13.2 with a compatible tensorflow-cpu==2.18.0 since TF 2.18+ is the first version that coexists with standalone Keras 3.

2. Python Version Incompatibility on Streamlit Cloud
Streamlit Cloud defaulted to Python 3.14.3, but TensorFlow has no wheels built for Python 3.14 yet. So every deployment failed at the dependency install stage with "no matching distribution found."
How you overcame it: runtime.txt was being silently ignored by Streamlit Cloud's newer infrastructure. The fix was using .python-version file containing just 3.11, and additionally manually setting Python 3.11 in the Streamlit Cloud dashboard under Settings → General, which takes absolute priority over any file-based configuration.

Team **Bug busters** -- [priyanshu bharti](https://github.com/priyanshub3443), [Puru Vashishtha](https://github.com/puruvashishtha776-ai), [Mohd Shaqueeb](https://github.com/mohdshaqueeb), [Mayank Chauhan](https://github.com/spiritchill)

### Watchr
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/watchr-88d4) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/LakshyaBetala/Watchr_Ai) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtube.com/shorts/WEfXDLrlW3o?si=TJH2HUsX1lHutZxY) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-DevsHouse%20'26-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://devshouse26.devfolio.co) ![Likes](https://img.shields.io/badge/Likes-1-FF6B6B?style=flat-square)

> VisionIQ: Multi-Signal Spatial Intelligence

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![Next.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Next.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![ONNX](https://img.shields.io/badge/ONNX-333333?style=flat-square) ![YOLOv3 Algorithm](https://img.shields.io/badge/YOLOv3%20Algorithm-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Traditional surveillance relies on exhausted security personnel staring at dozens of screens, making it almost impossible to catch coordinated theft or identify early-stage safety hazards. **Watchr** transforms passive CCTV cameras into an active, intelligent spatial-security engine that operates entirely locally on edge hardware. 

Here is how Watchr secures modern retail and enterprise spaces:

* **Automated Retail Shrink Prevention:** Instead of just identifying objects, our AI engine identifies *suspicious intent*. We run a custom 6-layer behavioral array that correlates store zone transitions (skipping the billing counter), abnormal velocity spikes, bounding box shrinkage (crouching/concealment), and active YOLO confidence-drop matrices to catch shoplifters in real-time before they leave the store. 
* **Zero-Latency Fire & Smoke Detection:** Traditional smoke alarms only trigger when smoke physically reaches the ceiling. Watchr uses a 7-layer statistical processing engine (evaluating thermodynamic plasma variance, hue flicker, and blooming area expansion) or a targeted YOLO segmentation model to catch fire and smoke visually the very second a hazardous spark occurs.
* **Spatial & Dwell Time Analytics:** Using our point-and-click UI calibrator, store owners can draw semantic zones (e.g., "Shelf 1", "Billing Counter"). The AI maps tracked shoppers to these zones to measure engagement dwell-times and flow paths.
* **Employee Security Kiosk:** A lightweight edge terminal utilizing face cascades and KMeans dominant-color mathematical extraction to isolate uniform colors, seamlessly registering and validating staff presence.

**Challenges we ran into**

Building a synchronized, multi-camera AI engine that runs at high speeds on local edge hardware presented massive hurdles in threading, tracking persistence, and heuristic filtering:

**1. Multi-Camera I/O Bottlenecking**
* **The Hurdle:** OpenCV's VideoCapture is heavily blocking. Polling 4 separate camera streams natively in a single loop completely strangled our YOLOv8 inference hardware, dropping the frame rate to unplayable levels.
* **The Fix:** I engineered multi_camera.py ,a multi-threaded daemon architecture that decouples camera read I/O from the AI inference loop. Independent threads constantly swallow the latest frames into a buffer while the main CNN executes inference exclusively on the freshest frames asynchronously. 

**2. Tracker Amnesia & Occlusion Failures**
* **The Hurdle:** Our initial `CentroidTracker` matched IDs based purely on Euclidean distance. When two customers crossed paths in an aisle, their IDs would swap randomly, completely destroying the chronological sequence of our theft-detection matrices.
* **The Fix:** We migrated to **BoT-SORT** directly layered over our Ultralytics backend. By utilizing Kalman Filters for velocity momentum and ReID (appearance embeddings), the engine successfully maintains object permanence even if a shopper is fully occluded behind a shelf for several seconds. 

**3. Fire Detection False Alarms**
* **The Hurdle:** Our early motion-diff algorithms for fire would falsely trigger if a person wearing a red shirt walked quickly across the room, or if neon lights flashed. 
* **The Fix:** We built the **"7-Layer Mathematical Engine."** Before alerting, the system specifically mandates that the pixels evaluate positively for *Fractal Edge Chaos* (real fire has a noisy Canny-edge signature, shirts don't) and *Thermodynamic Plasma Variance* (real fire has a multi-hue white/yellow/orange center, while a flashing ambulance light is homogeneously monochromatic).

**IoT & Smart Devices**

**Watchr** fundamentally transforms passive, "dumb" CCTV cameras and standalone webcams into an interconnected network of highly active, intelligent edge devices.

Here is exactly how our project embodies the IoT & Smart Devices track:

* **Edge Intelligence over Cloud Dependency:** Instead of streaming massive, high-bandwidth video feeds to a centralized cloud for processing (which introduces latency and privacy risks), Watchr’s AI Engine runs entirely locally at the edge. The system processes YOLOv8 inference, multi-camera synchronization, and complex 7-layer heuristic fire detection right on the local hardware (capable of running on edge devices like Jetson Nanos or retail NVR PCs).

* **From Video Feed to Structured IoT Data:** We don't just record video; we synthesize the environment. The cameras act as autonomous IoT sensors. The engine processes raw pixels and extracts structured environmental data in real-time—customer dwell-times, spatial zone transitions, velocity spikes, and hazard triggers. This data is packaged symmetrically into lightweight JSON payloads and dispatched seamlessly to our connected backend dashboard.

* **Smart Environment Calibration:** Traditional enterprise cameras require complex physical setups to designate limits. Watchr provides an integrated `auto_calibrator` that allows average store owners to treat their hardware exactly like smart devices: they simply click and drag to digitally map physical space (like "Billing Desk" or "Aisle 3") directly onto the camera feed, allowing the software to immediately understand its physical environment.

* **Autonomous Actuation:** Because of the zero-latency processing on the edge, the system acts as a high-speed intelligent trigger. If the Behavioral Theft Heuristics trip, or if the Thermodynamic Fire Variance confirms a spark, the system instantly fires webhooks that can automatically lock smart doors, sound localized alarms, and push notifications to employee mobile devices on the network.

Team **Chakravyuh Breakers** -- [Kavin G](https://github.com/gkavin2527), [Mouriyan Gandhi](https://github.com/Mouriyan-gandhi), [Lakshya Betala](https://github.com/lakshyabetala), [Yash A](https://github.com/Yash13606)
