![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

# 🛡️ ThreatPulse: Problem Statement, Use Cases & Value Proposition

---

## 1. The Critical Problems It Solves

Modern Enterprise Security Operations Centers (SOCs) and cyber defense teams face five massive industry bottlenecks:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ The Industry Bottleneck              │ How Modern Security Teams Suffer Today                                 │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. Adversarial ML Blind Spots        │ Antivirus and EDR tools rely on ML classifiers that collapse when      │
│                                      │ malware adds benign bytes, section padding, or altered headers.        │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 2. The 4-Day Detection Bottleneck    │ When a new attack technique emerges, security engineers spend days     │
│                                      │ manually writing and debugging SIEM rules for Splunk, Elastic, & Azure.│
├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 3. AI Hallucinations in the SOC      │ Single-prompt AI bots invent non-existent forensic events and generate │
│                                      │ false leads that waste hours of analyst investigation time.            │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4. "Blind" Detection Deployment      │ SOCs deploy detection rules without testing against benign baselines,  │
│                                      │ triggering false positive storms that flood analysts with alert fatigue│
├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 5. Rogue Autonomous AI Risk          │ Fully autonomous AI agents can accidentally isolate domain controllers │
│                                      │ or kill critical database servers without authorized human sign-off.   │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## 2. Who Can Use This & What They Use It For

### 🎯 1. SOC Analysts (Tier 1, 2, and 3)
* **What they use it for**: Accelerated incident triage and investigation.
* **How it helps**: Automatically correlates noisy raw endpoint logs into prioritized incident cases with an **explainable 10-factor risk score (0–100)**.
* **Key Feature**: **Forensic Incident Time-Travel Scrubber** to replay attacks second-by-second and understand exactly what happened.

### 🟣 2. Purple Teams & SecOps Leaders
* **What they use it for**: Continuous security posture validation.
* **How it helps**: Safely emulates adversary tactics (e.g., LOLBins, Ransomware, Cobalt Strike), identifies unresolved detection gaps, and **empirically proves coverage gains (+32.7% delta)** through closed-loop retesting.

### 🛠️ 3. Detection Engineers & SIEM Content Authors
* **What they use it for**: Rapid detection rule creation and multi-SIEM translation.
* **How it helps**: Translates validated detection logic simultaneously into **Splunk SPL**, **Elastic EQL**, **Microsoft Sentinel KQL (Azure)**, and **IBM QRadar AQL**.
* **Key Feature**: **Autonomous AST Self-Repair Loop** that catches compiler syntax errors and fixes YAML schemas automatically.

### 🧬 4. Adversarial ML & Threat Researchers
* **What they use it for**: Measuring classifier resilience and evasive malware behavior.
* **How it helps**: Drag-and-drop any custom binary or test 8 real-world threat archetypes to quantify exactly how mathematical perturbations (Section Appending, Slack Padding, Header Evasion) degrade model confidence.

### 🎓 5. Cybersecurity Educators & Students
* **What they use it for**: Interactive cyber defense and blue-team training.
* **How it helps**: Provides an end-to-end sandbox to visually understand how an attack flows through detection, investigation, rule authoring, and containment.

---

## 3. How ThreatPulse Makes Existing Tasks Faster, Easier & Safer

| Traditional SOC / Detection Workflow | With ThreatPulse Platform | Measurable Benefit |
|---|---|---|
| **Incident Investigation**: Analysts spend 2–4 hours manually querying logs across different SIEM dashboards. | **5-Agent LangGraph Swarm** triages, hunts, and audits the attack in **10 seconds**. | ⚡ **95% faster incident triage** |
| **Detection Authoring**: Engineers spend 2–3 days writing rules and translating them across Splunk, Elastic, & Sentinel. | **Autonomous Sigma Compiler** generates, self-repairs, and compiles to 4 SIEM dialects automatically. | 🚀 **From 3 days to 30 seconds** |
| **Rule Quality Assurance**: Rules are deployed untested into production, causing massive false positive spikes. | **110-Event Regression Lab** tests against

**Challenges we ran into**

Premature UI Rendering and State Synchronization
Initially, our React frontend was initializing state variables with completed evaluation results rather than starting at a baseline. When opening the Purple-Team tab, it immediately displayed the final 94.2% coverage metric before anyone even clicked "Run Retest." Similarly, the ML evasion meters pre-loaded their final results, making the dashboard feel static rather than interactive.

How we fixed it: We completely overhauled the React state management to enforce strict interactive triggers. We refactored the components to load only baseline data initially (e.g., showing 61.5% starting coverage) and tied the heavy backend executions—like the 5-agent LangGraph swarm and the PyTorch ML inferences—to explicit onClick events. We also had to battle aggressive Vercel browser caching during deployment, which we resolved by building dynamic clear-states tied to our dropdown menus to ensure the UI didn't hold onto stale data.

The "Rogue AI" Risk and LLM Hallucinations
While building the automated SOAR response, we realized a massive operational danger: if the AI hallucinated a threat or triggered a false positive, an ungated autonomous agent could accidentally isolate a critical server and cause an enterprise outage. Relying on a single LLM prompt for both analysis and containment was too risky.

How we fixed it: We decentralized the AI into a LangGraph state machine and built in two strict guardrails. First, we introduced an "Evidence Judge" agent whose sole job is to audit every claim made by the other agents against raw Sysmon log event IDs, instantly rejecting unsupported hallucinations. Second, we built a cryptographic SOAR gate. High-impact actions (like network isolation) are now intercepted, paused in a WAITING_APPROVAL queue, and secured with a SHA-256 HMAC digest so they cannot be executed or tampered with until a human approves them.

Dynamic Scenario Binding Across Microservices
Once we expanded the platform to handle multiple distinct threat scenarios (like CryptoLocker Ransomware vs. Living-off-the-Land LOLBins vs. Benign Admin tools), we struggled to keep the platform synchronized. Changing the threat sample in the ML lab wouldn't automatically update the Detection rules or the SOAR playbook, leading to mismatched data across the dashboard tabs.

How we fixed it: We engineered a Global Synchronized Scenario State Engine. We linked the application state so that choosing a new threat archetype from the navigation dropdown dynamically binds the correct telemetry replay, the specific LangGraph agent context, and the Sigma AST compiler to that exact scenario. This ensured that if we tested the CryptoLocker artifact, the regression lab accurately switched to testing volume shadow copy deletion rules without data bleeding over from the other tests.

**The problem it solves**

Crowded events can quickly become unsafe when too many people gather near the same entrance or exit. CrowdShield uses AI to detect overcrowding in real time and guide people toward less crowded exits. This helps security teams respond faster, reduce congestion, prevent panic, and make large events safer and easier to manage.

**Challenges we ran into**

One of our biggest challenges was integrating five separate parts of the project into one working, functional model. Each component worked individually, but making them communicate smoothly was a tedious task. We had issues with data flow, and getting real-time detection to work consistently.

We overcame this by testing each module separately, defining clear data flow between them, and then integrating them step by step. This helped us identify issues early and eventually bring everything together into one functional CrowdShield system.

### AML Sentinel
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/aml-sentinel-6651) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Hariprasath-5128/AML-SENTINEL.git) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://aml-sentinel-seven.vercel.app/) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/1JZGcUVqNO0) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackVerse:%20Into%20the%20Web-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackverse-into-the-web.devfolio.co)

> See the network. Catch the crime!

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![pandas](https://img.shields.io/badge/pandas-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Traditional Anti-Money Laundering (AML) systems rely on rigid, rules-based logic that evaluates transactions in isolation. This legacy approach creates two massive problems for financial institutions: an overwhelming flood of false-positive alerts (often exceeding 95%), and a complete failure to detect modern, sophisticated laundering rings that spread illicit funds across complex networks of accounts.

When a true positive is found, compliance analysts must spend hours manually tracing funds across databases and writing detailed Suspicious Activity Reports (SARs) for regulators. Furthermore, attempting to replace these legacy rules with standard "Black Box" AI introduces regulatory risk, as auditors demand strict explainability for why an account was frozen.

AML Sentinel solves this by shifting the paradigm from isolated transaction monitoring to graph-based network analysis paired with Generative AI explainability.

What people can use it for:
1)Uncovering Hidden Laundering Rings: Instead of looking at single transactions, compliance teams can upload transaction datasets and use AML Sentinel to evaluate the entire network topology. It automatically flags complex typologies like "smurfing" (structuring large sums into small, undetected transactions) and nested mule accounts.

2)Automated Alert Triage: Financial Investigation Units (FIUs) can use the platform as a primary alert queue. The system scores every entity using an ensemble of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs), pushing only the highest-risk networks to the top of the queue.

3)Instant SAR Generation: Investigators can use the Gemini AI integration to automatically synthesize raw transaction data and GNN model weights into a clear, human-readable narrative, dramatically accelerating the reporting process.

How it makes existing tasks easier and safer:
1)Contextual Accuracy over Rigid Rules: By utilizing a parallel ensemble of 4 Graph Neural Networks (GIN, GAT, PNA, RGCN), the platform catches sophisticated evasion tactics that traditional rules miss, while simultaneously ignoring benign behavior that rules normally flag. This drastically reduces false positives and analyst fatigue.

2)Traceable, Explainable AI: It bridges the gap between advanced machine learning and regulatory compliance. Rather than acting as a black box, AML Sentinel traces exactly which nodes and edges contributed to the risk score.

3)Regulatory Safety & Auditor Confidence: Every AI-driven verdict generated by Gemini is firmly grounded in the retrieved graph data. Analysts are presented with verifiable evidence alongside the AI's reasoning, ensuring that every account freeze or compliance action is safe, defensible, and fully auditable.

**Challenges we ran into**

Building a system that merges Graph Neural Networks (GNNs), Generative AI, and a real-time web interface brought several complex engineering challenges. Here are the major hurdles we faced and how we overcame them:

1. Untangling the "Hairball" of Graph Visualization:

The Hurdle: Our PyTorch Geometric (PyG) backend successfully detected complex laundering topologies, but rendering these transactions on the frontend initially resulted in a chaotic, unreadable "hairball" of overlapping nodes and edges. For a compliance analyst, an unreadable graph is a useless graph.

The Fix: We completely rebuilt the network graph rendering engine on the frontend. We implemented a custom d3.js collision force with a stronger negative charge to prevent node overlap. To make the data intuitive, we added animated directional particles along the edges (where speed and width scale based on the transaction amount) so you can actually see the money moving. Finally, we added a hover-to-focus feature that dims everything except the hovered node's immediate neighborhood, allowing analysts to easily trace illicit flows by eye.

2. Taming the LLM for Strict UI Schemas:

The Hurdle: We used Gemini AI to generate explainable narratives for the flagged graphs. However, Large Language Models are inherently non-deterministic. If Gemini returned a beautifully written narrative but broke our JSON structure, the React frontend would crash trying to parse the verdict, reasoning steps, and model weights.

The Fix: Prompt engineering wasn't enough. We implemented strict response_schema enforcement directly within the Gemini API orchestration layer and created a robust fallback chain. By locking down the output format and enforcing a strict budget on the AI's generation steps, we ensured 100% deterministic, parseable JSON payloads. The UI now reliably renders the AI's reasoning step-by-step without ever breaking the component state.

3. React Event Bubbling Infinite Loops:

The Hurdle: While building the drag-and-drop CSV upload zone for the GNN Ensemble, we ran into a bizarre browser bug. Clicking the drop zone was causing the OS file explorer dialog to stack and open multiple times infinitely, completely locking up the user's browser.

The Fix: The issue was an event bubbling recursion—the hidden <input type="file"> sat inside the div carrying the onClick handler. When input.click() was called, the click bubbled up to the parent div, which called input.click() again. We fixed this by un-nesting the input outside the clickable region and ensuring our onFileChange handler manually cleared the input value afterward, completely resolving the infinite loop and allowing seamless dataset switching.

4. Visualizing Parallel Asynchronous AI Pipelines:

The Hurdle: We needed to prove to users that our 4 models (GIN, GAT, PNA, RGCN) were scoring the graph simultaneously in parallel, not sequentially. Coordinating SVG path animations with React state changes to accurately reflect the backend's parallel processing was a synchronization nightmare.

The Fix: We built a centralized, sequence-based state machine (SEQ_STEPS) in React tied to a strict 820ms interval timer. We refactored our SVG <animateMotion> components so that the data pipeline dynamically splits—illuminating all 4 model paths simultaneously. Once a computation step completes, the moving particles transition seamlessly into static, solid lines. This creates a deeply satisfying, accurate visual representation of the backend's parallel architecture.

Team **Syntax Terrors** -- Hariprasath M, Jaswant Kumar, Monish C, [Jack Farrell](https://github.com/jackfarrell1221), [Abilash K](https://github.com/Abilash-AK)

### Orbital Edge Intelligence
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/orbital-edge-intelligence-d730) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/archittg/zenex2.git) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtube.com/shorts/s7vWKcbc9hc?feature=share) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackVerse:%20Into%20the%20Web-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackverse-into-the-web.devfolio.co)

> Don't Transmit pixels, Transmit Intelligence

![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![YOLOv3 Algorithm](https://img.shields.io/badge/YOLOv3%20Algorithm-333333?style=flat-square) ![Streamlit](https://img.shields.io/badge/Streamlit-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

Building an autonomous satellite simulator sounded cool on paper, but turning orbital physics, heavy machine learning, and a real-time web dashboard into something that actually worked smoothly gave us a serious run for our money. Our biggest hurdle was trying to run YOLO object detection alongside complex simulation loops like battery drain, CPU limits, and dynamic ground-station windows without completely choking our local processing power or making the app crawl. To fix this, we integrated ONNX Runtime with INT8 quantization to shrink the model and speed up inference, which finally let the system run smoothly on lightweight edge-style hardware. On top of that, keeping our React frontend in sync with a fast-moving FastAPI backend became a real puzzle when dealing with erratic telemetry data like fluctuating bandwidth and closing communication windows. We ended up overhauling our backend data-streaming and state management to lock the map view and live charts together, ensuring everything stayed rock-solid even when the satellite's virtual connection abruptly dropped.

**The problem it solves**

Traditional satellite imaging is surprisingly inefficient, forcing spacecraft to capture massive raw files and beam them entirely down to Earth just for ground-based computers to figure out what's in them. This legacy approach creates major bottlenecks by wasting precious bandwidth and battery power on useless data like clouds and empty ocean, while introducing dangerous latency because operators must wait for the next ground-station pass to see anything. Orbital Edge Intelligence solves this by flipping the script running lightweight Edge AI directly on the satellite to analyze images on the fly. Instead of transmitting heavy megabyte files, the system filters out the noise, prioritizes what actually matters (like a wildfire versus an empty field), and beams down tiny, actionable metadata packets. This makes satellite operations infinitely smarter, saving power, slashing bandwidth costs, and turning raw pixels into instant intelligence when every second counts.

### FloodSight
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/floodsight-a44b) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/quantum-banana/floodsight) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/rh8zOTnqQVQ) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-HackVerse:%20Into%20the%20Web-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://hackverse-into-the-web.devfolio.co)

> From Flood Data to Rescue Decisions.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![WebSockets](https://img.shields.io/badge/WebSockets-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Video.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

One of the biggest challenges was combining multiple types of disaster intelligence into one reliable system. FloodSight uses semantic segmentation for flood, road and building understanding, and object detection for people and vehicles. Making both models work together in the same coordinate space and produce stable rescue-zone decisions required careful fusion and validation.

Another major challenge was dataset inconsistency and model training preparation. FloodNet, RescueNet and VisDrone use different label formats, taxonomies and annotation styles. We had to reconcile class mappings, handle dataset leakage, preserve correct supervision and ensure that concepts such as flooded roads, blocked roads and pools were not incorrectly merged.

We also faced performance limitations on shared GPU infrastructure. Although we had access to NVIDIA H100 GPUs, the shared server was heavily CPU-constrained, which initially caused very low GPU utilization and extremely slow training. We optimized the data pipeline, removed redundant per-sample processing, introduced prepared caches and improved prefetching, increasing GPU utilization significantly.

Aerial object detection was another difficult problem. A generic COCO-pretrained YOLO model struggled with small, partially submerged vehicles and people viewed from above. We improved the fallback using tiled aerial inference, temporal tracking and SegFormer-guided reinspection, while preparing a VisDrone-specific fine-tuned detector for better aerial accuracy.

Finally, building a system that is not just visually impressive but operationally explainable was challenging. Instead of simply displaying AI outputs, FloodSight had to convert them into stable rescue zones, urgency scores, reason codes, accessibility decisions and rerouting suggestions while keeping the backend as the single source of truth.

**The problem it solves**

FloodSight solves the decision-making gap during flood emergencies.

During floods, drones and cameras can collect large amounts of footage, but responders still have to manually inspect that footage to understand:

where flooding is severe,
where people may be trapped,
which buildings and roads are affected,
which areas are inaccessible,
and which rescue location should be handled first.

FloodSight converts that raw visual data into actionable rescue intelligence. It detects flood conditions, people, vehicles, road accessibility and structural damage, groups the scene into rescue zones, ranks those zones by urgency, explains why a zone is critical, and suggests safer rescue routes.

> Future-proofing encryption, one scan at a time.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask-333333?style=flat-square) ![scikit-learn](https://img.shields.io/badge/scikit--learn-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![WebSockets](https://img.shields.io/badge/WebSockets-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

**Challenges We Ran Into**

- **Detecting cryptographic algorithms accurately:** Identifying different cryptographic implementations across programming languages and coding styles was challenging.
- **Assessing quantum risk:** Mapping detected algorithms to their potential quantum vulnerability and generating meaningful risk scores required careful analysis.
- **Code-level identification:** Pinpointing the exact files and lines where vulnerabilities occur while processing real repositories was challenging.
- **GitHub API limitations:** Handling rate limits, repository access issues, and different repository structures required robust error handling.
- **Balancing ML with practical security analysis:** We had to ensure that the ML-based risk assessment remained understandable and actionable for developers rather than just producing a score.
- **Building a clear developer experience:** Presenting complex quantum-security concepts through a simple, intuitive dashboard was an important design challenge.

**The problem it solves**

**Problem QuantumReady Solves**

Modern software systems rely heavily on cryptographic algorithms to protect sensitive data, authentication, and communication. However, many widely used cryptographic systems are vulnerable to future quantum attacks, and developers often lack a simple way to identify these risks in their existing codebases.

QuantumReady solves this problem by automatically analyzing a codebase, detecting quantum-vulnerable cryptographic algorithms, assessing their risk, identifying the exact files and code lines affected, and providing migration recommendations.

It helps developers understand what is vulnerable, how serious the risk is, where it occurs, and what should be migrated first, making the transition toward quantum-safe software more practical and actionable.

**The problem it solves**

I believe that there are so many creative and talented people in this world, but they often don't get the right opportunity to meet others who share the same hobbies, interests, or skills. Someone may be passionate about photography, coding, music, painting, gaming, writing, or any other skill, but finding people with the same passion can be difficult.

SkillMate is an idea to bring these people together through their shared hobbies and skills. Instead of connecting with people only because they are from the same place or have mutual friends, SkillMate would help people discover others who are interested in the same things.

**Challenges we ran into**

## Challenges I ran into

Building SkillMates as a solo founder was honestly **very stressful and challenging**. I had to handle everything myself — from designing and building the user dashboard to developing the backend, database, AI features, real-time chat, admin dashboard, and finally deployment.

One of the biggest hurdles was deployment. What worked perfectly on localhost didn't always work after deployment, especially with API connections, environment variables, database access, and Socket.IO. I had to debug each issue step by step and learn from every error.

There were also plenty of unexpected bugs while connecting all the different parts of the application together. Instead of giving up, I kept testing, debugging, researching, and fixing things until the complete flow started working.

The biggest lesson I learned is that **building a product is not just about writing code — it's about solving problems continuously.** 🚀

### MELD

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![PostgreSQL](https://img.shields.io/badge/PostgreSQL-333333?style=flat-square) ![AI/ML](https://img.shields.io/badge/AI/ML-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Finding the right people to build projects with is often difficult. **Students** and **developers** may have ideas and skills but struggle to discover teammates who share their interests, goals, and complementary abilities. **[MELD](meldit.me)** solves this by helping people discover, connect, and form teams based on their skills, interests, and project goals—making it easier to turn ideas into real projects.

**Challenges we ran into**

One challenge was figuring out how to match students with the right teammates based on their skills, interests, and project needs. We also had to make team formation simple enough that students could quickly discover and connect with suitable people.

Another challenge was designing **[MELD](meldit.me)** for students from different academic backgrounds, while keeping the platform flexible enough for both technical and non-technical projects. We addressed this by organizing skills and interests into a broad, multidisciplinary system.

Team **LinkUp** -- Khaleel Syed, SHIVARAJ SHEGUNASI, Saish Kelvekar

### NammaCity
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/nammacity-cd87) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/DevOpps-Lab/NammaCity) [![Demo](https://img.shields.io/badge/Demo-Live-00C853?style=flat-square&logo=googlechrome&logoColor=white)](https://namma.duckdns.org/) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/dS6HibHxz84) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-PEC%20HACKS%204.0-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://pec-hacks.devfolio.co)

> Namma City Namma Responsibility

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Twilio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Twilio-333333?style=flat-square) ![GeoJSON](https://img.shields.io/badge/GeoJSON-333333?style=flat-square) ![Google Analytics](https://img.shields.io/badge/Google%20Analytics-333333?style=flat-square)

**Challenges we ran into**

## Challenges We Ran Into

1. **Public vs. Private Property** – Determining whether an issue is on public or private property was challenging. We use **OpenStreetMap to identify the exact road/location** of the incident and determine the appropriate jurisdiction and need for government intervention.

2. **Inconsistent & Redundant Complaints** – Similar or repeated complaints about the same issue can create unnecessary reports. We solve this by **clustering similar complaints** based on their content and location.

3. **AI-Based Authority Classification** – Identifying the correct authority was challenging. We use a **two-tier location system**: **First ward-based detection in select major cities**, and if that fails, **OpenStreetMap (OSM) identifies the locality, city, and other location details**. This, combined with the issue category, determines the appropriate authority.

4. **Privacy & Data Protection** – Images may capture faces and vehicle number plates. We addressed this through **pixel redaction algorithm** to minimize exposure of personal data and align with DPDP requirements.

5. **Accountability & Legal Risks** – Holding responsible authorities accountable without personally targeting individuals or making unverified allegations that could create defamation risks. We focus on the **issue and responsible authority** through appropriate official escalation channels.

6. **Funding & Sustainability** – Developing a sustainable model to support infrastructure, AI processing, storage, and maintenance through **government partnerships, CSR, grants, and B2G/B2B models**.

**The problem it solves**

## Infrastructure & Deployment

NammaCity is a **citizen-verified civic accountability ledger running on a Vultr instance in Mumbai**.

### 1. Stable & Reliable Infrastructure
We use **Vultr's dedicated resources** to provide a stable and seamless connection between citizens and the platform, ensuring reliable access to civic reporting and tracking.

### 2. Secure & Controlled Processing
Sensitive and computationally intensive operations, such as **incident detection and processing images while keeping even redacted faces and number plates private**, are handled within our controlled server environment, giving us greater control over critical data and processing.

### 3. Automated Accountability
A **server-side scheduled process** continuously monitors complaints, deadlines, SLA breaches, escalations, and citizen notifications without requiring anyone to keep the application open.

### 4. AI-Powered Processing
Dedicated compute resources allow us to run **high-workload AI operations**, including infrastructure-issue detection from images and dashcam footage, without depending entirely on external processing providers.

**Best Use of n8n**

NammaCity is a citizen-verified civic accountability ledger. n8n runs the two jobs the app cannot do for itself.

**Escalation ladder (daily)**
- A charter deadline binds nobody. The RTI Act 2005 sets a statutory 30-day deadline on a named Public Information Officer, the only clock here with legal force.
- n8n finds reports stuck at `escalated` for 3 days with no RTI and files one: candidates → split → act → record.
- Idempotent, so it never files twice.
- Pressure continues with no browser open.

**Ward scoreboard (weekly)**
- Publishes what a department *claims* it fixed against what a resident *verified with a photograph*.
- Indian civic portals report 93-95% resolution because the accused department closes its own ticket. FixMyStreet, measured independently, is near 34%.

**Why this fits the track**
- **n8n never holds a privileged credential.** The scoreboard calls a Postgres function returning counts only, no IDs, coordinates, photos or numbers, so the public anon key suffices.
- We refused to give a third-party account a service-role key that bypasses every RLS policy just to read numbers. Authorisation stays in the database.
- Every RTI goes to a sandbox mailbox, never a real officer.

Team **DevOpps** -- [Aravind S](https://github.com/aravind5423), [Uvan Adhithya](https://github.com/UvanAdhithya), [Pranav Menon](https://github.com/PranavMenon07), [Tejas MD](https://github.com/Tejas-006), [Dheeran Sankaran](https://github.com/Dheeran-S)

> When Access Is Granted, Detection Begins

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![pandas](https://img.shields.io/badge/pandas-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![TypeScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/TypeScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Vercel](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vercel-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

# Continuous Behavioural Trust : Why This Exists

## The Problem in One Sentence
Once someone logs in with valid credentials, most security systems stop watching *how* they behave and that's exactly the gap insiders and hijacked accounts exploit.

## The Story Behind the Problem
Payments officers, admins, and service accounts all have real, legitimate permissions to move money, change account details, or access customer data. That's not a flaw,  it's how the business runs.

The problem is what happens *after* login. Traditional security asks one question: **"Is this person allowed to do this?"** Once the answer is yes, it mostly stops asking anything else. It doesn't notice that:

- A transfer is 40x larger than anything this person has ever approved
- This is the third sensitive action in ten minutes from someone who normally does one an hour
- An account changed its own permissions, then immediately used the new access
- A beneficiary was created and paid out minutes later

Every step here is individually "authorised." None trips an alarm alone. Together, they tell a story, and by the time a human notices it, the damage is usually done. **This is the exact gap our system closes:** the distance between "authorised to do it" and "actually behaving normally."

## What the System Does
Instead of scoring actions in isolation, it builds a live picture of *normal* — per person and per role — and watches for drift. On every action, it asks:

1. **Normal for this person?** (their own transaction sizes, hours, usual beneficiaries)
2. **Normal for anyone in this role?** So a first-time action doesn't get a free pass, and a person's legitimately-different work style doesn't get unfairly flagged.
3. **Does it match a known dangerous chain?** Permission change → sensitive data access → beneficiary added/edited → payment — even spread across hours, even if each step looks harmless alone.
4. **Is it a declared legitimate exception?** Audits, incidents, and maintenance windows are checked first, so security teams doing their job don't get flagged as attackers.

Every action gets a score *and* a plain-English reason, never just a number. When an analyst confirms a flagged action was genuinely malicious, the system remembers the beneficiary/counterparty involved and treats it as elevated-risk going forward. No new rule has to be hand-written.

## Who This Helps
**SOC analysts** get a prioritised, explained queue instead of a raw log firehose. Each alert says *why*, and can be read aloud as a short audio brief for fast triage.

**Fraud/insider-risk teams** get a head start distinguishing "normal Tuesday" from "early-stage beneficiary fraud," instead of manually stitching individually-fine actions into a story.

**Compliance/audit teams** get a system that shows its work. Every score comes with specific reasons (off-hours, amount deviation, role anomaly, chain match, prior flagged entity) which is essential when explaining a decision to a regulator.

**Security leadership** get graduated response instead of binary block/allow: low risk monitors quietly, medium asks for verification, high escalates to a human,  tightening scrutiny without freezing legitimate work.

## A Concrete Example
> A junior ops account  normally just lookups and small transfers, requests a permission change at 11 PM. Twenty minutes later it pulls customer records. An hour later it adds a beneficiary. Before shift-end, it pays that beneficiary.
>
> No single step trips a rule alone- all were authorised. But the system recognises the chain, checks the payment against both personal and role history, confirms no incident window covers tonight, and raises a HIGH alert with a plain-English reason and an audio brief, instead of a fraud discovered next month's reconciliation.

## What Makes This Different
- **Machine Learning + rules together** : an unsupervised anomaly model catches combinations no rule was written for, while explainable rules keep every score human-readable.
- **Compares to self *and* peers** : catches "unusual despite being authorised" without punishing people whose job is just different from their teammates'.
- **Learns from real outcomes** : confirmed incidents feed back into future scoring automatically.
- **Explains itself** : every alert states its specific reasons, not just a score.

## What This Is Not
A behavioural detection layer, not an IAM replacement.  It assumes authentication and permissions are already correct. High-risk alerts go to a human for action; the system doesn't unilaterally lock accounts. It's a second pair of eyes for a security team, not an autonomous decision-maker.

**Challenges we ran into**

# Challenges We Ran Into

## 1. Our model was blind to the exact thing the problem statement asked for
While reviewing our own pipeline against Problem Statement 9's requirements line by line, we caught something uncomfortable: `role` and `privilege_level` were present in our dataset and even carried through as columns, but never actually fed into the model's feature set. Our Isolation Forest was comparing every action against a single global baseline, with no notion of role at all. Which meant the exact thing the problem statement repeats twice- "detect deviations from role-specific and peer-group behavioural patterns" wasn't happening. It would've been easy to miss, since the pipeline ran without errors and produced plausible-looking scores.

**Fix:** we mirrored our existing per-user baseline logic (running mean/std of transaction amounts) but keyed it by role instead, adding `amount_zscore_vs_role_history` and `action_rare_for_role`. Same pattern, new axis of comparison- now an action gets judged against *both* "is this normal for you" and "is this normal for your role."

## 2. A hardcoded category list that would have silently failed
For our attack-chain detector (permission change → sensitive access → beneficiary edit → payment), we categorised actions based on the problem statement's *narrative description* : "beneficiary information," "beneficiary edit"  and guessed the raw action strings would be `modify_beneficiary` / `add_beneficiary`.

When we finally loaded the actual raw dataset and ran `value_counts()` on the action column, we found `create_beneficiary` (46 occurrences), not `add_beneficiary`. If we'd shipped the guess, every `create_beneficiary` event, the *more common* of the two beneficiary actions, and arguably the more suspicious one in a fraud chain, would have silently never matched the category. No error, no crash, just a chain detector that quietly never fired on nearly half its intended trigger.

**Fix:** we made it a rule to verify every category/threshold against real `value_counts()` output before writing detection logic, not after.

## 3. A "smarter" feature that would have made detection worse
Once we built the exact attack-chain matcher, our first instinct was to replace the old, cruder "actions in the last 2 hours" burst-counter with it. It felt more precise. But we caught the tradeoff before implementing it: the chain matcher only fires on that *exact* four-stage pattern. Any attacker who skips a stage (already had the permission, so no `permission_change` fires) would produce zero signal at all if we'd removed the general burst feature.

**Fix:** we kept both: the precise chain match as a high-confidence signal, and the general velocity counter as a fallback that still catches "something's unusual" even when the specific pattern doesn't match.

## 4. Deployment vs. local demo: the risk registry almost had a silent failure mode
Our continuous-learning loop (analyst feedback → flagged beneficiary/counterparty → future risk boost) was designed around a simple local `risk_registry.json`. Testing locally, it worked perfectly. Then we realised Vercel's serverless functions run on a **read-only filesystem** except `/tmp`, which gets wiped between invocations,  meaning feedback submitted on a live deployed demo would appear to succeed in the response, then silently vanish, with the registry never actually persisting.

**Fix:** rather than rushing a database integration under time pressure, we made a deliberate call to run our live walkthrough from a local instance (where file persistence is real and testable), and documented Vercel KV as the drop-in fix for a future version where judges interact with the hosted deployment directly rather than watching a driven demo.

## What This Taught Us
The recurring lesson across all four: **verify against real data and real environments before trusting a plausible-looking assumption.** Every one of these bugs would have run cleanly, produced reasonable-looking output, and only failed silently, which is exactly the kind of failure a security detection system can least afford.

Team **Prism** -- Nikhila Yanamandra, ADITI CHINMAYI, [Stuti Swaminathan](https://github.com/stutiswaminathan)

`2026-08-28`

### RoadWatch

> Detect. Report. Repair. Prevent

![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-333333?style=flat-square) ![Django](https://img.shields.io/badge/Django-333333?style=flat-square) ![TensorFlow](https://img.shields.io/badge/TensorFlow-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![React Native](https://img.shields.io/badge/React%20Native-333333?style=flat-square) ![Microservice Architecture](https://img.shields.io/badge/Microservice%20Architecture-333333?style=flat-square) ![RabbitMQ](https://img.shields.io/badge/RabbitMQ-333333?style=flat-square) ![Apache Kafka](https://img.shields.io/badge/Apache%20Kafka-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

The Problem RoadWatch Solves

RoadWatch addresses the problem of poor accountability and lack of transparency in road infrastructure management.

Currently, when citizens find damaged roads such as potholes, cracks, broken surfaces, or unsafe road conditions, they often do not know which government authority is responsible, whether the road has already been sanctioned for repair, how much budget was allocated, who the contractor is, or whether previous complaints were actually resolved.

This creates several problems:

Citizens don't know whom to complain to — different roads may belong to different authorities such as NH, SH, MDR, or Panchayat.
Complaints are fragmented across different systems and departments.
Road damage reporting is mostly manual, making it difficult to collect structured evidence.
Budget transparency is limited — citizens cannot easily see sanctioned vs. spent amounts.
Contractor accountability is weak because repair history and contractor information are difficult to track.
Repeated road damage is difficult to identify because historical repair records are not connected with new complaints.
Government departments lack a centralized view of complaints, road conditions, budgets, contractors, and repair progress.
SLA violations can go unnoticed, meaning complaints may remain unresolved beyond the expected time.
Citizens may report roads that are not yet registered in the system, creating gaps in road databases.
How RoadWatch Solves It

RoadWatch creates an AI-powered, end-to-end road accountability platform connecting citizens and government authorities.

A citizen can simply take a photograph of a damaged road. RoadWatch can analyze the image, identify the road condition, determine the relevant road type and authority, and connect the complaint with available road, contractor, budget, and repair-history information.

The platform provides:

Citizen → Road Damage → AI Analysis → Responsible Authority → Complaint → SLA Tracking → Contractor → Repair → Verification

This makes the complete lifecycle of a road complaint traceable and accountable.

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges We Ran Into

For your RoadWatch project, you can present the challenges like this:

Identifying the responsible authority: A road can belong to different authorities such as NH, SH, MDR, or Panchayat. Mapping a citizen's complaint to the correct authority was a major challenge.
Road identification and matching: A damaged-road photo does not always contain enough information to identify the exact road. Connecting the complaint with the correct road record required combining location, road data, and image information.
AI model integration: Integrating the road-damage detection/analysis model with the backend while maintaining reliable API communication was challenging.
Handling incomplete road data: Some roads reported by citizens may not already exist in the government database. We needed a mechanism to add these roads and send them through the appropriate approval and budget workflow.
Budget and contractor mapping: Connecting each road with its sanctioned budget, actual expenditure, contractor, and previous repair history required careful database relationships.
Real-time complaint tracking: Designing SLA tracking and complaint-status updates so that government officials can monitor pending, in-progress, and completed complaints was another challenge.
Offline support: Citizens may have poor or no internet connectivity when reporting road damage. Supporting offline complaint creation, local storage, map caching, and later synchronization added complexity.
Microservice communication: RoadWatch uses multiple backend components such as NestJS, PostgreSQL/PostGIS, Redis, Kafka, Elasticsearch, Kong Gateway, and Cloudinary. Making these services communicate reliably was challenging.
Geospatial data handling: Working with road locations and geographic information required spatial database capabilities, which is why PostGIS was incorporated.
Security and role-based access: The system needed different capabilities for citizens and government officials while protecting sensitive administrative operations.

**Road Safety**

RoadWatch addresses road safety by transforming citizens into real-time road-condition sensors. When a citizen reports a damaged road, our AI analyzes the damage, identifies its location and responsible authority, assesses its priority, and initiates an accountable repair workflow. By combining AI, geospatial intelligence, budget and contractor information, historical repair data, and SLA tracking, RoadWatch helps authorities identify high-risk roads earlier and take action before road defects turn into accidents

Team **Sudo Apt** -- [Nithish S](https://github.com/Nithish-1622), [RAGHAV V](https://github.com/VRaghav1806), [Prem Narayn N L](https://github.com/premnarayn13), [Mukesh Kannan](https://github.com/Mukesheshwar29)

> Describe a model. Idle machines train it for you.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![Go](https://img.shields.io/badge/Go-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![SQLite](https://img.shields.io/badge/SQLite-333333?style=flat-square) ![Tailwind CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/Tailwind%20CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![TRANSFORMERS](https://img.shields.io/badge/TRANSFORMERS-333333?style=flat-square) ![Vite](https://img.shields.io/badge/Vite-333333?style=flat-square) ![Distributed systems](https://img.shields.io/badge/Distributed%20systems-333333?style=flat-square)

**How Did You Use Claude?**

## Claude is the intelligence layer of the product, not a dev-time helper

The planning agent runs **in the product's request path**. Remove it and the platform loses the thing that makes it usable by a non-expert.

**Model:** `claude-opus-5` via the Messages API (Go SDK), with adaptive thinking, prompt caching on the system prompt, and a **forced tool call** (`submit_training_plan`) carrying a strict JSON schema — so the plan arrives as validated structured data, not prose to be regex'd.

**What Claude receives:** the user's plain-English request plus a *statistical profile* of the dataset — column types, ranges, cardinality, null counts, top values. **Never the raw rows.** That is both a privacy property and a token bound: a 60,000-row file costs the same to plan as a 600-row one.

**What Claude returns:** target column, task type, architecture, epochs, batch size, learning rate, optimizer, **shard count and sharding strategy**, ordered preprocessing steps, evaluation metric, a rationale written for the customer, and specific warnings. The scheduler compiles that directly into an executable model spec.

## Claude's plans were genuinely good — and caught a bug in my code

From the profile alone, on the churn dataset, it independently derived:

- the class balance (428/472) from the top-values histogram
- that `total_charges` is near-collinear with `tenure × monthly_charges` — *"mean 2424 is close to 36.2 × 67.8"*
- that both money columns had exactly 200 distinct values across 900 rows, so the file was probably synthetic. **It was.** I had generated it an hour earlier.
- that gradient-boosted trees beat a neural net at 900 rows
- that "don't miss real churners" means tuning the decision threshold for F2, not resampling

Then it chose `hyperparameter_sweep` over `data_parallel` **specifically because** 300 rows per shard would degrade every model — every shard needed all 900 rows. My scheduler was ignoring the strategy field and splitting the rows anyway. **Claude's reasoning is what exposed the bug**; the run had been quietly contradicting its own plan.

## Designed to degrade honestly

With no API key the platform falls back to a deterministic heuristic planner reading the same profile, and stays fully functional. Every job records which planner produced its plan, so a result is never ambiguous about its provenance. When Claude names something the engine cannot build (boosted trees, a pretrained BERT), the nearest neural equivalent runs and the substitution is written into the job's warnings — never silently swapped.

## Claude also built it

The entire codebase — the Go training engine, the hand-derived attention backward pass, the barrier, the React dashboard, the docs — was written with **Claude Code** across one continuous session. Notably, Claude wrote the gradient-checking suite that then caught its own bug in `Dense.Backward` (a double transpose) on the first run, before a single line of training code had shipped.

**What is the deployed URL for this project?**

https://github.com/gijutsu-hub/pushtoprod

**How you are solving it?**

## Disclosure first

**Everything described here was written during the hackathon, starting from an empty directory.** No prior code, no prior submission, no template. Nothing in this project has been presented anywhere before. The full history is one continuous build.

## What Club GPU is

A marketplace where members contribute idle machines, and other members upload a dataset, describe what they want in plain English, and get a genuinely trained model back — sharded across whatever hardware is online, metered per second, and settled against a credit ledger.

```
upload CSV ─▶ profiled on arrival (types, ranges, cardinality, nulls)
                     │
"predict churn" ─▶ Claude reads the profile, writes a concrete plan
                     │
           plan compiled to a model spec; data fitted + encoded once
                     │
     ┌────── sharded across the cluster ──────┐
     ▼               ▼                        ▼
  node A          node B                   node A
     └── every epoch: train, submit params, ───┘
         wait at barrier, get averaged model
                     │
         best-scoring round saved + NumPy predictor
```

## Three things had to be built

### 1. A real training engine, in Go, from scratch

The hard constraint was that a provider node must be **one binary** — no Python, no CUDA, no cgo. So there was no framework to reach for. I wrote one.

Dense tensors, explicit hand-derived forward and backward passes, AdamW with decoupled weight decay and gradient-norm clipping, cosine schedule with warmup, LayerNorm, GELU, dropout, residual blocks, multi-head self-attention, and an **FT-Transformer** — the architecture that makes each table *column* a token so attention can learn which columns inform which.

A framework you wrote yourself is worth exactly as much as its gradient check, so every backward pass is verified against central-difference numerical differentiation:

| Model | Tensors | Worst relative error |
|---|---|---|
| Linear / MLP / Residual MLP | 26 | ≤ 3.0e-11 |
| **FT-Transformer** | **33** | **3.9e-08** |

And it demonstrably learns: **96.3%** on two spirals (not linearly separable), **96.0%** on an XOR-style interaction where no single feature is predictive, **R² 0.994** on regression.

### 2. Real distributed training, not an ensemble

Shards do not train separate models that get merged. They train **one** model together through a synchronous parameter-averaging barrier:

1. Every shard builds the same model from the same spec and seed — bit-identical, so round 0 needs zero transfer.
2. Each trains one epoch on its own rows.
3. Each submits its parameters and **blocks**.
4. Once every live shard has arrived, the server computes a row-weighted average and releases all waiters with the new global model.

A barrier is a liveness hazard, so: reclaimed shards are explicitly dropped from the expected set; every round has a deadline after which partial contributions are averaged and flagged; a shard submitting NaN is rejected outright rather than poisoning the shared model; and a reassigned shard rejoins at the *current* round instead of restarting.

### 3. The marketplace and the delivery

Node registry with heartbeats and reaping, long-poll shard leasing, slot accounting, retry-with-failover, per-second metering, and a double-entry credit ledger with a platform fee.

Output is a downloadable bundle: weights, model card, the fitted preprocessing pipeline, the learning curve, and a generated **`predict.py`** that reimplements the exact forward pass in NumPy — so the model works with no Club GPU code at all. There is also a live inference endpoint.

## It actually works

Real run on 900 rows, FT-Transformer, 67,906 parameters, 3 shards:

```
round  1: train 0.9574  val 0.8057  acc 0.620  auc 0.708
round 22: train 0.5905  val 0.6088  acc 0.696  auc 0.744  <- saved
round 60: train 0.5397  val 0.6416  acc 0.635  auc 0.748
```

Textbook overfitting — so the platform ships round 22, not round 60. Predictions from that model on unseen rows: 2 months + 7 support tickets + month-to-month → `churn=yes` (97.2%); 70 months + two-year contract → `churn=no` (90.9%).

## What is not real, stated plainly

- **No GPUs.** Training runs on the CPU. A node's "VRAM" is self-declared and used for scheduling and pricing, not measured. GPU kernels are the obvious next step; the scheduler already treats a shard as an opaque unit of work.
- **Credits are a ledger.** Topping up writes a row; no money moves.
- **No pretrained weights.** A request for a BERT gets a transformer trained from scratch, and the substitution is recorded in the job's warnings rather than hidden.

Everything else — the gradients, the models, the metrics on a held-out split, the distribution and its failover, the predictions — is real.

**What is the problem your project solves?**

## Two walls stand between an idea and a trained model

**The first is compute.** Training capacity is concentrated in a handful of clouds, rented by the hour, and priced for companies rather than people. A student in Bengaluru with a good hypothesis and a CSV file has no realistic path to training on it. Meanwhile the hardware already exists — it is just scattered. Every gaming rig, every workstation, every laptop sits at single-digit utilisation for most of its life. The world is not short of silicon. It is short of a way to *point* the idle silicon at a problem.

**The second wall is expertise, and it is the one nobody talks about.** Suppose you solve compute. You still have to know that `total_charges` is collinear with `tenure × monthly_charges`, that 900 rows will overfit a 68k-parameter network, that your target needs label encoding while your categoricals need one-hot, that a 3-shard split of a small dataset makes every shard worse. That knowledge is the actual gate. Renting a GPU to someone who does not have it just gives them an expensive way to train a bad model.

## Why it matters

These two walls compound. Cheap compute without expertise produces garbage. Expertise without compute produces nothing. Almost every "democratise AI" effort attacks exactly one of them, which is why the floor has not actually moved.

The people this locks out are not marginal. They are the clinic with three years of patient records, the logistics operator with a spreadsheet of delivery times, the undergraduate with a research idea and a second-hand laptop. They have the data and the questions. They do not have a cluster or an ML team.

## The impact if both walls come down

If idle consumer hardware can be pooled into a real cluster, and if plain English is enough to direct it, then the requirement for training a model collapses to: **own a machine, or have a question and a CSV**.

That is a genuinely different world. Compute stops being a thing you buy from three companies and becomes a thing a community contributes to and draws from — with the people who supply it getting paid. And the ML expertise that currently gates the whole field becomes something the system supplies on your behalf, and *explains*, rather than something you must already possess.

Team **Ox_nanobit** -- [Sumon Nath](https://github.com/gijustu-hub)

> Self reliance using Technologies

![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![NVIDIA cuDNN](https://img.shields.io/badge/NVIDIA%20cuDNN-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Persistent monsoon clouds in the North Eastern Region of INDIA, blind optical satellites, paralyzing critical environmental monitoring operations. Our project using **Rastormer** based  ***Vision Transformer*** try to solves this by fusing ***Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR)*** data collected from ***sentinel-2*** with cloudy images( in .tif format ) uploaded by the users  to generate clear, usable data. It safely enables continuous flood tracking, urban planning, and forestry management, completely eliminating delays for monsoon cloudless passes.

**Challenges we ran into**

- **Dataset**: Finding paired imagery is hard; we solved this by injecting synthetic Perlin clouds tuned to monsoon profiles. 

- **Accuracy**: To improve PSNR/SSIM and prevent hallucinations, we used Vision Transformers with SAR cross-attention.  

- **Training Time**: We overcame heavy compute requirements and VRAM constraints using LoRA adaptation.

> The AI-Powered Failsafe for Indian Railways.

![OpenCV](https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

The existing Kavach system used by indian railways to prevent the collision is blind to physical obstacles and relies heavily on failure-prone track infrastructure. Train drivers currently cannot detect sudden physical hazards (landslides, stalled vehicles, unmapped derailments) fast enough to brake at high speeds, and centralized communication towers suffer from network blind spots.

**Challenges we ran into**

​The "Horizon Line" Geometry Bug: At first, our AI was calculating objects floating above the horizon. We had to rewrite the bounding-box logic so the AI only measures the exact X/Y coordinate where the object's "footprint" touches the ground.
Perspective Trajectory Intersections: When we added 1-point perspective scaling, the "passing train" mathematically drifted into our green braking zone as it grew larger! We fixed this by adjusting the coordinate trajectory angle to ensure adjacent tracks stay perfectly outside the 2km Safe Corridor.

> Discovering the Causes Behind the Outcomes.

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Machine Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Machine%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square) ![Deep Learning](https://img.shields.io/badge/Deep%20Learning-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

**Additional Details**

About the Project

The **CausalInsightAI** project is an open-source initiative focused on building AI systems that are more reliable, explainable, and transparent. Unlike traditional machine learning models that primarily identify patterns and correlations within data, this project emphasizes understanding cause-and-effect relationships, enabling AI systems to make decisions that are easier to interpret and trust.

At the core of the project is a powerful causal discovery framework that allows users to identify causal structures from data. The platform provides various algorithms, tools, and datasets that help researchers and developers understand how different variables influence one another rather than simply determining whether they are statistically related.

The project supports applications across multiple domains, including healthcare, finance, manufacturing, education, and business analytics. By combining causal inference, graph learning, and machine learning techniques, CausalInsightAI helps organizations gain deeper insights into complex systems and make more informed decisions.

 **Key Features**

* Causal discovery and causal structure learning.
* Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) generation and analysis.
* Synthetic and real-world dataset support.
* Multiple state-of-the-art causal learning algorithms.
* Research implementations for causal representation learning.
* Benchmarking and evaluation tools.
* Scalable architecture with support for modern machine learning frameworks.
* Open-source resources for academic research and industrial applications.


 **Impact and Benefits**

CausalInsightAI contributes to the development of next-generation AI systems that are more dependable and interpretable. By enabling machines to reason about causal relationships, the project helps improve decision-making quality, supports explainable AI initiatives, and enhances trust in automated systems.

Its tools and frameworks are valuable for researchers, students, and organizations seeking to understand complex data relationships, perform root-cause analysis, and build intelligent systems capable of making evidence-based decisions.

**Challenges we ran into**

** Challenges Addressed**

One of the major challenges in artificial intelligence is distinguishing correlation from causation. Traditional machine learning models often identify relationships between variables but cannot determine whether one variable actually influences another. CausalInsightAI addresses this limitation through advanced causal learning techniques that help uncover underlying causal mechanisms.

Another challenge is the lack of transparency in many AI systems. Complex models often behave like black boxes, making it difficult to understand how decisions are made. The project provides explainable methodologies that allow researchers and practitioners to better interpret model behavior and reasoning processes.

Handling large-scale data and complex causal networks is also challenging. To address this, the framework includes efficient algorithms and optimized implementations that support experimentation with high-dimensional datasets and graph structures.

The project further tackles the difficulty of evaluating causal discovery methods by providing benchmark datasets, performance metrics, and standardized evaluation procedures that enable fair comparison of different approaches.

Team **AB positive** -- [Ananya A](https://github.com/Aquaia), [Bharat Darshankar](https://github.com/bharat-0917)

### CyberSight
[![Devfolio](https://img.shields.io/badge/Devfolio-View%20Project-4B32C3?style=flat-square&logo=devfolio&logoColor=white)](https://devfolio.co/projects/cybersight-0c17) [![GitHub](https://img.shields.io/badge/GitHub-Source%20Code-181717?style=flat-square&logo=github&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/Adithyaa-Kumar/threat_matrix) [![Video](https://img.shields.io/badge/Video-Watch-FF0000?style=flat-square&logo=youtube&logoColor=white)](https://youtu.be/NaabAYq4jTw) [![Built at](https://img.shields.io/badge/Built%20at-MicroCraft%20--%20ArcNight-0052CC?style=flat-square)](https://microcarft-arcnight.devfolio.co)

> Understand. Detect. Respond.

![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/React-333333?style=flat-square) ![PyTorch](https://img.shields.io/badge/PyTorch-333333?style=flat-square) ![scikit-learn](https://img.shields.io/badge/scikit--learn-333333?style=flat-square) ![NumPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/NumPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![pandas](https://img.shields.io/badge/pandas-333333?style=flat-square) ![Matplotlib](https://img.shields.io/badge/Matplotlib-333333?style=flat-square) ![SciPy](https://img.shields.io/badge/SciPy-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

Most people have no visibility into what their devices are doing online.

Applications, background services, and automated processes constantly communicate with external systems, but users rarely know:

What data is being sent or received
Whether a connection is normal or suspicious
If their device is being targeted by malicious activity
What action they should take when a threat is detected

Existing cybersecurity tools often present this information through packet captures, logs, and technical alerts that are difficult for non-experts to understand.

CyberSight solves this by translating network behavior into simple, actionable insights. Instead of displaying raw traffic data, it helps users understand their digital footprint, detect suspicious activity in real time, and receive clear explanations and recommendations.

This makes cybersecurity more accessible, allowing users to identify potential threats earlier and respond with confidence rather than confusion.

**Challenges we ran into**

A surprisingly difficult problem was deciding what the product actually was.

At first, we were building a network traffic classifier. The model worked, the predictions looked good, and the dashboard showed attack categories.

But after revisiting the challenge statement, we realized we weren't solving the user's problem—we were only solving the machine learning problem.

The challenge wasn't "Can we classify packets?"

The challenge was "Can we help someone understand their digital footprint and respond to threats?"

That forced us to rethink the entire presentation layer. We shifted from displaying model outputs to explaining behavior, risk, and recommended actions. The AI stayed the same, but the product became much more useful.

That change probably had a bigger impact on the project than any model improvement we made.

Team **Cipher** -- Harshita K, Adithyaa K, Nisha Anandi, Lakshetha RR

**The problem it solves**

Traditional studying is passive, overwhelming, and lonely. Students waste hours manually reading dense textbooks, creating flashcards, and guessing what will be on an exam.
 
**What People Can Use It For**

1. Instant Gamified Studying: Upload any study material (text or images) and instantly get custom mock tests and active-recall flashcard decks.

![image](https://assets.devfolio.co/content/25032d0c049448b1876b98713d7920a3/cd216e12-dff7-4e63-95f7-268051521cc8.png)

**Challenges we ran into**

Challenges We Ran Into & How We Overcame Them
Building **LevelUp** :

1. The Local OS Package Trap (psycopg2 Compilation Failure)
The Hurdle: When setting up our backend environment, installing the standard psycopg2 package completely crashed on Windows machines. The package tried to compile C++ source code from scratch, which failed because local laptops lacked the heavy build tools and compilers.

The Fix: We bypassed the compilation phase entirely by switching our virtual environment over to psycopg2-binary. This forced Python to download a pre-compiled Windows wheel, instantly resolving the environment roadblock and keeping our dependency tree lightweight.

2. Cloud File Service Restriction vs. Our Local TrOCR/OCR Fallback System
The Hurdle: When testing our core feature—uploading raw document files—the backend returned a 401 Unauthenticated error to the FileService. CreateFile method. We discovered that Google's latest Generative AI SDK strictly blocks standard free-tier API keys from accessing their cloud file storage containers directly, requiring complex enterprise OAuth 2.0 service credentials instead.

The Overcome: Instead of letting the app crash, we built a hybrid fallback architecture using a localized OCR and TrOCR pipeline powered by Hugging Face Transformers and PyTorch (ocr_service.py). When Google's cloud file service throws a 401 block, our backend catches the error and automatically routes the document to a local Hugging Face Vision Transformer model (VisionEncoderDecoderModel). This local engine extracts the text line-by-line and feeds the raw string payload straight into standard text endpoints—completely bypassing cloud container restrictions while ensuring stable, offline-ready document parsing.

Team **CodeCrafters** -- Diya Jabin, [Gnanamrutha Pallapothu](https://github.com/Gnanamrutha2007), HithaPragnyaa M

> AI For Everyday Investors

![HTML](https://img.shields.io/badge/HTML-333333?style=flat-square) ![CSS](https://img.shields.io/badge/CSS-333333?style=flat-square) ![JavaScript](https://img.shields.io/badge/JavaScript-333333?style=flat-square) ![Flask](https://img.shields.io/badge/Flask-333333?style=flat-square) ![TensorFlow](https://img.shields.io/badge/TensorFlow-333333?style=flat-square) ![REST API](https://img.shields.io/badge/REST%20API-333333?style=flat-square) ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-333333?style=flat-square) ![Long short-term memory (LSTM)](https://img.shields.io/badge/Long%20short--term%20memory%20(LSTM)-333333?style=flat-square)

**The problem it solves**

What can people use it for?

This platform helps retail investors make smarter and safer investment decisions using AI-powered portfolio intelligence. It analyzes real-time market data, insider trades, bulk deals, corporate filings, and technical chart patterns to identify high-potential investment opportunities.

Users can:

-Discover promising stocks using AI-driven signals
-Optimize portfolios with LSTM-based predictions
-Get personalized buy/sell/hold recommendations
-Understand portfolio risk through simple dashboards
-Detect bullish and bearish chart patterns automatically
-Receive plain-English explanations instead of complex financial jargon

The system combines:

-Opportunity Detection
-Chart Pattern Intelligence
-AI Portfolio Optimization
to reduce emotional investing and improve decision-making accuracy.

How does it make existing tasks easier and safer?
Traditional investing requires manually analyzing huge amounts of financial data, which is difficult for beginners and retail investors. Our platform automates this process using AI and real-time analytics.

It makes investing easier by:
-Saving hours of manual research
-Simplifying technical analysis
-Automatically calculating optimal portfolio allocations
-Providing real-time risk analysis and rebalancing suggestions

It makes investing safer by:
-Using multi-signal confirmation before recommendations
-Monitoring risk metrics like Sharpe Ratio, CVaR, and volatility
-Encouraging diversification and disciplined investing
-Providing transparent, explainable AI insights

The goal is to give everyday investors access to institutional-grade portfolio intelligence in a simple and accessible way.

**Challenges we ran into**

-Integrating multiple real-time financial data sources such as NSE APIs, SEBI filings, bulk deals, insider trades, and market sentiment feeds into a unified pipeline.
-Training and optimizing the LSTM model for accurate stock return prediction while avoiding overfitting and maintaining stable performance during volatile market conditions.
-Combining deep learning predictions with Modern Portfolio Theory (MPT) for dynamic portfolio optimization and risk-adjusted allocation.
-Processing large datasets efficiently and generating real-time signals with low latency.
-Building explainable AI outputs so that complex financial metrics and predictions could be understood easily by retail investors.
-Detecting reliable chart patterns and validating them using historical market data.
-Handling asynchronous backend workflows for optimization, risk analysis, and portfolio rebalancing.
-Designing a beginner-friendly UI that simplifies advanced financial analytics without overwhelming users.
-Ensuring scalability, data reliability, and secure handling of sensitive portfolio information.

**BEST ACTIVE TEAM**

Our team demonstrated strong collaboration, active participation, and rapid execution throughout the hackathon by building a fully functional AI-powered portfolio intelligence platform for retail investors. We worked continuously across AI/ML development, backend APIs, frontend dashboards, real-time financial data integration, portfolio optimization, and risk analytics. Instead of a static prototype, we developed an integrated system featuring live opportunity detection, LSTM-based portfolio optimization, chart pattern intelligence, and real-time risk analysis. The team actively iterated on the project by replacing mock data with real datasets, improving model performance, optimizing APIs, debugging workflows, and refining the user experience under strict time constraints. Through parallel development, constant communication, and efficient task coordination, we successfully transformed a complex fintech idea into a scalable and impactful real-world solution.

**BEST INNOVATIVE IDEA**

Our project introduces an AI-powered portfolio intelligence platform that combines LSTM deep learning, portfolio optimization, real-time opportunity detection, and chart pattern intelligence into a single ecosystem designed specifically for Indian retail investors.

What makes the idea innovative is the integration of:

1.AI-based stock return prediction
2.Dynamic portfolio optimization using LSTM + MPT hybrid models
3.Real-time analysis of insider trades, bulk deals, and filings
4.Explainable AI for beginner-friendly financial guidance

Unlike traditional investing platforms that mainly provide charts or raw data, our system acts as an intelligent decision-support engine that:

Detects investment opportunities,
1.Confirms them using technical pattern intelligence,
2.Optimizes portfolio allocation automatically,
3.Explains everything in plain English.

The platform democratizes institutional-grade financial intelligence for ordinary investors, making advanced portfolio management accessible, affordable, and easier to understand. It transforms investing from emotional and manual decision-making into a data-driven, AI-assisted process.
